  trace, no printed labels at all), so it's also the one crane where this
  actually adds information instead of duplicating it.
  Mechanism: `shortLabel: ''` on a component means "render no label"
  (falsy, skipped) - used for every non-1130 component and for 1650's
  zero-weight linked Center Section. Omitting `shortLabel` entirely would
  fall back to the full `name` string as the label, which is worse than
  either option here, so every component across all 5 cranes now sets it
  explicitly one way or the other - don't add a new component without
  also setting this, or it'll silently fall back to printing its full
  name on the diagram.
  If the person re-does another crane's diagram from CAD (per 10.15's
  process) the way they did for 1130, give it real shortLabels too at
  that point - the reason to skip it is "this diagram already has printed
  numbers," not "this feature is 1130-specific by design."

10.17 "Jump to Known Combination" dropdown now syncs to manual selection
  Previously the dropdown only ever changed the selection (one-way: pick
  a combo -> plates highlight). Tapping plates by hand never touched the
  dropdown, so a person building a combo manually who happened to land on
  a real OEM-published total had no way to see that confirmed there
  except the "OEM-approved combination" checkmark on the total-weight
  card - the dropdown itself stayed on whatever it last showed (usually
  "0.0 t - No counterweight"), which read as if the app hadn't noticed.
  Fixed by making it two-way: cwtFindMatchingCombo now returns the
  matched combo's own index in COUNTERWEIGHT_DATA[model].combos (was
  previously just the combo object via Array.find - switched to
  findIndex and spread the index onto the returned object as `.idx`, one
  call site, no other callers to break), and renderCwtTab sets
  #cwt-combo-select's value to that index whenever a match exists. When
  the manual selection does NOT match any published combo, the dropdown
  is explicitly set to selectedIndex = -1 (no visible selection) rather
  than left showing a stale/misleading previous value - the existing
  "not an OEM-approved combination" warning box already covers that case
  so there's no need for the dropdown to also try to communicate it.
  Verified both directions still work correctly together: picking from
  the dropdown drives the selection (unchanged, onCwtComboSelect), and
  building the same combo by hand now drives the dropdown back (new) -
  tested by tapping LTM 1130's plates 1-7 one at a time (lands on the
  real 29.3t combo, dropdown updates to show it), then adding plate 8
  (42.0t, also real, dropdown updates again), then removing plate 2 only
  (39.6t, not a published combo, dropdown drops to no selection).

10.18 LTM 1110-5.1 diagram replaced too - same DXF-exploded-CAD process as
      10.15, plus a real box-to-plate mix-up in the person's own CAD
      export, caught via the manufacturer's weight stamp baked into the
      geometry instead of guessed
  Same overall pipeline as 10.15 (DXF "boxes" layer -> render PDF_Visible/
  PDF_TANGENT_LINES geometry -> exact affine vector-to-pixel math -> new
  diagram + hotspots), so this entry only covers what's different.
  First upload had 7 boxes; needed 8 (one per component that owns a
  hotspot: p1, p2, p4 (see below), p5, p6, p7, p8w, plus - unlike 1130 -
  a genuinely separate 8th here because this crane's p3/p4 pair turned
  out to have its own clean split, see below). Same "count is short by
  exactly one, don't guess the shape, ask for the missing box" call as
  10.15 - the person confirmed and re-sent with the 8th box added.
  Orientation: raw CAD Y-up (unflipped) was correct again, same as 10.15
  - box0 (smallest Y) is the bottom of the stack, item 1.
  The person warned up front that they'd mixed up two plates while
  exploding the 3D model, so position/order alone couldn't be fully
  trusted this time (unlike 10.15, where the naive top-to-bottom guess
  turned out right). Rather than ask them to re-check by eye, checked the
  DXF geometry itself first: two of the boxes contain actual embossed
  weight text as part of the traced CAD geometry (the physical plates
  have their tonnage cast/stamped into the steel, and since this is a
  literal 3D-model trace rather than a photo, that text came through as
  real vector line-work, legible after rendering) - one box clearly reads
  "2,5t" (matches plate 7's 2.5t) and another reads "3,6t" (matches plate
  5's 3.6t). That pinned two positions with certainty rather than
  inference. The box sitting where the naive bottom-up count expected
  "plate 6" was the one stamped "2,5t" - i.e. actually plate 7 - and the
  thin sliver box one level up (proportioned right for the lightest
  plate, 0.3t) was actually plate 6. Confirmed this read back to the
  person with the interpreted image before touching index.html, same as
  every other ambiguous case this session - they confirmed it verbatim,
  plus clarified which physical side of the 3/4 pair is which (box2=4,
  box3=3 - doesn't change app behavior either way since they're linked,
  but worth getting right for anyone reading the data later).
  Lesson: when a person flags their own source data might have an error,
  don't just re-ask them to double check by eye - look for independent
  evidence in the data itself first (here, literal printed numbers that
  had nothing to do with position/order) and lead with that. It's a
  stronger confirmation than "does this look right to you" on a second
  pass of the same reasoning that produced the mistake in the first
  place.
  p3/p4 upgrade: 10.7 originally merged these into one shared hotspot
  because the OLD extracted-photo diagram had no clean dividing line
  between their silhouettes. This new CAD tracing has a real, clean split
  (they sit side by side, not overlapping), so they now each get their
  own precise hotspot - kept in sync both directions via linkedId on
  BOTH components now (previously only p4 had linkedId: 'p3'; toggling
  p3's own hotspot did nothing since it had none). Verified both
  directions: tapping plate 3 alone selects plate 4 too and vice versa,
  and toggling either back off deselects both. This is a real, confirmed
  fleet rule per the person directly ("those two slabs always get
  selected together cause you can't use one and not the other ever"),
  not just a diagram-tracing artifact - keep the linkedId even if a
  future diagram somehow made them look separable.
  Since this new diagram also has no baked-in printed reference numbers
  (only the incidental weight stamps noted above, not "1".."8" leader-
  line labels), it gets the same on-diagram shortLabel treatment as 1130
  (10.16) - '1' through '7', plus '8 / Winch #2' / '8' for the p8w/p8b
  slot pair, matching 1130's "N / Winch #M" convention for a plate/winch
  dual-purpose slot.
  New file: counterweight/img/ltm1110-cwt-exploded-v2.png (1122x1400).
  Old ltm1110-cwt-exploded.png deleted, nothing else referenced it.

10.19 LTM 1110-5.1 combo list gap - a genuinely missing 29.0t combo, found
      by trusting the person's arithmetic over my own extraction
  The person asked why the "full stack" combo showed 28.2t instead of
  29.0t. I explained the countsToward:false rule (correct, unchanged) but
  that didn't fully answer them - they went and checked their own PDF and
  came back with more pages ("it was cut off from the pdf i supplied").
  Re-extracted the new pages with the same -29 decode as always and ran
  every entry through the same subset-sum check used throughout this
  whole feature. Result: 39 real combos on the supplementary pages, 38 of
  which were ALREADY in the app's data (this new PDF overlapped almost
  entirely with the original extraction) and exactly one new one: 29.0t =
  full 7-plate stack + Replacement Ballast (as opposed to the existing
  28.2t = full stack + Winch 2*). Two different, both genuinely
  published, full-stack combos - not the same combo miscounted. Added it
  to the '1110' combos array (see comment there) and to the crane's combo
  count in the header comment.
  Also found a real anomaly while doing this: one entry on the new pages
  prints "28.7 t" for a composition (plates 1,2,3,4,5,6 + Replacement
  Ballast) that actually sums to 26.5t - a 2.2t gap that does NOT match
  the countsToward:false/Winch-2* exclusion pattern (no Winch 2* is even
  present in that entry's own component list) and doesn't correspond to
  any known plate weight either. Checked this thoroughly before writing
  it off: pulled the raw per-character codes for that specific text run
  via get_text("rawdict") and decoded them by hand against the same -29
  shift used everywhere else - the digits "28.7" really are what's
  encoded in the PDF, not a decode error on my end, and there is no
  hidden/overlapping text block near it that could be a dropped 7th
  component. This is a single, isolated instance in the whole fleet's
  extracted data (every other entry across all 5 cranes has reconciled
  exactly) - treated as a genuine printing error in Liebherr's own
  document rather than invented a 7th component to make it add up.
  NOT added to the app. If a future page of this same document clarifies
  it, revisit; until then this specific 28.7t line should be treated as
  unreliable if anyone encounters it in the physical manual too.
  Lesson for next time this kind of question comes up: when someone
  pushes back on a total that "should" be a round/expected number, don't
  just re-explain the existing rule and stop there - the discrepancy can
  be a real gap in what was extracted, not a misunderstanding on their
  end. Re-checking the source here found a real missing combo AND a real
  document error, neither of which a second explanation of the same rule
  would have surfaced.

10.20 Winch 2* vs Replacement Ballast - added an explicit non-
      interchangeability warning, LTM 1110 only so far
  Prompted by the same conversation as 10.19: once the person understood
  the weight/total mechanics, they raised the more important point - the
  app was presenting these two as basically the same choice (same slot,
  same 0.8t, one UI toggle) with nothing calling out that they are NOT
  interchangeable in what the crane can actually do. Winch 2* fitted
  means the crane has a working second auxiliary winch. Replacement
  Ballast in the same slot means it does not - that slot is inert dead
  weight only. Someone rigging for a job that needs winch 2 could easily
  read "same weight, same slot" as "doesn't matter which," which is
  wrong and could leave them without a winch they were counting on.
  New per-component field `slotWarning` (set on both p8w and p8b, worded
  from each one's own perspective) plus a small addition to renderCwtTab's
  existing notices-array pattern (same mechanism already used for
  "combination not OEM-approved" and LTM 1250's centerTrim warning) - any
  currently-selected component with a slotWarning string gets it appended
  to the warning box, regardless of which one is active. Verified both
  directions render correctly (selecting Winch 2* shows the "this IS a
  winch" wording, cycling to Replacement Ballast swaps to the "this is
  NOT a winch" wording).
  Scope note: only LTM 1110 has this slotGroup pattern with a real
  functional (not just cosmetic) difference between alternatives right
  now. If another crane's data ever has a similar "same slot, same
  weight, different real-world capability" pair, give it a slotWarning
  too - don't assume every slotGroup/quantityGroup pairing needs one
  (LTM 1650's 10t plate quantity ladder, for example, has no such
  distinction - every rung really is just more of the identical plate).

10.21 LTM 1250-5.1 diagram replaced too - same DXF process, plus the
      first case where weight stamps couldn't fully disambiguate
  Same pipeline as 10.15/10.18. 9 boxes on the DXF's "boxes" layer for 8
  components (7 main-stack plates + 2 wing halves for p8) - correct count
  first try, no missing box this time.
  Orientation: raw CAD Y-up again correct, same as every crane so far -
  box0 (smallest Y) = bottom = plate 1.
  Identity: plates 1 (11t), 5 (12t), 6 (5t), and both p8 wings (10t each)
  were all confirmed via the manufacturer's own weight stamps baked into
  the CAD geometry, same as 10.18. But plates 2, 3, and 4 are all
  coincidentally 10.0t EACH - three boxes, all stamped "10t", with
  nothing in the geometry itself to tell them apart. This is different
  from 10.18's swap: there weight stamps resolved the ambiguity directly;
  here they can't, because the stamps themselves are identical across
  the genuinely different plates. Identity of 2 vs 3 vs 4 matters for
  real - the combos array picks specific subsets (e.g. {p1,p2,p4,p5}
  without p3), so getting the order wrong would highlight the wrong
  physical plate for a real published combo even though the total
  weight would still happen to come out right in some cases.
  Sent the rendered guess (bottom-to-top positional order matching the
  OLD diagram's already-established 1-2-3-4-5-6-7 order, i.e. assuming
  this new export preserves the same physical stacking sequence) and the
  person confirmed it directly rather than independently re-deriving it -
  worth being explicit that this one component of the mapping rests on
  their confirmation plus positional continuity with prior data, not on
  independent evidence the way 10.18's swap catch did. If a future
  diagram for this crane ever contradicts this order, 10.18's swap is the
  reminder that position-matches-old-diagram is a reasonable prior, not a
  guarantee.
  New file: counterweight/img/ltm1250-cwt-exploded-v2.png (2625x1400).
  Old ltm1250-cwt-exploded.png deleted. Also caught and fixed a stale
  reference this time before shipping: the tab's static HTML had a
  hardcoded placeholder `<img src>` and default SVG viewBox pointing at
  LTM 1250's OLD file/dimensions (used only as the pre-JS initial markup,
  overwritten by renderCwtTab() on load, but still worth keeping correct
  so there's no flash of a broken image / wrong-aspect-ratio hotspot
  layer before the first render call completes) - updated both to the
  new file and 2625x1400. Checked 1130 and 1110 for the same issue when
  this was noticed; neither had a stale reference left over.

10.22 LTM 1650-8.1 hotspots re-traced from a user-supplied alignment DXF -
      the 10.13/10.14 undershoot was systemic, not one bad box
  Unlike 1130/1110/1250, this crane's diagramImage stayed the original OEM
  JPG (Fig.156378) - no exploded-CAD replacement was available for it. What
  the person supplied instead: the SAME JPG re-opened in AutoCAD with an
  IMAGE entity anchored at insert (0,0) and u_pixel/v_pixel scaled so DXF
  world coordinates map directly onto the JPG's pixel grid (confirmed by
  image_size 1831x1266 in the IMAGE entity matching this crane's existing
  diagramWidth/diagramHeight exactly, and by rendering the DXF geometry
  over the actual JPG and checking it lands on the real edges rather than
  guessing blind). On top of that image the person hand-traced boundary
  LINEs (layer '0', the 8 real plate/receptacle edges per stack) and
  LWPOLYLINEs (winches w2/w3, the combined p1 receptacle footprint with a
  notch cut out for p4center) directly on the true printed edges - i.e.
  the DXF's only job here was precise pixel measurement of an existing
  image, not a new rendering.
  Conversion: pixel = (dxf_x / 1.313675, image_height - dxf_y / 1.313675).
  The scale factor is u_pixel's magnitude, NOT 1 - the DXF world extents
  (2405.3 x 1663.1) are the image's pixel size already multiplied by that
  factor, and skipping the division silently produces coordinates that
  are still roughly plausible-looking (all boxes land in the right
  general area, right order, right relative spacing) but are wrong by a
  constant factor - the kind of error that only surfaces by overlaying
  the result on the source image and looking, not by inspecting the
  numbers alone. First attempt here made exactly that mistake.
  Result vs. old hand-picked rectangles (10.10e/10.13/10.14): every
  10t-plate quantity-ladder level (p10_2..p10_14) shifts up to ~27px off
  the true printed edge, in both directions independently (a hotspot's
  top and bottom don't drift by the same amount, since the old ones were
  evenly-spaced guesses rather than measurements of the real, unevenly-
  gapped plate positions). p5x2 shrinks from a 60px-tall guess to its
  true ~38px (it really is a thinner plate than the 10t ones - confirmed
  by the DXF, not assumed). w2/w3 and p1/p4center also tightened to the
  DXF-measured boundaries. All 21 hotspots re-verified by rendering them
  as red outlines over the actual diagramImage and confirming each one
  hugs its real printed edge before shipping.
  p4center's hotspot now spans the full center-column height (932-1249),
  merging the DXF's small upper "notch" polyline (932-1001, physically
  just the bit of the center section poking up above the two receptacles)
  with the unmarked gap below it (1001-1249, same y-range as the p1
  boxes) - the DXF only explicitly traced the notch, but the wider outer
  polyline's path shows that lower gap belongs visually to the center
  section, not to either p1 receptacle, consistent with the original
  hand-picked p4center already covering that same full height.
  No combo data, weights, or component list changed - hotspot geometry
  only. diagramImage/diagramWidth/diagramHeight all unchanged (still the
  same 1831x1266 JPG).

10.23 LTM 1300-6.2 (global/export variant) added under crane key '1300' -
      PROVISIONAL combo list, first crane shipped without an OEM chart
  Different from every prior crane addition: no OEM counterweight-
  combination document exists yet for THIS variant. What exists is the
  10.11 EU-model chart (held, never wired in) plus a person-supplied DXF
  of this variant's actual physical counterweight stack (exploded 3D CAD,
  10 boxes visible: box0 through box9) and the person's own direct
  knowledge of each box's weight/position, worked out interactively box
  by box rather than read off a document.
  Component mapping (box -> app id):
    box0 (bottom, 6t)            -> p1 "Receptacle Plate 1"
    box1 (10t)                   -> p2 "Counterweight Plate 2"
    box2, box3 (10t each)        -> p3a, p3b "Counterweight Plate 3"
                                     (identical/interchangeable, not a
                                     mounted pair - same modeling as
                                     LTM 1160's repeated Plate 3, 10.9)
    box4 (10t)                   -> p4 "Counterweight Plate 4"
    box5 (10t, top of stack)     -> p5 "Counterweight Plate 5" - unique
                                     to this variant, no EU equivalent
    box6+box9, box7+box8 (side-  -> auxA "Pair A", auxB "Pair B" -
    mounted, 10t each, always       both-or-neither toggle-together,
    both-or-neither per pair)       same "separate" modeling as 1130's
                                     p8 (10.8). Real names/numbers for
                                     these two pairs are UNCONFIRMED.
  Why box0 is 6t but the EU chart's equivalent base is 16t (13t
  Receptacle + 3t Plate 7): this variant's receptacle is a single 6t
  piece, not two separate parts - box5 (Plate 5, no EU equivalent) makes
  up the remaining 10t. Confirmed directly by the person, not derived.
  Combo list is PROVISIONAL: no OEM chart for this variant exists yet, so
  the 10.11 EU chart's verified tier structure (16/26/36/46/56/66/76/86/
  96t, each tier = previous + exactly one more item, confirmed by
  subset-sum against the real EU PDF) was mirrored directly, substituting
  this variant's p1+p5 for the EU chart's p1+p7 base pair at every tier.
  The EU chart's 13t tier (Receptacle alone, no Plate 7) has no
  equivalent here since box0 isn't a separate piece from... itself - the
  lightest non-zero tier is p1+p5 together (16t, matching the EU chart's
  own 16t tier's total). An empty (0t) combo was added despite the EU
  chart never listing one, for consistency with every other crane in this
  app - low-risk addition, easy to remove if the real chart confirms this
  crane has no true zero-ballast state.
  NOT modeled: the EU chart's 66t/86t spacer-pin mechanic (three spacer
  pins physically replace the second Plate 3 copy at those two tiers,
  10.11) - carries no weight and isn't confirmed to apply identically to
  this variant's hardware, so left out rather than guessed.
  sourceNote on this entry says PROVISIONAL and DO NOT use for real lift
  planning in plain language, not just in this file - visible directly in
  the app's Composition panel for this crane. Replace the combos array
  (and re-verify components) the moment the person supplies this
  variant's own OEM counterweight-combination document; do not silently
  treat this data as equivalent to a verified chart in the meantime.
  New file: counterweight/img/ltm1300-cwt-exploded.png (4643x1300),
  rendered from the person's DXF - white background, matching 1130's own
  DXF-exploded PNG convention (confirmed by inspecting pixel (0,0) on
  both files before treating the white background as a bug).

10.24 LTM 1650's quantity ladder replaced with free independent toggles;
      LTM 1300's auxiliary-ballast pairing corrected and confirmed-named
  Two related fixes from the same conversation, both about matching what
  a crew member actually expects when tapping the diagram.
  a) LTM 1650 10t-plate stack: 10.10 modeled this as a quantity LADDER
     (selectCwtSlotLevel) - clicking any position jumped straight to that
     level and force-filled every position below it, matching how the
     stack is physically BUILT (bottom-up, no gaps possible). Correct
     mechanically, but the person pushed back hard: they wanted to tap
     specific boxes - in whatever order - and have the total build up,
     using the visible boxes purely as "here's what you're selecting"
     labels, not as an enforced build sequence. Confirmed the ladder
     logic itself had no bug (verified 95t was reachable, p1+p5x2+the
     correct single level-6 hotspot, in one click each) - this was a
     product decision, not a defect.
     Replaced the 7 quantityGroup/slotGroup components (p10_2..p10_14,
     cumulative weights 20-140) with 7 fully independent components
     (p10a..p10g, each a flat 20t = one L+R pair) using a NEW
     `interchangeableGroup` flag instead of slotGroup/quantityGroup. Each
     keeps its own hotspot pair (both sides of one position share one id,
     same as before - a position is filled/removed as a unit, you can't
     leave one side of a pair unbalanced) and now falls through
     renderCwtTab's plain toggleCwtPlate path - no special-case rendering
     needed once quantityGroup was removed, since "independent toggle" was
     already the default behavior for anything without slotGroup.
     The catch: 7 interchangeable positions means C(7,k) different ID
     sets all represent "k pairs mounted," but the combos array can only
     literally list ONE set per weight tier. Naively comparing exact ID
     sets (the pre-existing cwtFindMatchingCombo behavior) would flag
     every selection except the one exact canonical subset as "not
     OEM-approved," even though the source document never distinguishes
     WHICH position is filled, only how many. Fixed by adding
     cwtMatchSignature(): before comparing, any component carrying
     interchangeableGroup collapses to a `groupName:count` token instead
     of its own id, on BOTH the live selection and every stored combo's
     item list - so a match only requires the same COUNT from the group,
     not the same specific members. cwtFindMatchingCombo now diffs
     signatures instead of raw sorted-id-joins. cwtComboWeight needed no
     change (already summed by iterating whatever ids are actually
     selected). This wasn't needed for LTM 1160's own repeated-plate
     components (p3a/p3b/p3c, 10.9) since that crane's combos only ever
     reach 3 total copies and were already always listed as the same
     front-loaded canonical subset - the mismatch risk exists there too
     in principle, just never surfaced given only 3 members and shallow
     combo depth. Not retrofitted onto 1160 in this pass - flagging here
     in case it's worth doing if 1160 ever gets the same free-click
     complaint.
     selectCwtSlotLevel() and the quantityGroup branch in renderCwtTab
     were deleted outright rather than left dead - nothing else in
     COUNTERWEIGHT_DATA used quantityGroup once 1650 stopped.
     Verified in-browser: clicking 3 non-contiguous positions in
     arbitrary order (7th, 1st, 4th) correctly sums to 85t and is flagged
     OEM-approved, same as clicking the "canonical" first 3.
  b) LTM 1300 (global/export variant, 10.23) auxiliary ballast pairing
     was wrong: the first pass paired box6+box9 together and box7+box8
     together because those are the two boxes drawn adjacent to each
     other on the SAME side of the exploded-view layout - but the person
     confirmed the real physical pairing is LEFT+RIGHT symmetric (one
     ballast box mounted on each side of the crane, like every other
     component on this diagram), i.e. the INNER pair is box8 (left, near
     the main stack) + box6 (right, near the main stack), and the OUTER
     pair is box7 (left, far from the stack) + box9 (right, far from the
     stack) - the opposite grouping from what the layout's spatial
     adjacency suggested. ids renamed aux5/aux6 (from the placeholder
     auxA/auxB, then Inner/Outer mid-fix) once the person separately
     confirmed the numbering itself: "Auxiliary Ballast 5" and "Auxiliary
     Ballast 6" carry over directly from the held EU chart (10.11)
     regardless of variant, unlike the main plate numbering (which does
     differ - this variant's 6t single-piece Receptacle Plate 1 vs the EU
     chart's separate 13t Receptacle + 3t Plate 7). sourceNote updated to
     call this specific piece of the data confirmed rather than
     provisional, while the combo list as a whole stays provisional
     pending this variant's own OEM chart.

10.25 LTM 1160 diagram replaced with a person-supplied side-angle
      reference image
  10.9's isometric-corner-view diagram (imprecise hand-approximated
  hotspots, two overlapping faces at once, too intertwined for contour
  tracing) is replaced with a cleaner side-angle exploded view the person
  supplied directly - every plate drawn as its own separate,
  non-overlapping shape, with weights (4t/10t) and two dimensions
  (2750mm, 4270mm) baked into the image itself. Source is NOT confirmed
  to be the same chapter 4.07 document used for the combination data -
  the image crops off partial multi-language caption text on its left
  edge (French "...nt simplifié", Cyrillic "для"), suggesting a separate
  spec-sheet/brochure source rather than the German assembly manual.
  sourceNote and figRef both now say so explicitly rather than implying a
  single unified source, matching how the DXF-sourced diagrams (10.15/
  10.18/10.21) already caption themselves as person-supplied rather than
  claiming OEM artwork.
  Component ids/weights/combos are UNCHANGED from 10.9 - only
  diagramImage/diagramWidth/diagramHeight and each component's hotspots
  moved. Low-res source (523x464), upscaled 3x with Lanczos resampling
  before saving (matches the resolution other diagrams render at) -
  saved as JPEG not PNG, since this is a photographic/rendered raster
  with gradients, not clean CAD line art; PNG came out to ~970KB for the
  upscaled version vs ~210KB as JPEG at quality 90, for no visible
  difference on this source.
  Still hand-picked rectangles, not traced outlines - irregular die-cut
  plate edges on a low-res raster, same call as 10.9 and every other
  raster-photo-sourced diagram in this app.
  Caught one real bug before shipping: the first-pass rectangle for
  Plate 4 (the top 4t plate) extended down to y=178 (in the original
  523x464 coordinate space) to cover its full visible edge profile, which
  overlapped Plate 3's top box (starting at y=97) across roughly two
  thirds of the width - any click landing in that overlap zone hit
  Plate 4's hotspot instead of Plate 3's, since it's the topmost element
  in SVG paint order for overlapping regions. Found by clicking through
  all 6 components in a Playwright test and hitting a real timeout ("Plate
  4 intercepts pointer events") rather than by eyeballing the overlay
  image, where the overlap wasn't obviously visible. Fixed by shrinking
  Plate 4's box to y=27-90, sacrificing coverage of its lower edge
  profile (visible only on the left portion of the plate) in exchange for
  zero overlap with the stack below. Worth remembering for any future
  hand-picked-rectangle diagram: a visual overlay check alone doesn't
  reliably catch overlapping click targets - a scripted click-every-
  component pass does.

10.26 Diagram sizing normalized across all cranes; pinch-to-zoom added
  Two related UI requests. Before this, .cwt-image-wrap used width:100%
  + height:auto on the <img>, so each diagram rendered at ITS OWN native
  aspect ratio - fine while every diagram happened to be roughly
  landscape, but LTM 1110's diagram is portrait (0.80, width < height)
  while every other crane's is landscape (1.13 to 3.57), so 1110 rendered
  more than twice as tall as the others at the same container width,
  dominating the page and requiring much more scrolling to get past it.
  Fixed by giving .cwt-image-wrap a fixed height (min(460px, 60vw), the
  vw term only matters on very narrow viewports) and switching the <img>
  to width:100%/height:100%/object-fit:contain, which letterboxes any
  aspect ratio to fit the same box instead of dictating the box's own
  height. The hotspot SVG needed NO matching logic added - it already
  used preserveAspectRatio="xMidYMid meet" (same letterbox-to-fit
  behavior as object-fit:contain) with position:absolute/inset:0 filling
  the same box as the img, so once both share one fixed-size parent, they
  stay pixel-aligned automatically at any aspect ratio, zoomed or not.
  Verified all 6 cranes now report identical #cwt-image-wrap bounding
  boxes at a given viewport, and that hotspot clicks still land correctly
  on both extreme aspect ratios (1110 portrait, 1300 at 3.57).
  Pinch-to-zoom: added as a self-contained feature (cwtZoomState /
  cwtInitZoom() etc.) applying a CSS transform to a new #cwt-zoom-inner
  element wrapping the img+svg pair, itself a child of the now
  fixed-size, overflow:hidden #cwt-image-wrap - so zooming/panning can
  never grow the page layout, only pan/scale within the fixed box.
  Listeners are bound once (wrap.dataset.zoomBound guard) directly to
  #cwt-image-wrap, which renderCwtTab() never replaces - only the img src
  and the SVG's own children get swapped on re-render, so the zoom
  listeners and current zoom state both survive a crane switch or combo
  selection (zoom state is explicitly reset on crane switch in
  onCwtCraneChange() though, so switching crane doesn't carry over a
  zoom/pan position sized for a differently-shaped diagram).
  Deliberately did NOT set touch-action:none on the wrapper - that would
  block the browser's native single-finger vertical scroll for ANY touch
  starting on the diagram, even when not zoomed in, trapping a user's
  attempt to scroll past the diagram. Used touch-action:pan-y instead
  (blocks native pinch-zoom/horizontal-pan, allows native vertical
  scroll), and only claim a single-finger touch as a pan gesture in JS
  when cwtZoomState.scale > 1 at touchstart - at scale 1, single-finger
  touchmove is never preventDefault'd, so normal page scroll passes
  through untouched. Verified both cases directly: a vertical swipe over
  the diagram at scale 1 scrolls the page (confirmed via window.scrollY
  changing), and the same swipe after zooming in pans the image instead.
  Pinch math: track the two touch points' distance and midpoint at
  gesture start, scale relative to that start distance, and solve
  translate each frame so the content point that was under the midpoint
  at gesture start stays there as scale changes (standard "anchor the
  pinch center" approach) - without this the image would zoom from its
  top-left corner instead of from wherever the fingers actually are.
  Clamped so panned/zoomed content can never show empty space past its
  own edge (cwtClampZoomState). Double-tap and double-click both reset to
  1x - the only way back without a dedicated UI button - gated on the
  gesture never having moved (hadGesture flag) so releasing a real pinch
  or pan doesn't also fire a false reset.
  Verified end-to-end via Chromium's CDP Input.dispatchTouchEvent (not
  just Playwright's synthetic .tap(), which can't simulate true
  multi-touch): two-finger pinch reaches scale 4 and clamps there,
  single-finger drag after zooming in pans and updates translate to match
  the finger's exact delta, and double-tap correctly resets state to
  {scale:1, tx:0, ty:0}.

10.27 LTM 1650 diagram background inverted to match the other 5 cranes
  Only LTM 1650 still used its raw OEM JPG extraction (Fig. 156378) as
  diagramImage, and that extraction had a black background baked into
  the pixels - every other crane's diagram (DXF-rendered PNGs, plus
  1160's new reference-image JPG) has a white background, so 1650 stood
  out. Confirmed the file was pure grayscale first (R=G=B for every
  pixel, zero channel difference measured across the whole image) before
  touching it, since a plain color invert on a genuinely-colored image
  would produce nonsense - safe here because it isn't one. Inverted with
  PIL's ImageOps.invert() and re-saved in place (same filename, same
  dimensions) - white background, dark linework, matching the other
  diagrams' convention exactly. No data/hotspot changes; this is pixels
  only.

10.28 Real bug: counterweight diagrams could never actually update on a
      returning user's device, regardless of how many times
      CACHE_VERSION was bumped
  Person reported still seeing the old black-background LTM 1650 diagram
  after refreshing, despite 10.27's fix being live on main with a fresh
  CACHE_VERSION. Root cause: counterweight diagram images were never in
  sw.js's APP_SHELL list (only index.html/manifest/icons were) - the
  service worker's fetch handler cache-first-then-network's everything
  NOT in APP_SHELL into CONTENT_CACHE, a permanently-unversioned cache
  that the activate handler explicitly never clears (intentional, so a
  crew doesn't lose offline access to reeving diagrams they've already
  viewed across app updates). The first time anyone's browser fetched a
  counterweight diagram, it landed in CONTENT_CACHE and stayed there
  forever - every subsequent CACHE_VERSION bump only ever touched
  APP_SHELL_CACHE, which these images were never part of. This means
  EVERY counterweight diagram fix shipped this session (10.24's LTM 1160
  replacement, 10.23's LTM 1300 addition, 10.27's LTM 1650 invert) was
  invisible to anyone who'd viewed that crane's tab before the fix
  shipped, no matter how many version bumps followed.
  Fix, two parts:
  a) Added all 6 counterweight diagram paths to APP_SHELL, so they're
     precached and refreshed on every CACHE_VERSION bump exactly like
     index.html.
  b) That alone isn't sufficient - a stale copy could already be sitting
     in CONTENT_CACHE from before this fix, and caches.match() (used by
     the fetch handler for anything not explicitly checked against a
     named cache first) doesn't guarantee it prefers the newer entry in
     APP_SHELL_CACHE over an older one in CONTENT_CACHE; iteration order
     across caches isn't something to rely on. Fixed by (1) making the
     fetch handler check APP_SHELL_CACHE explicitly FIRST and return
     immediately on a hit, before ever consulting CONTENT_CACHE, and (2)
     having the activate handler purge any CONTENT_CACHE entry whose URL
     matches something now in APP_SHELL, cleaning up the orphaned
     duplicate rather than leaving it to linger unused.
  Verified with a real service-worker lifecycle test, not just a code
  read-through - Playwright against a local server, swapping the served
  sw.js and image mid-test to simulate "old file cached on a device" then
  "fix deployed to origin, browser checks for SW update":
    1. Serve the OLD sw.js (pre-fix, matching what was actually live) and
       the OLD black image. Load the app, browse to LTM 1650, confirm the
       image is black and lands in CONTENT_CACHE (reproducing the bug).
    2. Swap the served files to the NEW sw.js and the NEW white image
       (simulating a fresh deploy to origin) without touching the
       already-loaded page.
    3. Call registration.update() and wait for the controllerchange
       event specifically (not just polling reg.active, which can
       resolve fractionally before THIS client's controller actually
       flips over and produced a false negative on an earlier, less
       careful pass at this same test).
    4. Reload and re-check the same <img> element used by the real
       Counterweight tab: black -> white, confirmed pixel-for-pixel.
  Also confirmed CONTENT_CACHE's stale entry for the same URL was gone
  after the new SW activated, not just shadowed.
  This class of bug - "shipped a fix, bumped the version, it's still not
  visible" - won't recur for future counterweight diagram changes now
  that they're in APP_SHELL, but is worth remembering as a category: any
  asset that CAN change over the app's development lifetime needs to be
  in the versioned precache, not left to fall into the permanent content
  cache by default just because it's an image fetched on demand.

================================================================================

10.29 LTM 1300 diagram line weight thickened - was 1-2px on a 4643px
      canvas, effectively invisible at the app's actual display width
  Measured before touching anything: sampled dark-pixel run lengths
  across the image and confirmed line strokes were 1-2px wide on a
  4643x1300 canvas - at the app's real display size (~900px wide in a
  normal viewport), that's sub-pixel, explaining why it read as washed-
  out/faint compared to the other diagrams.
  Deliberately did NOT re-render from the source DXF to fix this - the
  DXF's own $EXTMIN/$EXTMAX header didn't cleanly match the existing
  PNG's 4643x1300 aspect ratio (7162x1775 modelspace units vs a 3.57
  aspect ratio image), meaning the exact figsize/dpi/margins used for the
  original render aren't recoverable from the DXF header alone, and
  guessing at them risks silently shifting where each plate lands in
  pixel space - which would have broken every hotspot coordinate already
  measured and shipped for this crane (10.23/10.24). Thickening the
  EXISTING raster in place sidesteps that risk entirely: it's a pure
  image-space operation (PIL ImageFilter.MinFilter, grayscale erosion,
  applied once) that darkens/expands existing dark pixels without moving
  anything, so every previously-derived hotspot rectangle is still
  exactly as valid as before.
  Tested single vs double MinFilter(3) pass side by side, downscaled to
  the app's actual ~900px display width before judging (full-resolution
  comparison would have been misleading - the goal is legibility at
  display size, not at 4643px). Single pass was the right call - clearly
  more defined, "10t" labels still crisp; double pass started looking
  heavy-handed on the small text. Same 4643x1300 dimensions preserved,
  re-verified hotspot click-through and alignment in-browser before
  shipping - unaffected, as expected from a pixel-darkening-only change.

================================================================================

10.30 LTM 1300 Receptacle Plate 1 - genuine gap in the source DXF export,
      one support post's inner line stopped short of the base line
  Person spotted this visually from a cropped screenshot (no coordinates
  given), not from a report against a specific known plate - took several
  wrong guesses across LTM 1130/1250/1110/1650 before landing on the
  right crane (confirmed by the person after being shown candidates) and
  then the right shape within it (the "5"-like notch cutout at the top of
  one of Receptacle Plate 1's two support posts, an actual physical
  detail on the part, not a text label). Once located: the post's LEFT
  edge (x=2682-2685) ran continuously from the notch at y=867 down to
  y=1081, then just stopped - a ~95px gap before the black base line at
  y=1176-1180. The MATCHING right edge of the same post, and the mirror-
  image post on the other side of the same receptacle, both ran
  continuously to the base with no gap - confirming this was a one-off
  export artifact, not a systematic issue.
  Fixed by measuring the existing segment's exact color (129,129,129,
  matching its anti-aliased 132,132,132 edge) and width (4px) directly
  from the image, then drawing a matching line segment closing the exact
  gap (y=1081 to y=1177) with PIL - not a re-render, not a redrawn
  outline, just closing the one missing stroke to match its neighbors
  pixel-for-pixel. Same image dimensions, no hotspot coordinates
  affected. Re-verified click-through in-browser before shipping.
  Worth remembering: when a person reports "missing line" or similar
  without pixel coordinates, checking every candidate crane's full
  diagram at thumbnail scale isn't enough - this defect was invisible at
  thumbnail/overview zoom and only became findable by cropping into the
  specific shape at high magnification with a coordinate grid overlaid,
  the same technique used for hotspot alignment work throughout this
  session.

10.31 Second, genuinely separate gap on LTM 1300 Receptacle Plate 1,
      found via a person-supplied alignment DXF pointing at it directly
  Person drew a single LINE entity over a screenshot of the diagram (a
  downscaled ~1996x559 copy of the shipped 4643x1300 PNG - image_size in
  the DXF's own IMAGE entity confirmed the scale factor, same recovery
  method as the LTM 1650 alignment DXF, 10.22) and sent the DXF rather
  than describing coordinates. Converted the line's endpoints through the
  same wcs-to-pixel math, scaled up by the ratio between the reference
  image and the shipped PNG (4643/1996 = 2.326), landing on x=2535-2683,
  y=1079 in the full image.
  This is NOT the same defect as 10.30 (same general area, easy to
  confuse) - 10.30 was a missing VERTICAL segment on the support post;
  this is a missing HORIZONTAL segment on the wedge-shaped part directly
  below it, where the wedge's flat top should run continuously into the
  post's edge but instead had a 143px gap (x=2538-2681 at y=1079) between
  the end of the ramp's diagonal-then-flat top and where the vertical
  post line crosses that row. Confirmed both the gap's existence and its
  exact extent via a raw pixel row/column scan (not just eyeballing a
  crop) before patching - the visual crop alone was ambiguous about
  whether a line was continuous through that junction or not, since the
  post's vertical line crosses directly through the same area.
  Fixed the same way as 10.30: sampled the existing segment's exact color
  (6,6,6, near-black, distinct from the gray (129,129,129) used for
  10.30's line - different parts/layers in the source use different
  shades) and width (~3px), drew the missing stroke with PIL. Same
  dimensions, hotspots unaffected, re-verified click-through in-browser.
  Method worth keeping: when a person can supply a DXF with even a single
  guide line over a screenshot, that's far more precise and far less back
  -and-forth than describing "there's a gap around here" - convert
  through the image's own embedded scale info exactly like the alignment
  DXFs, don't ask them to estimate pixel coordinates by hand.

10.32 LTM 1160 hotspots upgraded from hand-picked rectangles to traced
      outlines - person drew a box around each plate's real silhouette
  10.25 shipped the new side-angle diagram with rectangle hotspots
      ("still hand-picked rectangles, not traced outlines... irregular
      die-cut plate edges"). Person sent back a DXF (same alignment-DXF
      technique as 1650/1300) with an actual outline traced around each
      of the 6 plates in AutoCAD, following each plate's real chamfered/
      notched silhouette rather than a bounding box.
  Complication: the three 10t plates (p3a/p3b/p3c) are drawn touching
  each other in the diagram, and were traced as ONE connected outline
  (86 LINE entities total, forming 4 separate connected components: p4
  alone, the merged p3a+b+c stack, p2 alone, p1 alone) rather than 3
  independent closed shapes - the person asked directly whether they
  needed to redraw complete separate boxes instead. They didn't: since
  the perspective in this diagram is a true isometric angle (each
  plate's own top/bottom edges are NOT flat horizontal lines - they
  slope), a naive "split by Y-coordinate range" approach would have cut
  through the wrong points. Used proper planar-graph face-tracing
  instead (treat the shared-edge graph as a planar subdivision, sort
  each vertex's neighbors by angle, walk consistently-oriented half-
  edges to recover each bounded face) - this is the general solution for
  "person traced touching shapes as one connected outline, recover the
  individual polygons," not a 1160-specific hack, and doesn't care about
  the perspective distortion since it works purely from graph topology,
  not coordinates. Confirmed the 3 extracted faces against the known
  divider vertices (p3a contains the stack's topmost-left corner, p3c
  contains the bottommost-left corner, p3b is the remainder) before
  trusting the split.
  The other 3 shapes (p4: 9 vertices, p2: 22, p1: 21) were already simple
  closed loops (degree-2 everywhere) - ordered by direct cycle walk, no
  face-tracing needed.
  Scale/insert recovery used the exact same method as the 1650/1300
  alignment DXFs (IMAGE entity's insert point + u_pixel/v_pixel + own
  image_size, cross-checked against the shipped diagram's known
  1569x1392 dimensions before trusting it - this reference image was the
  full-resolution black-background inversion sent for annotation, so no
  extra reference-to-shipped scale-up factor was needed this time, unlike
  1300's downscaled screenshot in 10.31).
  Verified all 6 polygons render without self-intersection, checked
  visually against the diagram, then re-verified in-browser: all 6 tap
  targets register correctly, sum to the right weight, gold highlight now
  hugs each plate's actual outline instead of a rectangular
  approximation. No component ids, weights, or combos changed - hotspot
  geometry only.

10.33 LTM 1160 and LTM 1300's interchangeable Plate 3 copies weren't
      actually interchangeable for combo-matching purposes - swept every
      crane for the same latent bug flagged back in 10.24
  Person reported directly: selecting p1+p2+p3a on LTM 1160 correctly
  flagged 30t as OEM-approved, but p1+p2+p3b (or p3c) - same weight,
  physically identical plate - did not. Root cause was exactly what
  10.24 predicted when fixing LTM 1650's interchangeable 10t positions:
  "This wasn't needed for LTM 1160's own repeated-plate components
  (p3a/b/c, 10.9) since that crane's combos only ever reach 3 total
  copies and were already always listed as the same front-loaded
  canonical subset - the mismatch risk exists there too in principle,
  just never surfaced... flagging here in case it's worth doing if 1160
  ever gets the same free-click complaint." It did.
  Fix was one line per component - just adding
  interchangeableGroup: 'plate3_1160' to p3a/p3b/p3c. No logic changes
  needed; cwtMatchSignature() (built for 1650) already handles any
  interchangeableGroup-tagged component generically.
  Person then asked for a full sweep, not just the reported crane.
  Checked every crane's component list for duplicate `name` values
  (the actual signal for "physically identical/interchangeable part")
  lacking the tag. Found three affected cranes total:
    - LTM 1160: p3a/p3b/p3c ("Counterweight Plate 3" x3) - fixed here.
    - LTM 1300: p3a/p3b ("Counterweight Plate 3" x2) - same bug, same
      fix, same session (was flagged as a known risk in 10.31/10.32's
      area but not yet triggered/fixed until now).
    - LTM 1650: p10a-p10g - already fixed in 10.24, confirmed still
      correctly tagged, no action needed.
  LTM 1250, 1110, and 1130 have NO duplicate-named components at all -
  every part in those three is uniquely positioned, so this bug class
  structurally cannot occur there. Nothing to fix, not just nothing
  found.
  Verified both fixes directly: LTM 1160 p1+p2+p3b now correctly reads
  30.0t/OEM-approved (previously flagged as not approved despite correct
  weight). LTM 1300 p1+p5+p2+p3b (note p5 is mandatory in every listed
  1300 combo, unlike 1160 - a first test pass without it correctly
  showed "not approved" for the RIGHT reason, worth remembering before
  assuming a not-approved result means the interchangeableGroup fix
  didn't work) now correctly reads 36.0t/OEM-approved.

10.34 Added a "Full OEM manual" reference link to the Counterweight tab,
      starting with LTM 1110
  Person wants a way to point at the actual complete OEM manual for each
  crane, not just the extracted/re-drawn diagram and the short sourceNote
  citation already shown. Explicit design constraint from the person:
  "id rather not chop up the offical document as this is my way of
  showing a source of truth maybe we just put the hyperlink in there and
  tell them what page number to go to them selfs." Two attempts at
  auto-jumping to the right page were tried and both failed on live
  testing before that decision was made: Google Drive's viewer does not
  honour a #page=N URL fragment on either /view or /preview - person
  confirmed both open to page 1 regardless of the fragment. A
  direct-download link would let the browser's native PDF viewer handle
  #page=N (which does support it), but large files can trip Drive's
  "can't scan for viruses" interstitial, which would break the link for
  no real gain over just asking the person to flip to a page number
  themselves - not worth it against the stated preference to keep the
  source document whole and unmodified anyway.
  Implementation is deliberately tiny: two new optional fields per crane
  in COUNTERWEIGHT_DATA, manualUrl and manualPage. When present, a plain
  link ("open document") plus a "go to page N for the counterweight
  combinations" line renders under the Composition card's existing sourceNote
  disclaimer; when absent (every crane except 1110 so far) the element
  stays hidden, so this doesn't force placeholder text onto cranes
  without a manual link yet. Person has the manuals for the other cranes
  and confirmed they'll supply a link + page number for each in turn -
  this field just gets filled in per crane as those arrive, no further
  code changes needed per crane after this.
  First link: LTM 1110's Google Drive copy, page 861 for the
  counterweight combinations (originally mislabelled "LTM1100" by the person in
  the same message that shared the link - initially read as the
  separate, still-pending LRT 1100-2.1 tab per 10.12's page-mismatch
  issue; person corrected this directly: "what i shared was for the ltm
  1110t" - so it maps to the already-shipped LTM 1110, not LRT 1100).
  Verified in-browser: selecting LTM 1110 shows the link with the
  correct href and "page 861" text; every other crane's tab shows no
  link element at all (checked LTM 1250 directly via Playwright, and by
  code inspection the same applies to 1130/1160/1650/1300 since none
  have manualUrl set yet).

10.35 Reference Documentation card redesign - matched the Reeving tab's
      "Offline Availability" block, added best-effort auto-page-jump,
      and set aside a placeholder for a future "Driving with the
      Equipment in Place" tab
  Person wanted the manual link to look more substantial, pointing
  directly at the Reeving tab's "Offline Availability" card (title +
  muted description line + full-width gold .btn) as the visual target,
  rather than a plain disclaimer line buried under the Composition
  table. Rebuilt cwt-manual-link as its own card, cwt-manual-card: card
  title, a .hook-badge showing "Page N" (reused from the Hook Block tab
  - green pill, already used elsewhere for this exact "here's the
  number that matters" purpose), a short muted explanation, and a
  full-width .btn styled/sized identically to reeve-cache-all-btn that
  opens the document in a new tab. Hidden entirely (display:none) for
  any crane without manualUrl set, same as before.
  Person also asked to convert the stored links into whatever form
  gives the auto-page-jump function its best shot, accepting it may
  only work on some devices: "if no too bad they can manually do it
  them selfs." Google Drive's own /view and /preview viewers don't
  honour #page=N (confirmed dead in 10.34), but a raw PDF response
  opened directly in a browser's native PDF viewer does support #page=N
  per the PDF Open Parameters spec - Drive's direct-download endpoint
  (drive.google.com/uc?export=download&id=<id>) serves exactly that.
  Implemented as a generic helper, cwtManualHref(data), rather than
  hand-converting each stored link: it regex-matches the standard
  /file/d/<id>/ pattern out of whatever share-link URL is stored in
  manualUrl, rebuilds it as the uc?export=download form, and appends
  #page=N from manualPage. This means every future crane's link just
  gets pasted in as the normal Drive "view" share link (whatever format
  the person copies from Drive's share dialog) with no manual
  reformatting - the conversion happens once, generically, at render
  time. Falls back to using manualUrl as-is (plus the #page= fragment)
  if a link doesn't match the expected Drive pattern, so a non-Drive
  link still works as a plain link. Explicitly best-effort per the
  person's own framing - large files can still hit Drive's "can't scan
  for viruses, download anyway" interstitial, and there's no way to
  detect either outcome in advance, so the Page N badge is always shown
  regardless of whether the jump itself works.
  Separately, person flagged a new tab coming later - "Driving with the
  Equipment in Place" (Driving_with_the_equipment_in_place) - and said
  documents/links for it should just be held per crane until that tab
  is actually built, not forced into the counterweight tab's Reference
  Documentation card. Noted here for the record; no data field or UI
  added yet since no tab exists to render it in and no links have been
  shared for it yet - next thing to do when the first one arrives is
  hold it in a plain per-crane object (not yet designed) until the tab
  itself is scoped.
  Verified in-browser (Playwright): LTM 1110 shows the card with badge
  text "Page 861" and button href
  "https://drive.google.com/uc?export=download&id=1MFDP-Uu-HRINNT5bitsJU3TrK79YTVuf#page=861";
  switching to LTM 1250 hides the card entirely (display:none).

10.38 Reverted the direct-download link conversion from 10.35 - it forced
      an actual file download instead of opening in a tab
  Person noticed the Reference Documentation buttons were triggering a
  download rather than opening the manual to look at. Root cause: Google
  Drive's uc?export=download endpoint (used in 10.35 to give the
  #page=N auto-jump its best shot) sends a Content-Disposition:
  attachment response header, which forces the browser to save the file
  instead of displaying it - regardless of whether the browser has a
  native PDF viewer that would otherwise honour #page=N. That's a strictly
  worse outcome than what the button is for (open and read the manual),
  and the auto-jump it was chasing was already only a maybe (10.34 found
  Drive's own /view and /preview never honour #page=N at all). Reverted
  cwtManualHref() to a plain pass-through of the stored manualUrl -
  Drive's normal /view link, which opens in a new tab like any other
  link and doesn't force a download. Also softened the description text
  in cwtManualDescText() to stop promising a "some devices" auto-jump
  that isn't attempted anymore - now just tells the person to use the
  page number themselves. This lands back on the person's original
  design from 10.34: plain hyperlink + page number, nothing clever
  attempted. Verified in-browser: LTM 1300's button href is now the
  plain drive.google.com/file/d/.../view?usp=drive_link link, not a
  uc?export=download one.

10.39 Built the LTR 1220 Counterweight tab - new crane, box-traced DXF +
      OEM combo PDF, both supplied by the person
  Two-part delivery: first a DXF with the diagram's actual OEM artwork
  imported via AutoCAD PDFIMPORT plus 8 hand-drawn boxes around each
  distinct plate silhouette (same box-tracing workflow as 1160's 10.32),
  sent for review before any data was attached; then, once boxes were
  confirmed good, a combo PDF with the actual weights/composition table.
  Box review: no IMAGE entity this time - the entire diagram was
  PDFIMPORT'd as vector LWPOLYLINE geometry (216 entities on
  PDF_Visible) with 8 more on a Boxes layer. Rendered the DXF to a PNG,
  zoomed into every shared box boundary, both lifting lugs, the notched
  base plate, and the stepped hitch/pin detail at the top of the
  pedestal - every box hugged the actual linework exactly, no clipping,
  no gaps at shared edges. Sent back a numbered reference render (not
  live) for the person to identify each box against.
  PDF decode: same -29 character-shift substitution cipher as every
  other Liebherr combo PDF this session (10.1/10.4/10.34's caveat that
  it needed re-confirming per document) - confirmed it still applies,
  but this time the plain page.get_text() concatenation lost the
  numeric weight values entirely because digits are encoded as control
  characters (0x13-0x1A) sitting below the printable-ASCII floor my
  original decode function handled. Switched to per-span dict-mode
  extraction and a decode that shifts every character except literal
  space by +29 with no wraparound (rather than the mod-94 rotation used
  before) - recovered the full combo table: 0.0t (empty), 10.0t
  (receptacle plate 1 alone), 30/50/70t (receptacle + 1/2/3 pairs of
  "counterweight plate 2" + "counterweight plate 3").
  Person's plain-language box assignment, verbatim: "0 and 1 are the
  same thing" (the base plate and pedestal outlines are two disjoint
  shapes but one physical/OEM-listed part), "3/2 are a pair, 5/4 are a
  pair, 7/6 are a pair" (three stacked tiers, each a left+right pair),
  "the pairs get selected together as you cant just put on one"
  (corrected mid-sentence from "can just put one on" - both-or-neither,
  not independently selectable), and "like the other cwt combos with
  multi of the same slab type i want them to be able to built and
  selected any any order" (the 3 tiers are interchangeable with each
  other, not fixed positions).
  Modelled as: p1 (Receptacle plate 1, 10.0t) with separate:true and
  TWO hotspots (boxes 0+1) for one id - same pattern as 1300's
  aux5/aux6, just reused for "one part, two disjoint outline pieces"
  instead of "one part, two mirrored mounting positions". Each tier is
  two ids - p2x (right, "Counterweight plate 2") and p3x (left,
  "Counterweight plate 3") - linked to each other via linkedId (1110's
  p3/p4 pattern, gives the both-or-neither click behaviour) AND each
  tagged with an interchangeableGroup shared across all 3 tiers'
  same-named id (plate2_1220 for all three p2x, plate3_1220 for all
  three p3x - two groups, not one, since the OEM chart names them as
  two distinct parts even though they're always equal in count). This
  is the first crane to combine linkedId and interchangeableGroup on
  the same components - confirmed in the engine they're orthogonal
  (linkedId governs toggle-time selection, interchangeableGroup only
  affects combo-signature matching) with no special-casing needed.
  Diagram rendering picked up a real refinement over the dpi-cranking
  workaround from 10.somewhere (ezdxf's Frontend.draw_layout(finalize=
  True) silently overriding whatever figsize/xlim/ylim was set
  beforehand, discovered again here): rather than fighting it, let
  finalize=True auto-fit as it always does, then READ BACK ax.get_xlim()
  /get_ylim() afterward and use those (now-authoritative, content-driven)
  values plus the actual saved-canvas pixel size to build an exact
  linear world-to-pixel transform - spot-checked against multiple known
  box corners/edges before trusting it for the real hotspot coordinates,
  all within 1-4px (line-width tolerance). Also used
  Configuration(background_policy=BackgroundPolicy.WHITE, color_policy=
  ColorPolicy.BLACK) to get a clean white-bg/black-line render directly
  instead of the invert-after-the-fact approach used for 1650 (10.28) -
  cleaner since this DXF's dark-bg render didn't have a uniform
  near-zero-channel-difference background the way 1650's did, so a
  blind invert produced an off-white/beige cast rather than true white.
  Wired up as a normal new crane entry - manualUrl/manualPage moved out
  of PENDING_REFERENCE_LINKS.cwtCombosPendingTab now that the tab it was
  waiting on exists (LRT 1100-2.1 remains held, still no tab). Verified
  in-browser: p1 alone reads 10.0t/OEM-approved; clicking only the
  right-side hotspot of any tier auto-selects its left-side partner
  (and vice versa) via linkedId; deselecting one side drops both;
  selecting tier-2 + tier-3 (skipping tier-1) still correctly reads
  50.0t/OEM-approved, proving the interchangeableGroup matching doesn't
  care which physical tiers were picked, only how many.

  Person flagged LTM 1300's figRef directly: "Re-drawn from a
  user-supplied exploded 3D CAD model (DXF) of this variant's actual
  counterweight stack - not OEM artwork." Same class of issue as
  10.36/10.37's sourceNote sweeps, just a different field this time -
  10.36 only checked sourceNote and left figRef alone on the (wrong)
  assumption that "not OEM artwork" needed the production-process detail
  attached to be credible. Checked every crane's figRef and found the
  same pattern in five of seven: 1250/1110/1130 all said "diagram
  re-drawn from an exploded 3D CAD model, not the original OEM artwork"
  verbatim; 1160 said "Re-drawn from a user-supplied reference image,
  not the chapter 4.07 OEM artwork..."; 1300 had the DXF mention flagged
  above; 1220 (built this session, 10.39) said "...AutoCAD PDFIMPORT..."
  - same mistake made fresh, not even old debt.
  Stripped the process description from all six, keeping only the
  user-relevant fact (is this the actual OEM artwork or not, and for
  1160/1300, whether it's confirmed to be the same source as the combo
  data): 1250/1110/1130 -> "diagram re-drawn as clean line art, not the
  original OEM artwork"; 1160 -> "Not the chapter 4.07 OEM artwork used
  for the combination data above."; 1300 -> "Diagram re-drawn as clean
  line art from this variant's actual counterweight stack — not OEM
  artwork."; 1220 -> "Diagram traced directly from the OEM combination
  chart's own artwork, not a freehand redraw." 1650 was already clean
  ("actual OEM exploded-view artwork, extracted directly, not
  redrawn.") - no DXF/CAD/PDFIMPORT/user-supplied mention, left as-is.
  Grepped both sourceNote and figRef together afterward for
  DXF/3D CAD/user-supplied/PDFIMPORT/AutoCAD/pixel-exact across all
  seven cranes - zero matches. Verified all seven render their new
  figRef text correctly in-browser.

10.41 New tab: Driving with Equipment in Place - test build, LTM 1110 only
  Built as an explicit test per the person's request ("lets do a test to
  see how you go and send a preview before anything goes live") before
  committing to the full design. Preceded by a training exchange (not
  logged here in detail, see chat) that established the vocabulary:
  VEHICLE (carrier/chassis) vs SUPER STRUCTURE (rotating turret/cab/
  boom), FRONT/REAR as fixed ends of the vehicle (not the boom's current
  direction), and that "boom direction to front/rear" means the
  superstructure has been slewed 180° so the boom points over one end
  of the vehicle or the other - confirmed directly by the person: "you
  are right it was rotated 180 degrees if the booms facing the rear
  thats the rear charts and if the facing the front front chart."
  Source PDF (LTM 1110-5-1-000, doc 0213844-00, chapter 15.05) has NO
  character-shift cipher, unlike every Counterweight Combinations PDF
  processed so far this session - page.get_text() came back plain and
  immediately readable. Worth remembering per-document-category, not
  assumed universal: the cipher is a Counterweight-chart-specific export
  quirk, not a property of every Liebherr PDF in this app.
  Data model, per the person's own field breakdown from the training
  exchange:
    - Inputs: crane model, boom direction (front/rear, forced choice -
      no default), counterweight location (superstructure/vehicle,
      forced choice, only rendered for cranes with
      hasVehicleOption:true - not exercised yet, no crane in the test
      data has this set), counterweight weight (populates every output
      field below it).
    - Outputs per matched row: hook block max weight, boom angle range,
      support width, max axle load for axle group A, max axle load for
      axle group B. Axle group labels (e.g. "1 ... 2" / "3 ... 5") are
      stored per-crane-per-direction rather than hardcoded, since axle
      count/grouping varies by chassis.
    - Forced-choice reasoning matches the Counterweight tab's existing
      philosophy (10.something on Counterweight location) - a silent
      default risks someone reading pre-populated axle-load numbers as
      correct for their actual setup without ever confirming which
      configuration they're really in. Both toggles start with neither
      button active; the weight dropdown stays disabled with a
      placeholder until a valid direction (+location where applicable)
      is chosen.
    - Duplicate-weight rows (same counterweight, two valid chart entries
      differing in boom angle/support width/axle loads) are never
      merged - both render stacked under one "Option A" / "Option B"
      label with a divider between them, per explicit instruction:
      "id like them both displace one below each other with clean
      deliniation please."
      UPDATE (person found the actual OEM explanation later, in LTM
      1160-5.2's own Driving w/ Equipment manual - supersedes whatever
      informal reasoning either of us had for why two rows exist per
      counterweight): the manual's own Note reads "For 'travel operation
      with equipment in place', for the same set up configuration there
      are different values for the 'Telescopic boom angle to the
      horizontal' and the resulting 'maximum axle load per axle'."
      followed by two labelled examples - Example 1 = the permissible
      boom angle WITHOUT optimized axle load values, Example 2 = the
      OPTIMAL boom angle WITH optimized (i.e. more favorable, lower)
      axle load values for that same physical setup. So "Option A" /
      "Option B" in the app aren't two arbitrary alternate readings of
      the chart - they're specifically the un-optimized vs
      axle-load-optimized boom angle for driving with the same
      equipment fitted, straight from the OEM's own worked-example
      structure. Current UI labels ("Option A"/"Option B") don't convey
      this - worth relabeling if the person wants it (e.g. "Permissible
      angle" / "Optimized angle (reduced axle load)"), not yet done as
      of this note.
  Data extraction: per-span dict-mode text extraction (ArialMT for data,
  Arial-BoldMT for headers), rows auto-grouped by pattern-matching 6
  consecutive fields against expected shapes (weight: \d+\.\d, hook
  block: "N ... N", boom angle: contains °, support width: \d.\d\d,
  two integer axle loads) rather than fixed positional slicing, since
  row count varies per crane/direction and this needed to self-
  terminate at the trailer/footer text. Verified against the source: 23
  rows on each of front/rear, correctly split into 14 distinct
  counterweight values front (9 duplicated) and 12 distinct rear (11
  duplicated) - reconciled by hand against the row count before trusting
  the parse.
  One field intentionally NOT built yet: support width as a percentage
  of full outrigger spread (100/75/50/0%) - person asked for this, but
  the only outrigger dimension data in the app (cadFleetData, used by
  the Outrigger Support Positioning tab) is pad radius+angle from the
  slew center at full deployment only, a different measurement to the
  "min. B" beam-spread figure in this chart, and the two front pads'
  angles aren't symmetric (135°/143° for the 1110) so deriving a width
  from that data risked reading it wrong. Declined to guess at a
  safety-relevant dimension - person will locate the actual
  percentage-to-metres reference document instead.
  Sent three screenshots as the requested preview (empty forced-choice
  state, Front 0.0t, Rear 29.0t - both showing the stacked Option A/B
  layout) before anything was committed. Person approved pushing to
  live afterward: "lets just leave as default for now till i have find
  the info push to live and lemme see it in actions."

10.42 Driving with Equipment in Place - added LTM 1300, which forced a
      real schema change (boom length is now a variable, not fixed)
  10.41's LTM 1110 test only had one boom notation (T-11.5) across both
  its front/rear pages, so DRIVING_EQ_DATA had no concept of boom
  length at all - direction was the only axis. LTM 1300's chapter
  (029931-00, 15 pages) broke that assumption immediately: TWO boom
  configurations - T-14.7 (0/0/0/0/0/0), fully retracted, with front,
  rear, AND vehicle-mount tables; and T-19.6 (0/0/46/0/0/0), section 3
  extended to 46%, front only, no rear, no vehicle option at all.
  Person's instruction before building: "only list the options for
  cranes that has chart that differ from full retracted." Restructured
  DRIVING_EQ_DATA so every crane has a boomConfigs array - LTM 1110
  keeps its single T-11.5 entry, LTM 1300 gets two. The new Boom
  Configuration picker (mode-toggle, same forced-choice pattern as
  direction/location) only renders when boomConfigs.length > 1, so
  every single-config crane (1110, and every crane added after this
  without a partially-extended chart) never shows it at all - confirmed
  1110 unaffected, boom-config-group stays display:none exactly as
  before.
  Cross-availability had to be wired properly rather than just hidden:
  Rear and Vehicle-location are only valid for boom configs that
  actually have that data. Added .mode-btn:disabled CSS (opacity 0.35,
  cursor not-allowed) and disable Front/Rear per the selected boom
  config's own directions object (T-19.6 disables Rear entirely, since
  cfg.directions.rear doesn't exist for it) - Location group itself
  hides completely when the boom config has no vehicleWeights, same
  pattern as before just re-evaluated on boom config change now, not
  only crane change. Additionally, choosing Vehicle location forces
  Direction to Front and disables Rear regardless of boom config, since
  every vehicle-mount page in the source PDF is boom-to-front only -
  there's no rear vehicle-mount table to ever show.
  Vehicle-mount data itself is stored differently to the
  front/rear tables - keyed by the discrete package weight (96, 86, 76,
  66, 56, 46, 36, 26, 16, 13, 6t) rather than living inside a
  direction's row list, since these are 11 separate OEM pages, not one
  table with a varying weight column. Confirmed on every single page:
  "Counterweight on superstructure" reads 0.0t, matching the design
  decided before any of this data existed - added a
  "Counterweight on superstructure: 0.0t" row to the output specifically
  for the vehicle-location case, since it's genuinely present in the
  source table and worth surfacing, not implied.
  Nailed down an ambiguity from the original design conversation: the
  person's own worked example ("96.0 500...1600 55°...82° 2.68 33 34 /
  96.0 ... 69°...79° ... 32 32") is NOT from a vehicle-mount page at
  all - it's the T-14.7 REAR superstructure table's own 96.0t row,
  confirmed by exact match against the extracted data. The "96t" in the
  vehicle-mount page titles ("96t counterweight, placed on the vehicle")
  is an unrelated, separately-numbered package - worth flagging since
  skimming the source PDF could easily conflate the two.
  Extraction used the same per-span dict-mode + pattern-matched 6-field
  grouping as 10.41, run per-page across all 15 pages; boom notation,
  direction, and (where present) the vehicle package weight were parsed
  from each page's own header spans rather than assumed from page order.
  Verified in-browser end to end: T-14.7 Rear 96.0t reproduces the
  person's example exactly; Vehicle location correctly lists all 11
  packages, forces Front, disables Rear, and shows 0.0t superstructure
  per row; switching to T-19.6 disables Rear and hides Location
  entirely, front-only 16.0t/13.0t data renders correctly; no console
  errors on any path.
  Per the person's own note: they may drop T-19.6 (and any other
  not-fully-retracted chart) from this tab later on. Kept for now since
  it's real OEM data and they explicitly said to try it and push to
  live regardless.
