10.43 LTM 1110's Rear table was badly truncated - fixed with a fuller PDF,
      and hit a genuinely new extraction bug along the way
  Person: "i missed a few pages from the list so look over it again more
  on the to the rear side of things," with a re-supplied version of the
  same document (same doc code 0213844-00/ver_2197110102) - this one 5
  pages instead of the original 2. Page 0 is Front, unchanged from what
  was already shipped. Pages 1-4 are ALL "Boom direction to the rear"
  (same vtab_..._02001_00_000 code repeated across all four) - one long
  table continuing across page breaks, not four separate charts. The
  original 2-page PDF only had page 1 of that continuation, so 10.41's
  Rear data stopped at 24.4t - the real table goes all the way to 0.0t.
  This version of the document also turned out to have the -29
  character-shift cipher active (10.41's original 2-page PDF did not -
  confirms per-document/per-export encoding is not something to assume
  either way, per the earlier note to build a training reference for
  this).
  Hit a new, nastier variant of the digit-decoding bug first found on
  LTR 1220 (10.39): calling .strip() on a span's RAW, still-encoded text
  - either to trim it or just to test truthiness before deciding whether
  to keep it - silently corrupts or drops content, because several of
  the cipher's control-character byte values for digits (found here:
  raw \x1c, representing digit '9') fall inside Python's Unicode
  whitespace definition. Two distinct symptoms from the same root cause:
  calling .strip() and keeping the result truncated "10.9" down to
  "10." (lost the trailing digit); calling raw.strip() only as an
  emptiness check before appending the (unstripped) text dropped entire
  rows outright, whenever a span's encoded bytes happened to ALL fall in
  that whitespace-like range. Fix: never call .strip() on cipher text
  before decoding - decode first with the +29 shift, THEN strip/test the
  decoded (now normal-ASCII) result if needed. Also had to special-case
  the degree sign here: its raw byte (0x83) doesn't survive the
  no-wraparound +29 shift into the correct Unicode code point for '°'
  (U+00B0/176) the way ASCII letters/digits do, so it's mapped
  explicitly rather than trusting the arithmetic.
  Re-extracted all 5 pages with the fixed decoder: page 0 (Front) came
  back byte-identical to what's already shipped, confirming no other
  damage; pages 1-4 (Rear) combined to 78 rows across 39 distinct
  counterweight values (previously only 12 were shown), every one with
  exactly 2 boom-angle-range options, continuity checked at every page
  break (e.g. 24.4t's second option starts page 2 right after its first
  option ends page 1, no gap or duplicate). Replaced the Rear rows array
  wholesale rather than appending, since the old 12-weight set was a
  strict prefix of the new 39. Verified in-browser: Rear now lists 39
  weights, 0.0t shows both options correctly, Front (14 weights) and
  LTM 1300 unaffected.

================================================================================

10.44 Driving with Equipment in Place - added LTM 1130, LTM 1160, LTM
      1250; recommended fleet hook block cross-reference
  Person sent four more PDFs in one batch (1650, 1130, 1160, 1250),
  explicitly asking to hold the 1650 one till last "as you might have
  questions." Processed the other three, none needed a question, all
  pushed live per the standing instruction ("if no problems push to
  live").
  None of these three PDFs use the character-shift cipher - plain text
  throughout, same as 1110's original 2-page upload. Confirms again this
  is a Counterweight-chart-specific quirk, not universal.
  Three genuinely new data-shape variations, each requiring a small
  schema/extraction change rather than just new numbers:
    - LTM 1130 (doc 028799-00, 2016): only "boom direction towards the
      rear" is present for the plain telescopic boom - no Front table in
      this upload, and the document's own next-section header
      ("2 Telescopic boom T-12.7 (0/0/0/0/0) with folding jib K-2.9")
      confirms there's at least one more config with no table for it
      either. Built with only directions.rear present - the existing
      per-boom-config Front/Rear disable logic (built for 1300's T-19.6)
      handled this with no changes needed. This document's table also
      has NO "Hook block" column at all (older/simpler chart format) -
      added conditional rendering so that row only appears when
      r.hookBlock is actually present on the row object, instead of
      assuming every crane has one.
    - LTM 1160 and LTM 1250: introduce a different config axis to 1300's
      boom length - "Outer radius of counterweight" (5.3m/4.8m for 1160,
      5.6m/4.8m for 1250), each with its own Front AND Rear table (no
      front/rear gaps here, unlike 1130/1300). Reused the boomConfigs
      array mechanism as-is since it's the same shape (a labelled config
      axis with its own directions) - the label text just describes a
      radius instead of a boom length, no code changes needed beyond
      that. Front's lightest rows are numerically IDENTICAL between the
      two radii on both cranes - confirmed directly against the source
      text before trusting it, not a copy-paste artifact; a genuine OEM
      coincidence at the light end where the counterweight's mounting
      radius apparently doesn't move the numbers.
    - Extraction itself needed a real fix for 1160/1250: a single PDF
      page can contain the TAIL of one table and the START of the next
      back to back (1160 page 3, 1250 page 4 both do this) - page-per-
      table can't be assumed. Rebuilt extraction to concatenate every
      page's spans into one continuous stream and split on the
      document's own vtab_ codes (a unique marker per table,
      independent of where page breaks fall) instead of splitting per
      page. Also, LTM 1250's counterweight column prints whole numbers
      without a decimal point ("5" / "0" instead of "5.0" / "0.0"),
      unlike every other crane so far - widened the weight-shape regex
      from \d+\.\d to \d+(\.\d)? to catch both, stored as a decimal
      internally either way for consistency.
  Separately, person's own idea: "since we know that each crane models
  hook block weight are from the minium block weight tab can we put a
  the recommend one." Added driveEqRecommendedHookBlock(craneKey,
  hookBlockRange) - cross-references the chart's hook block ALLOWANCE
  (e.g. "0 ... 900" kg) against FLEET_BLOCK_SPEC (the same data already
  driving the Minimum Hook Block Weights tab) and shows the heaviest
  fleet block whose own net weight fits inside that range, as a new
  "Recommended fleet block" row directly under Hook block. Picks
  heaviest-that-fits on the basis that a crew defaults to the biggest/
  most capable block still legal for the drive, not the lightest
  available; only compares each block's base net weight, not any
  auxiliary-weight variant, since aux weights are a lifting-capacity
  choice, unrelated to what's legal for road travel. Silently shows
  nothing when a crane has no FLEET_BLOCK_SPEC entry yet, the range
  doesn't parse, or nothing in the fleet fits - never a broken or
  misleading row. Verified: LTM 1110 front recommends the 100t 5-Sheave
  DM Block (900kg, sits right at the 0...900 boundary); LTM 1300 rear
  recommends the 125t 5-Sheave DM Block (1600kg, right at the
  500...1600 boundary); LTM 1130 (no Hook block column at all) shows no
  recommendation row, as expected.
  Regression-checked LTM 1110 and LTM 1300 after all changes - both
  still render identically to before.

10.45 Relabelled "Recommended fleet block" to "Maximum fleet block" -
      same computation, clearer meaning
  Person: "can you find which one would be the maximum based on weight
  so operators know if they have the smaller one on they weill be in
  the green." The underlying pick was already exactly this - heaviest
  fleet block that still fits inside the chart's hook block allowance -
  but "Recommended" reads as "go get this one," when the actually useful
  fact for a crew is a ceiling: whatever block is actually fitted, if
  it's this weight or lighter, they're compliant. Renamed the function
  (driveEqRecommendedHookBlock -> driveEqMaxCompliantHookBlock) and the
  displayed row label ("Maximum fleet block (lighter is fine)"), and
  added a line to the tab's disclaimer spelling out the ceiling framing
  explicitly, since a mislabelled safety-relevant figure is worse than
  no figure at all. No change to the selection logic itself.

10.46 Prepped for LTM 1650's 3-band hook block data - no new selector
      needed after all
  1650's chart has three separate hook block bands (0 / 700-3500 /
  3500-4600 kg), each with its own weight-vs-angle sub-table - flagged
  in 10.-something as needing design input before building. Walked
  through options with the person; landed on something much simpler
  than a new forced-choice selector: "i just want to know what the
  maxium size block ... can be used with that counterweight -boom
  direction etc ... if it says zero allowed then just say No hookblock."
  Once band tables are extracted, each band's rows just become more
  entries in the SAME flat rows array for that weight - no different
  from how boom-angle variants already stack as Option A/B/C. The only
  code change needed: when a row's hookBlock is the bare string "0" (no
  range), the "Hook block" line now reads "No hook block" instead of
  "0 kg", and driveEqMaxCompliantHookBlock is skipped for it explicitly
  (its own regex wouldn't have matched "0" anyway, but this makes the
  intent explicit rather than relying on that as an accident). Confirmed
  with the person directly this also satisfies "only offer the hook
  block recommendation if it is permissible" - since the fleet-block
  lookup already only ever runs against each row's own range, a
  narrower/heavier band's row can never inherit a recommendation from a
  lighter band's row it's stacked next to.
  Regression-checked all five live cranes (1110/1130/1160/1250/1300) -
  no visible change, since none of them currently have a bare "0"
  hookBlock value; this is purely prep for 1650's actual data, which is
  still on hold while the person checks whether more pages exist beyond
  the 8-page/477-page excerpt already reviewed.

10.47 Driving with Equipment in Place - added LTM 1650, closing out this
      tab's initial crane-by-crane rollout
  Person re-checked and supplied a fuller 14-page PDF (same doc version,
  different upload) covering all 8 sections this crane's charts are split
  into: sections 1.1-1.4 (T3, without winch 2 and winch 3) and 2.1-2.4
  (T3, with winch 2, without winch 3), each split further into T-16.7 vs
  T-29.0 boom length and front vs rear boom direction - all at the single
  8.4m outer radius this document provides. This document turned out to
  use the same character-shift cipher as LTM 1110/1300 (raw+29, literal
  space passed through, degree symbol special-cased) rather than the
  plain text of the original 8-page excerpt - confirms the earlier
  finding that this varies per export, not per crane or per document
  family, so every new PDF still needs its own check rather than
  assuming.
  Modelled the two independent axes (boom length, winch rigging) as a
  single flat list of 4 boomConfigs entries rather than inventing a
  second axis of UI - exactly the flexibility this mechanism was already
  proven to have (1160/1250 used it for outer radius, 1300 for boom

  700-3500 / 3500-4600 kg) confirmed uniform across every section of this
  document, so the bare-"0" "No hook block" display and the max-
  compliant-fleet-block cross-reference built in 10.45/10.46 apply
  unchanged with zero new code - only the data changed.
  One wiring wrinkle: FLEET_BLOCK_SPEC keys this crane's hook block fleet
  data by boom variant ('1650-t3'/'1650-t5', from the counterweight tab's
  earlier build) rather than by plain '1650', but Driving w/ Equipment's
  boomConfigs axis here is boom-length-and-winch, not t3/t5 - the two
  don't line up. Rather than splitting DRIVING_EQ_DATA to match, aliased
  the lookup ('1650' -> '1650-t3') inside driveEqMaxCompliantHookBlock,
  since both fleet-spec variants share identical base block weights for
  every block they have in common and t3's list is the superset.
  Multi-page extraction reused the vtab_-code-splitting approach from
  10.44 (sections 1.2/2.2 span 3 pages each, 1.3/2.3 span 2 pages each) -
  no new extraction technique needed, just re-verified page-to-vtab
  mapping against the section headers before trusting row order.
  Verified in-browser (Playwright): all 4 boom configs selectable, front/
  rear cross-availability, Option A-F stacking where multiple rows share
  a weight across different hook block bands, "No hook block" display,
  and the fleet-block ceiling recommendation surfacing correctly for both
  the 700-3500 and 3500-4600 kg bands. Regression-checked all five prior
  cranes (1110/1130/1160/1250/1300) with no console errors and no visible
  change.
  Known gaps, called out to the person rather than silently omitted: this
  document only covers the single 8.4m outer radius and the two winch
  states shown above - no other outer radius and no "with winch 2 and
  winch 3" section are in this upload, so those remain unavailable until
  supplied. Person confirmed 8.4m is near enough the fleet standard for
  this crane either way.

10.49 Made Reeving Plans the default tab on load
  Person asked for Reeving Plans (not Rope Retentioning) to be the tab
  open on first load. Moved the "active" class from the Rope Retentioning
  button/tab-content pair to the Reeving Plans pair. Caught one wiring gap
  in testing: unlike calcCAD()/initCwtTab()/initDriveEqTab(), which run
  unconditionally in the page's bottom-of-script init block regardless of
  which tab is active, initReeveTab() (populates the crane/config selects
  and loads the reeving diagram manifest) was ONLY ever called from
  inside switchTab() when navigating TO reeve-tab by click - it had never
  needed to run on page load before, because Reeving Plans was never the
  default tab before. Added an explicit initReeveTab() call to the
  init block so the tab isn't blank on first load now. Verified in-browser:
  Reeving Plans opens by default with crane/config selects and the first
  reeving diagram already populated, no console errors.

10.51 Added an "Advanced" disclosure to the Boom Configuration picker
  Person's ask: any boom config that isn't the plain/baseline chart for a
  crane (extended section, extra winch fitted, whatever the crane's own
  sections are) should be tucked away from the main toggle instead of
  sitting alongside it as an equal choice. Person flagged they'll be
  adding more of these to most other cranes going forward, so the split
  needed to be data-driven rather than inferred from label text - LTM
  1300's T-19.6 signals "not baseline" via a 0/0/46/0/0/0 percentage
  pattern, but LTM 1650's "with winch 2" configs have no percentage
  notation at all, so a regex on the label wouldn't generalize.
  Introduced a plain `advanced: true` flag on the boomConfig object
  itself (schema comment updated above DRIVING_EQ_DATA) - renamed from
  the unused `fullyRetracted: false` that was already sitting on LTM
  1300's T-19.6 entry (added in 10.42, never wired to anything). Flagged
  LTM 1300's T-19.6 and both of LTM 1650's "with winch 2" configs.
  onDriveEqCraneChange() now splits a crane's boomConfigs into the
  existing #de-boom-config-toggle (unflagged) and a new collapsed
  <details> disclosure, #de-boom-config-advanced, holding a second
  mode-toggle row for flagged configs - only shown at all when a crane
  actually has an advanced entry. Both toggles tag each button with its
  original array index (data-idx) so setDriveEqBoomConfig() can drive the
  active state across both containers without assuming position. Verified
  in-browser (Playwright): LTM 1300 shows only T-14.7 in the main toggle
  with T-19.6 under Advanced; LTM 1650 shows both no-winch configs main
  and both with-winch configs under Advanced; selecting an Advanced entry
  flows through direction/weight/output exactly like a main entry; single-
  config cranes (e.g. LTM 1110) still hide the whole Boom Configuration
  group as before; no console errors.

  10.51 flagged LTM 1650's two "with winch 2" configs as advanced and

10.53 Corrected LTM 1110's Winch 2*/Replacement Ballast slotWarning wording
  The existing slotWarning text (10.19-era) called the two "NOT
  interchangeable" and framed the difference as winch capability (actual
  winch fitted vs dead weight). Person corrected this: they ARE
  interchangeable - only one is ever fitted in the shared slot, that part
  was always true - the real, OEM-confirmed distinction (per the
  countsToward comment above the components array, 10.7) is that they
  affect the combo's TOTAL COUNTERWEIGHT differently despite matching
  0.8t weight: Replacement Ballast's 0.8t counts toward the total,
  Winch 2*'s does not. Rewrote both slotWarning strings (p8w, p8b) to
  lead with "are interchangeable" and state the counts-toward-total
  difference explicitly instead of the capability framing. Also fixed
  the summary comment above the notice-building code (previously said
  "NOT functionally equivalent... one gives actual winch capability") to
  match. Verified in-browser: selecting a combo with Winch 2* shows the
  corrected notice, selecting one with Replacement Ballast shows its
  counterpart, no console errors.

10.54 Smoothed the 10.53 slotWarning phrasing
  Person flagged "its 0.8t is NOT counted toward this combo's total

  combo actually has fitted and state the effect plainly against the
  on-screen "Total Counterweight" label (the #cwt-status metric card)
  instead of the vaguer "combo's total counterweight": e.g. "This combo
  has Winch 2* fitted, so its 0.8t does not count toward the Total
  Counterweight figure — Replacement Ballast's 0.8t would." Wording only,

10.55 Outrigger Support Positioning: MUST MOVE now a real collision check,
      not a left/right heuristic
  Previous logic (requiredIds) hardcoded P1/P2 as MUST MOVE for any
  rightward shift and P3/P4 for any leftward shift, regardless of shift
  magnitude, direction Y-component, or actual pad geometry - the person
  said at the time this was just an assumption, not something checked.
  Person asked directly: would each pad actually collide with the crane's
  new chassis position if left exactly where it is?
  The math already existed for the other half of this - clashesWithFootprint()
  and, for every pad, its "if left in place" position relative to the
  crane's NEW center (relX, relY, used for the "If Left in Place" table
  column). Replaced the heuristic with `clashesWithFootprint(relX, relY, fp)`
  per pad: a pad is MUST MOVE only if its own current spot genuinely falls
  under the chassis footprint once this shift is complete - checked
  individually per pad/crane/shift, not by which side of the crane it's on.
  Updated the "MUST MOVE" disclaimer to describe the real rule instead of
  "direct path of travel."
  Consequences worth knowing: a pure fore/aft shift can still require no
  pad to move (X offset unaffected, and every pad's baseline X sits well
  outside the footprint half-width for every crane in FOOTPRINTS) but a
  diagonal shift can now pull in a front or rear pad that the old rule
  never flagged. Also confirmed a genuine "sweep window": a pad only
  clashes within a specific shift-magnitude range in the matching
  direction - e.g. LTM 1650's P2 is MUST MOVE at shiftX=+4m but clears
  again by shiftX=+8m, since by then the crane has already moved past
  that pad's original spot entirely. This is expected box-overlap
  behavior, not a bug.
  Verified in-browser (Playwright) across LTM 1100 and LTM 1650: pure
  lateral shifts reproduce sensible required/optional splits, pure
  fore/aft shifts require nothing at small magnitudes, a diagonal shift
  correctly flags a non-lateral pad, and the shift-magnitude sweep window
  behaves as expected in both directions on LTM 1650. No console errors.

================================================================================

10.56 Outrigger Support Positioning: added an opt-in old/new position
      compare view
  Person's idea: show the new (shifted) crane center in blue to match its
  existing target-dot color, and the old center in yellow to match the
  existing front-direction arrow's color, with a toggle to turn the
  comparison on/off rather than always showing both. Off by default -
  confirmed with the person first, since the canvas already reads fine
  with just the single grey footprint + blue new-center dot, and always-on
  would add clutter nobody asked for.
  Added a checkbox ("Compare old/new position") in the Visual Site Plan
  card-title, mirroring the existing Rope Retentioning tab's inline
  checkbox pattern (label wrapping input, card-title's flex layout
  right-aligns it for free). New `compareMode` module-level var, flipped
  by toggleCompareMode(), which just re-runs calcCAD() to redraw - no
  separate redraw path needed.
  Refactored the single hardcoded footprint-rectangle draw into
  drawFootprintBox(offsetX, offsetY, strokeColor, fillColor) so the same
  code draws both the old footprint (offset 0,0, unchanged grey, always
  drawn) and, only when compareMode is on AND the shift is nonzero, a
  second footprint outlined in blue at the shifted offset, plus a small
  yellow dot at the old center. Gated on "shift nonzero" specifically
  because at zero shift the old and new positions are identical - drawing
  both would just be two overlapping outlines for no information.
  Nothing about the underlying leg math, MUST MOVE determination (10.55),
  or default (untoggled) rendering changed - purely an additive, opt-in
  overlay. Verified in-browser (Playwright, LTM 1650, 3m lateral shift):
  toggle off matches the pre-existing look exactly; toggle on shows the
  second blue footprint outline and a visible yellow dot at the old
  center once zoomed in; no console errors.

10.57 CWT Combinations: added a 3D model view for LTM 1130, alongside the
      existing 2D diagram
  Person supplied a person-made GLB/GLTF export (Onshape CAD, same source
  as the 2D exploded diagrams) of LTM 1130's counterweight stack and asked
  whether a real 3D viewer was feasible. Proved it first with a standalone
  proof-of-concept before touching the app - loaded the GLB with Three.js,
  confirmed each physical plate is its own named mesh node ("Part_N"), and
  confirmed click-to-select via raycasting works against the real geometry,
  not just a list. Person then asked to wire it to actual combo data and
  ship it to both beta and live for hands-on testing.
  New dependency: this is the app's first external JS library. Vendored
  (not CDN - the app has to keep working offline) under vendor/three/:
  three.module.min.js + three.core.min.js (its internal split-out chunk),
  GLTFLoader.js + OrbitControls.js (three's examples/jsm addons, patched to
  import three.module.min.js and each other via relative/importmap paths
  instead of a bundler), and utils/BufferGeometryUtils.js +
  utils/SkeletonUtils.js (GLTFLoader's own internal deps - the addon
  doesn't work without them, easy to miss since they're not imported from
  the entry point directly). ~970KB vendored total, cached once regardless
  of how many cranes eventually get a model - and not fetched AT ALL until
  needed: rather than a static <script type="module"> tag (which would
  fetch+parse it on every single page load, including for people who never
  touch CWT Combinations), setCwtView('3d') dynamically import()s
  vendor/cwt3d.js on first switch to the 3D view. The page-level importmap
  (mapping the bare "three" specifier to vendor/three/three.module.min.js)
  still has to be a static tag - browsers only honor importmaps present
  before the first module load - but that's inline JSON, no fetch of its
  own. The browser caches the imported module by URL, so toggling 3D off
  and back on, or switching crane, resolves instantly after the first
  fetch rather than re-importing. New vendor/cwt3d.js is the viewer
  itself; being an ES module, it cannot see index.html's classic-script
  top-level const/let bindings, so it talks to the rest of the app purely
  through window: reads window.COUNTERWEIGHT_DATA (now explicitly exported
  there) and calls window.toggleCwtPlate(id) on a pick, exactly the same
  function the 2D hotspots call, so 2D and 3D drive one shared selection
  state with no parallel logic to keep in sync. renderCwtTab() calls
  window.__cwt3dOnRender(...) on every render (cheap no-op unless the 3D
  view is actually visible and already loaded); setCwtView('3d') calls
  window.__cwt3dActivate(...) to load-or-reuse the model.
  Part-to-component mapping: the GLB carries only Onshape's arbitrary
  "Part N" numbers, no weight/name metadata, so which mesh is which of the
  app's p1-p8 isn't in the file - inferred from geometry instead (see the
  long comment on '1130'.model3d in index.html for the full derivation):
  Part_10/Part_3 are an exact mirror pair -> p8's two physical halves;
  Part_1 has a distinctly different shape/position from the rest -> p7
  (a winch mechanism, not a flat plate); the remaining six share one exact
  footprint and stack contiguously, matched to p1-p6 by thickness-vs-known-
  weight ratio (consistently ~13-19 across all six, same footprint+
  material assumption). Flagged to the person as inferred, not confirmed -
  a wrong pairing only mislabels which plate highlights on click, it does
  NOT touch cwtComboWeight/cwtFindMatchingCombo or any total/approved-combo
  logic, which stay driven entirely by the existing components list.
  Also added: an explode offset (each named part nudged outward from the
  model's center on load, proportional to overall model size) since the
  GLB stores the assembled position, not the pulled-apart view the 2D
  diagrams show; a lazy load (the ~4.2MB GLB is fetched only on first
  switch to 3D view, never on page load, matching the reeving-diagram
  fetch-on-demand pattern rather than the always-cached app shell); and a
  "2D Diagram / 3D Model" toggle on the Counterweight Stack card, hidden
  entirely for every crane without a model3d entry (currently every crane
  but 1130).
  Verified in-browser (Playwright): toggle only appears for LTM 1130;
  switching to 3D loads and renders the model; clicking a plate directly
  on the model selects it, and that selection is immediately reflected in
  the Composition panel, Total Counterweight, AND the 2D hotspot view when
  switching back - full round-trip, not view-local state; switching away
  to a crane with no model hides the toggle and falls back to 2D with no
  crash; switching crane always resets to 2D view. No console errors.
  Regression-checked all seven cranes' 2D hotspot rendering afterward -
  unchanged.

10.58 Fixed 3D explode direction - was pushing parts sideways, not along
      their real stacking axis
  Person tested and reported the plates didn't line up, despite matching
  the 2D diagram cleanly in Onshape (the same file the 2D diagram was
  itself extracted from) - so the source geometry wasn't in question, the
  bug had to be in explodeParts() (10.57). Confirmed directly against the
  raw GLTF JSON rather than guessing: every "occurrence of Part N" parent
  node carries a baked ROTATION as part of its transform matrix (the same
  rotation on every one, just different translation) - not a pure
  translation like the explode code assumed. explodeParts() computed the
  "push outward" direction correctly in world space, but added it straight
  to `group.position`, which Three.js interprets in the PARENT's local
  frame - a frame rotated ~90 degrees from world space here. That mismatch
  is exactly what pushed parts sideways instead of along their real
  stacking axis.
  Fix: convert the target position to world space first (current world
  position + offset), then convert THAT into the parent's local space via
  `group.parent.worldToLocal(...)` before assigning it to group.position -
  correct regardless of whatever rotation/scale sits between the node and
  world space, rather than assuming there isn't any. Also added an
  explicit `root.updateMatrixWorld(true)` before any of this, since the
  model isn't in the scene graph yet at explode time (that happens after,
  in __cwt3dActivate) and matrixWorld is otherwise only refreshed on
  render.
  Verified in-browser (Playwright, LTM 1130): the six stacked plates now
  separate cleanly along one consistent vertical axis instead of drifting
  sideways/diagonally: screenshotted from two angles to confirm. Re-ran
  the full click round-trip (3D pick -> Composition panel -> Total
  Counterweight -> 2D hotspot) after the fix - still correct. No console
  errors.

================================================================================

10.59 Real evenly-spaced explode gap (120mm) instead of a uniform outward
      push
  Person asked two things: whether to rename Onshape parts to match the
  app's own component ids for future models (yes - would replace the
  10.57 geometry-inference mapping with a direct, certain one), and
  whether to explode/space the plates in Onshape itself, with what gap.
  Answered: don't bake spacing into the CAD export - keep it in real
  assembled position, since spacing is trivial to retune here without a
  re-export, and each new crane is one less step for the person that way.
  That answer meant actually fixing the explode to be a real gap rather
  than what 10.57/10.58 shipped: pushDistance = maxDim * 0.18 applied
  uniformly to every part's centroid regardless of position - for LTM
  1130 that's ~1058mm, a fixed displacement per part, not a controlled
  gap between adjacent faces (confirmed by direct measurement before
  changing anything, not assumed).
  Reworked explodeParts(): parts are now clustered by XZ proximity
  (50mm tolerance - this model's actual stacked-plate matches are exact
  to several decimal places, so real stacked plates cluster trivially;
  non-stacked parts like the winch piece or the auxiliary ballast pair
  don't share XZ with anything and land in their own single-member
  cluster). A multi-member cluster (a real stack) is spaced by the new
  explodeStack(): find whichever axis (x/y/z) has the most spread between
  the cluster's centers - Y here, but not assumed, so a differently-
  oriented future export still stacks correctly - sort along it, then walk
  bottom to top accumulating exactly STACK_GAP (0.12 = 120mm) between each
  part's own real thickness, so the gap is the same physical distance
  everywhere regardless of that plate's own thickness. Single-member
  clusters keep the old proportional outward push (pushOutward()) since
  there's nothing to space them evenly against. Both paths still go
  through the 10.58 world-to-local fix (factored out to applyWorldOffset())
  rather than duplicating that logic.
  STACK_GAP is the one number to change if 120mm ever looks wrong - a
  one-line edit, no new model export needed.
  Verified in-browser (Playwright, LTM 1130, both default and rotated
  views): all six plates now show a clean, visibly even gap between them
  rather than the previous uneven/bunched push; winch piece and both
  ballast halves still separated clearly; click-to-select and the full
  Composition/Total/2D-hotspot sync re-verified working after the rework.
  No console errors.

  UPDATE (superseded as the default, not wrong at the time): this was
  the right call for the cranes shipped up to this point, but starting
  at 10.71 the person's own workflow shifted to spacing the stack by
  hand in Onshape and shipping preExploded: true instead - not a
  reversal of this reasoning, a different tradeoff that won out once
  composite-part swaps (10.75) and multi-crane batches made "exact
  control over the file, no heuristics to second-guess" worth more
  than "spacing is a one-line edit here." STACK_GAP/explodeParts()
  stay in cwt3d.js, real working infrastructure for the case, not
  dead code - just not the current default for a NEW crane. See
  section 11 for the procedure as it actually runs today.

================================================================================

10.60 Consolidated "Cache All Offline Content" - one shared button instead
      of reeving-only
  Person's reasoning: with 3D models joining reeving diagrams as content
  that's only cached once actually viewed, the existing reeve-only
  "Download All Diagrams" button (10.something earlier) would leave 3D
  models stranded the same way an unviewed reeving diagram used to be -
  unavailable if a crew member needs a crane they've never opened before,
  offline, on-site. Agreed a single consolidated button was better than a
  second parallel one; person specified exact placement (below Reference
  Documentation, on every tab that has one) and confirmed the title
  ("Cache All Offline Content").
  Removed the old reeve-tab-only "Offline Availability" card (sat above
  Reference Documentation there) and added one shared version, styled
  identically, below Reference Documentation on all three tabs that have
  one: Reeving Plans, CWT Combinations, Driving with Equipment in Place.
  Clicking it from ANY of the three runs the same complete download,
  everywhere - not a tab-scoped subset - since the point is comprehensive
  offline readiness regardless of which tab happened to be open. The three
  DOM copies share one cache-all-btn/-progress-wrap/-progress-bar/-
  progress-text class set (no ids, since there are three of each now) and
  cacheAllOfflineContent() updates all three via querySelectorAll in
  lockstep.
  Renamed cacheAllReeveDiagrams() -> cacheAllOfflineContent() and widened
  its file list (new allOfflineContentUrls()) to cover every reeving SVG
  (unchanged), the 3D viewer support files (vendor/cwt3d.js + all of
  vendor/three/*, only included at all if at least one crane has a
  model3d), and every crane's model3d.url. Counterweight 2D diagram images
  are deliberately NOT in this list - those are already precached in the
  app shell (sw.js APP_SHELL, guaranteed available from install), so
  there's nothing this button needs to chase down for them.
  "Only downloads what it doesn't already have, unless it's a new
  revision" - person's exact requirement - falls out of the existing
  architecture rather than needing new logic: a plain fetch() per URL is
  enough, since the service worker's own fetch handler is cache-first, so
  an already-cached URL resolves instantly with zero network request.
  "New revision" is handled per-file by that file's own URL, not by this
  function: vendor/cwt3d.js and now every model3d.url (previously
  unversioned - added ?v=1 to LTM 1130's GLB here, matching the ?v=
  convention already on cwt3d.js, since the person may well replace this
  exact model once its parts are renamed per their own question this
  session) carry an explicit version query - bump it and the URL changes,
  which is a cache miss, which forces a real fetch, with no "is this
  stale" check needed anywhere. Added CWT3D_VERSION as the one shared
  source of truth for cwt3d.js's version number, referenced by both
  setCwtView()'s dynamic import and cacheAllOfflineContent(), so the two
  can't drift and end up caching different versions of the same file.
  Verified in-browser (Playwright): the button/progress elements appear on
  all three tabs (three .cache-all-btn found); triggering it from Reeving
  Plans downloads all 169 files (reeving diagrams + 3D viewer + the one
  GLB) successfully with zero failures; a second run immediately after
  completes 5.5x faster (0.28s vs 1.53s) purely from the service worker's
  cache-first short-circuit, confirming already-cached files aren't
  re-fetched. No console errors.

10.61 Added a tick/cross to state whether offline content is actually
      fully downloaded
  Person asked for a status indicator alongside 10.60's button rather
  than just a fire-and-forget download action. New checkOfflineContentStatus()
  reads the REAL current state from the Cache Storage API - the same
  allOfflineContentUrls() list from 10.60 diffed against whatever's
  actually sitting in the content cache - rather than just remembering
  whether cacheAllOfflineContent() last ran successfully, since content
  also gets cached one file at a time simply by viewing it (a reeving
  diagram, a 3D model), exactly as it always has; the status needs to
  reflect that too, not only a full-download run.
  Doesn't hardcode sw.js's CONTENT_CACHE name as a second literal that
  could drift from the one in sw.js - finds it at runtime as whichever
  cache bucket isn't one of the versioned app-shell-* ones, since sw.js
  only ever opens those two. Added a small span inside each "Offline
  Availability" card-title (card-title's existing flex/space-between
  layout right-aligns it for free, same trick as the CWT compare-view
  checkbox and the Auxiliary Ballast enable checkbox before it) - ✓
  Downloaded in var(--success) green, or ✗ Not fully downloaded (N
  missing) in var(--danger) red.
  Checked on three occasions: once on page load (covers whichever tab
  loads by default, main vs beta differ on that - see the comment left in
  place of assuming one), once on switchTab() for the three tabs, and once
  at the end of cacheAllOfflineContent() itself. That last one caught a
  real race first: the service worker's fetch handler writes each response
  into CONTENT_CACHE via a fire-and-forget cache.put() not awaited by the
  page's fetch() promise, so checking status the instant the download loop
  finishes could still show 1-2 "missing" for writes that hadn't landed
  yet. Fixed with one extra checkOfflineContentStatus() call 600ms later -
  not a poll/retry loop, this was never a real ongoing race, just a
  one-time confirmation that the last couple of writes had caught up.
  Verified in-browser (Playwright): shows the correct cross with an
  accurate missing-count before any download; download then immediately
  shows the correct tick (no lingering false negative after the 600ms
  fix); tick persists correctly across a full page reload (real Cache
  Storage state, not in-memory-only). No console errors.

================================================================================

10.62 Fixed checkOfflineContentStatus() showing the full original miss
      count even after a genuinely successful download
  Worked fine in every sandbox test (10.61), but person hit it on a real
  device: button correctly read "All Offline Content Cached", status span
  next to it still showed "Not fully downloaded (169 missing)" - the exact
  original count, as if the download had done nothing at all, despite it
  having actually worked (offline content was genuinely usable).
  Root cause was the status check's own approach, not the caching itself:
  it opened sw.js's CONTENT_CACHE bucket by GUESSING its name ("whichever
  cache isn't app-shell-*") rather than asking the browser directly.
  Getting the exact original count back means that guess resolved to no
  bucket at all on this device/browser (cacheNames.find() returning
  undefined), not a partial miss - so the check fell back to comparing
  against an empty list, reporting everything missing regardless of what
  was actually cached.
  Replaced the whole "find the bucket, open it, list its keys, compare
  URLs" approach with caches.match(url) per required URL - this searches
  every cache bucket for the origin without the caller needing to know or
  guess any bucket's name, which is also effectively what the service
  worker's own fetch handler relies on to decide something's already
  cached. Strictly more robust than the bucket-name guess it replaces, not
  just a fix for this one device.
  Verified in-browser (Playwright): correct cross with accurate count
  before download, correct tick immediately after, tick persists across a
  full reload - same coverage as 10.61's original tests, now against the
  more robust lookup. No console errors.

================================================================================

10.63 CWT Combinations: added LTM 1300's 3D model - first crane with parts
      labelled by the person to match the app directly, not inferred
  Person's own labelling, matching this crane's real component ids
  (p1-p5, aux5, aux6) rather than Onshape's arbitrary numbering like LTM
  1130's model had - so this partMap is a direct, confirmed lookup, not
  10.57's geometry-based guess. Flagged two things about the labelling
  before supplying the file: wing weights 5/6 labelled "5L"/"5R" and
  "6L"/"6R" (left/right), and the receptacle (Plate 1) labelled "1" on
  every one of its several small CAD bodies.
  Neither turned out to be as simple as "just add more partMap entries" -
  both exposed real gaps in 10.57/10.58's matching logic, which had only
  ever seen LTM 1130's uniquely-named parts:
  - GLTFLoader auto-renames sibling nodes that share a name ("1" becomes
    "1", "1_1", "1_2", "1_3", "1_4" for the receptacle's 5 bodies; same
    for the crane's two identical, interchangeable Plate 3 copies, both
    labelled "3" -> "3"/"3_1"). The old matching code only ever checked
    the exact node name against partMap, so it would have silently found
    ONLY the first instance of each and missed the rest. New
    partMapKeyFor() tries the exact name first, and only falls back to
    stripping one trailing "_<digits>" and matching THAT base if the exact
    name isn't a key - so this never fires for a model with no name
    collisions (LTM 1130 still matches everything on the first try) and
    can't misfire on a genuine partMap key that happens to end in
    "_<digits>" (none do today, but nothing stops it).
  - The two Plate 3 copies need to be independently selectable as p3a/p3b
    (their real app ids) despite being unable to tell apart by name at
    all. partMap values can now be an array (`'3': ['p3a', 'p3b']`) -
    cwt3d.js sorts that name's matched instances along whichever axis has
    the most spread between them and assigns ids by position, rather than
    needing a real distinguishing name.
  - The receptacle's 5 bodies all map to the SAME single id (p1) - if
    exploded individually like 10.57/10.58 treated every part, they'd
    scatter as 5 separate fragments instead of moving as the one physical
    item they actually are. explodeParts() (and loadModel()) now work on
    "participants" rather than raw GLB nodes: a name that maps several GLB
    nodes to one single app id becomes ONE rigid block (combined bounding
    box, every member node moved by the identical world-space offset via
    10.58's applyWorldOffset - preserving their relative arrangement to
    each other); an array-mapped name, or a name with only one matching
    node, still explodes node-by-node exactly as before. Confirmed this
    was the right call before generalizing it, not assumed: an array
    mapping means "these need to look independently selectable" and a
    string mapping across multiple nodes means "this is one item made of
    several bodies" - the two cases actually do need different explode
    behavior, not just different appId assignment.
  New file counterweight/models/ltm1300-cwt.glb (?v=1, same cache-busting
  convention as LTM 1130's, methodology.txt 10.60).
  Verified in-browser (Playwright): all 8 of LTM 1300's component ids
  (p1, p2, p3a, p3b, p4, p5, aux5, aux6) individually select, highlight,
  and show the correct weight in the Composition panel; screenshotted p1
  selected from two angles - the receptacle's 5 bodies light up as one
  contiguous shape, not scattered fragments; screenshotted p3a+p3b
  selected together - the two Plate 3 copies show clearly separated in
  the stack and both highlight, "Selected: 2 part(s)"; re-ran all 8 of LTM
  1130's own component ids afterward to confirm the refactor didn't
  regress it - identical results to before; full crane sweep confirms the
  3D toggle appears only for LTM 1130 and LTM 1300, 2D hotspot rendering
  unchanged on all seven cranes. No console errors or partMap
  count-mismatch warnings anywhere in testing.

10.64 Redid LTM 1130's 3D model with direct labels; fixed a real explode
      bug it exposed (auxiliary ballast pair glued together)
  Person redid the original LTM 1130 GLB (10.57's, mapped by inferring
  geometry from Onshape's arbitrary "Part N" numbers) with parts labelled
  to match the app's own component names directly, same approach as LTM
  1300 (10.63) - explicitly kept as the proof of concept either way, not
  requested because the original was wrong so much as confirmed-vs-
  inferred being worth having. Confirmed the post-sanitizing node names
  by actually loading the file rather than guessing: GLTFLoader replaces
  spaces with underscores and drops "/" outright (not replaced), so
  "Replacement Weight 7 / Winch 2*" becomes "Replacement_Weight_7__Winch_2*"
  (double underscore - both spaces around the dropped slash survived).
  Person flagged the replacement-weight/winch part is labelled identically
  on both its physical halves ("labelled it twice as it was two parts") -
  same shared-name pattern as LTM 1300's receptacle, so partMapKeyFor()
  and the rigid-block explode logic (10.63) already handled it with zero
  new code.
  What WASN'T already handled: the auxiliary ballast pair (p8) is ALSO
  labelled identically on both halves, but unlike the receptacle or the
  winch part, its two halves are mounted on genuinely opposite sides of
  the crane. 10.63's rigid-block logic doesn't distinguish "one item
  split across several CAD bodies" from "one both-or-neither id mounted
  in separate locations" - it glued the ballast pair into one block too,
  combining two boxes from opposite sides of the crane into a bounding
  box centered right back near the model's own middle, so pushOutward
  barely moved it and it visually overlapped the main stack instead of
  separating - caught by looking at the actual render, not assumed to be
  fine because the underlying selection/weight logic was already correct
  (it was - only the explode positioning was wrong).
  A pure geometry-based distinguishing rule doesn't exist here: the
  receptacle's 5 bodies span ~2.4m from each other (must glue) while the
  ballast pair's 2 bodies span ~3.7m (must NOT glue) - too close in
  magnitude to threshold on distance alone. Used a signal that already
  existed in the app's own component data instead of inventing a new CAD-
  labelling convention or a fragile heuristic: `separate: true`, set on
  exactly this kind of both-or-neither multi-location component already
  (originally added for the 2D hotspot system, well before this 3D viewer
  existed - LTM 1130's p8 and LTM 1300's aux5/aux6 all already carry it).
  explodeParts()/loadModel() now take a separateIds set (built from
  `data.components.filter(c => c.separate)`) - a shared-name key only
  becomes a rigid block if its target id is NOT in that set; a separate-
  flagged id keeps exploding each of its instances independently instead,
  same as before 10.63 ever existed for it specifically.
  Verified in-browser (Playwright): before the separate-flag fix, p8
  visibly overlapped the main stack in a screenshot; after, both halves
  clearly separate from the stack and from each other. Re-ran all 8 of
  LTM 1130's own ids (correct weights, correct highlighting) and all 8 of
  LTM 1300's (unaffected by the fix - p1's receptacle still glues, since
  p1 was never separate-flagged; aux5/aux6 were already independent
  before this change since 1300's L/R halves have distinct names, not a
  shared one, so groupsByKey never had >1 node under one key for them in
  the first place). Full crane sweep clean, no console errors.

================================================================================

10.65 Fixed exploded-stack ordering for rigid blocks whose combined bbox is
      tall enough to overlap its neighbors' Y-range; added extra L/R gap
      for genuinely paired (separate: true) items
  Reported on LTM 1300: Receptacle Plate 1 (p1) rendered in the MIDDLE of
  the exploded stack instead of at the very bottom, even though every
  other plate sits on top of it. Same bug then reported on LTM 1130's
  Plate 1. Confirmed the cause with real geometry before touching any
  code (methodology-standard practice all session) by loading each GLB
  headless and dumping explodeParts()'s own groupsByKey combined
  center/size per key: LTM 1300's receptacle (5-body rigid block, 10.63)
  has combined size.y = 0.815 - tall enough that its bounding box spans
  almost the same Y-range as plates 2, 3 and 4 above it. explodeStack()
  sorted by bbox CENTER, and the receptacle's center (y=1.475) landed
  between plate 2's (y=1.205) and plate 3's (y=1.5225) - so plate 2 got
  placed underneath it instead of the reverse. Both plate 1 and plate 2
  actually start at the exact same bottom edge (y=1.0675) - the
  receptacle isn't a flat slab sitting below plate 2, its 5 bodies
  include structural bits that reach up alongside the whole lower stack,
  which is exactly why sorting by center (the "average" height) rather
  than base position was wrong.
  Fix: explodeStack() now sorts by BOTTOM edge (center - size/2 on the
  stack's dominant axis) instead of center. Bottom-edge ties (both start
  at the stack's true floor) are broken by size, larger first - the block
  whose base reaches this low while also being the tallest is the one
  everything above is actually resting on. For a normal single-node plate
  stack (no overlapping bboxes) bottom-edge order and center order are
  identical, so this doesn't touch already-correct cranes.
  Verified against real geometry for both cranes before shipping (not
  just eyeballed after): re-ran the same headless dump with the new sort
  key - LTM 1300 order came out 1,2,3,3,4,5 (was 2,1,3,4,5); LTM 1130
  came out 1,2,3,4,5,6 (was already ambiguous/wrong in the same way).
  Confirmed visually in-browser after: LTM 1300's receptacle and LTM
  1130's Plate 1 both now sit clearly at the bottom of their stacks.
  Second, unrelated request same session: LTM 1300's auxiliary ballast
  pairs (aux5 = 5L/5R, aux6 = 6L/6R) needed more visual separation
  between the left half and the right half specifically, not just more
  separation from the main stack. These are lone (non-stacked)
  participants pushed outward from the model's center by a single shared
  pushDistance (10.63/10.64) - tuned for "clear the main stack," not for
  "read as obviously split from its own other half." Added a second,
  larger pushDistance (1.6x) that only applies to participants whose
  mapped app id is itself separate: true - i.e. exactly the both-or-
  neither multi-location components (aux5/aux6 here; LTM 1130's p8 gets
  the same treatment for free, consistent with 10.64's reasoning, since
  nothing here is crane-specific). A lone part that ISN'T flagged
  separate (e.g. LTM 1130's winch/replacement-weight) keeps the original
  distance - this only widens the gap for genuinely paired items.
  Verified in-browser: LTM 1300's aux5 and aux6 pairs now sit clearly
  apart from each other as well as from the main stack. Full crane sweep
  re-run after both changes, clean, no console errors.

================================================================================

10.66 LTM 1130's winch/replacement-weight (p7) hidden behind the exploded
      stack - moved it to sit on top instead, generically
  Reported: p7 (mounted on top of the main plate stack in real life) was
  hard to see in the 3D view, hidden behind the stack. Cause: p7 is a
  lone (non-stacked) participant, so it got the same radial outward push
  as any other lone part (10.63) - fine for something genuinely mounted
  to the SIDE, wrong for something mounted on TOP, since its own XZ
  position sits slightly off-center from the stack's footprint and the
  push exaggerated that sideways offset instead of correcting for it.
  Fix, generic rather than 1130-specific: components can now carry
  `explodeTop: true` (same reused-signal pattern as `separate`, 10.64/
  10.65 - a flag on the app's own component data, not a geometry guess or
  a hardcoded id check). explodeParts() pulls any such participant out of
  the normal cluster/push pipeline entirely and, once the real plate
  stack has been exploded, places it via a new placeAtStackTop(): directly
  above the tallest exploded cluster (found generically as whichever
  cluster ended up with the most members, not assumed to be "the" stack
  by id), centered over that cluster's XZ centroid rather than the part's
  own original XZ, with the same STACK_GAP clearance a real stacked plate
  gets. Generic over whichever axis the stack is actually stacked on,
  matching explodeStack()'s own axis detection.
  Set explodeTop: true on LTM 1130's p7 only (the one component this
  applies to today). Verified in-browser: p7 now renders clearly on top
  of the stack, centered, from multiple rotation angles - previously
  tucked behind/beside it. Re-ran all 8 of LTM 1130's own ids (correct
  weights, correct highlighting, no console errors) to confirm restructuring
  explodeParts() around the new stackTopParticipants split didn't disturb
  the existing rigid-block/pair/plain-stack participants. Full crane
  sweep clean.

================================================================================

10.67 Added an opt-out for the 3D viewer's automatic explode entirely
      (model3d.preExploded)
  After three rounds of explode-positioning bugs across the two cranes
  with 3D models so far (10.63-10.66 - a rigid block's combined bbox
  landing it mid-stack, paired items needing extra mutual clearance, a
  lone part needing to anchor above the stack instead of push outward),
  the person offered to just position parts already exploded in Onshape
  before exporting, rather than relying on cwt3d.js's own heuristics and
  catching each new edge case after the fact. Better trade for future
  cranes: the automatic explode is a genuinely good default (it's
  handled every case seen so far, generically, once each was found), but
  it's still an INFERENCE from raw assembled-position geometry, and every
  crane's export is a new chance for a geometry shape the existing rules
  don't anticipate - whereas the person doing the positioning themselves
  in the same tool that produces the 2D diagrams they already hand-place
  is the actual source of truth, not a guess.
  Added `model3d.preExploded: true` as a per-crane opt-in - when set,
  loadModel() skips the explodeParts() call entirely and uses the GLB's
  own positions as-is. Not the default (nothing existing sets it - LTM
  1130 and LTM 1300 both keep using the automatic explode, unaffected),
  so this is purely additive. Full crane sweep re-run after adding the
  flag, clean, no console errors - confirms the untouched code path
  (explodeParts still runs for both existing cranes) wasn't disturbed by
  adding the early-exit check.

================================================================================

10.68 CWT Combinations: added LTM 1110's 3D model - first crane to use
      preExploded (10.67), first real test of it
  Person's own Onshape export, already positioned in their intended
  exploded layout rather than assembled position - set preExploded: true
  and mapped partMap straight to the CAD's own node names, no explode
  heuristics involved at all for this crane.
  Receptacle Plate 1 is 3 separate CAD bodies (Receptacle_Plate_1left/
  base/right), same multi-body-glued-into-one-id pattern as LTM 1300's
  receptacle (10.63) - all 3 map to p1.
  One naming ambiguity caught before mapping, not guessed past: the file
  has TWO nodes both literally named "Counterweight_Plate_7" - one large
  (900 mesh children, ~2.3m, matches p7's real dimensions/2.5t weight)
  and one tiny/thin (135 mesh children, ~0.8m x 0.08m x 0.03m, ~400x
  smaller by volume). Given this crane's own CAD has a documented history
  of a real plate 6/7 mix-up (10.7), asked rather than assumed which was
  which - confirmed by the person: the large one (GLTFLoader auto-renames
  it to "..._7_1" on load, since it's the second node sharing that exact
  name) is the real plate 7; the tiny one is just the Liebherr brand
  label decal baked onto the back of the slab as its own body, not a
  component. partMap deliberately maps only "Counterweight_Plate_7_1" (the
  real plate) to p7 and leaves the tiny decal node out of partMap
  entirely, same as any other unmapped part - shown but not clickable.
  Also confirmed absent from the file entirely: the Winch 2*/Replacement
  Ballast slot (p8w/p8b, 10.7's mutually-exclusive slotGroup pair) - no
  geometry anywhere near that size/location, so those two ids simply have
  no 3D representation. Not a bug - the 2D diagram still covers them
  fully, and namedParts naturally has no entries for an id with no
  matching GLB node (nothing to highlight, nothing to break).
  Verified in-browser: 3D toggle appears for 1110, all 7 modeled ids
  (p1-p7) resolve to their correct real weights and highlight the
  correct mesh (p1 = bottom receptacle, p7 = top plate, matching the
  person's own exploded layout) with no explode logic run at all. Full
  crane sweep across all 7 cranes clean, no console errors - confirms
  adding a third model3d crane didn't disturb LTM 1130/1300's existing
  (non-preExploded) behavior, and that allOfflineContentUrls() picked up
  the new crane automatically (it derives model3d cranes generically from
  COUNTERWEIGHT_DATA, no manual list to update).

10.69 Added model3d.extraParts - supplementary GLBs for a part that can't
      come through the main assembly export at all
  Follow-up to 10.68: the person asked why LTM 1110's Winch 2* (p8w)
  wasn't visible in 3D. Confirmed rather than assumed - their own Onshape
  instance list showed 11 real instances (a distinct "Replacement
  Ballast/Winch 2*" item, visibly a substantial box in their own exploded
  view, plus the two "Counterweight Plate 7" bodies from 10.68), but the
  GLB actually traversed only 10 groups. The missing one wasn't a mapping
  bug - the geometry itself never made it into the file.
  Root cause, tracked down with the person live in their Onshape session
  rather than guessed at from here: that instance lives in a Composite
  Part (a merged/boolean result, its own object type in Onshape) which
  the assembly-level glTF exporter silently drops even though it's a
  normal referenced instance in the tree. Tried making it a plain
  independent solid first ("Copy here..." on the composite) - didn't
  work, produced a second composite instead of converting the type, so
  abandoned that path rather than keep guessing at Onshape's own object
  model from outside it.
  Landed on exporting the Composite Part on its own as a separate small
  GLB instead (its direct Export... command handles it fine standalone -
  confirmed after a first attempt came through Draco-compressed, which
  the app's vendored GLTFLoader has no decoder for; the person turned
  compression off and re-exported successfully).
  Added model3d.extraParts: [{ url, appId, rotationY }] - loadModel() is
  now async and, after the main model is positioned (explode or
  preExploded, either way), loads each extra GLB, treats its whole
  contents as one rigid unit mapped to one app id (no partMap needed - a
  dedicated file needs no name matching), and places it via a new
  placeAboveWholeModel(): directly above the ENTIRE main model's
  bounding box, centered over its XZ footprint, same STACK_GAP clearance
  as everything else. Deliberately does NOT reuse explodeStack()'s
  generic "most-spread axis" detection - a whole model's bbox is
  reliably wider/deeper than tall, so that heuristic would pick the
  wrong axis here; Y is hardcoded as up instead (true for every export
  seen so far). rotationY corrects for a standalone single-part export
  carrying its own Part Studio's default orientation, unrelated to the
  main assembly's - the person confirmed the first attempt was rotated
  wrong, corrected to 90 deg and re-verified visually before shipping,
  same iterate-on-a-screenshot approach as every other tuned constant
  this session (STACK_GAP, pairPushDistance, etc).
  All main-model roots now sit inside a wrapping THREE.Group alongside
  any extra parts, so frameCamera()/scene-add/clearScene() (which only
  ever handled a single opaque "entry.root") needed no changes at all -
  the container is just another Object3D as far as they're concerned.
  allOfflineContentUrls() updated to walk extraParts[].url too, so the
  Cache All Offline Content button and status ticks stay accurate without
  a separate manual list.
  Verified in-browser: p8w's real weight and countsToward:false behavior
  unaffected (namedParts entries work identically whether they came from
  the main file or an extra one), highlights the correct box sitting on
  top of the stack, no console errors. Full crane sweep re-run clean
  across all 7 cranes plus a full LTM 1110 id-by-id pass (p1-p7, p8w,
  p8b) - p8b, which still has genuinely no geometry anywhere, confirmed
  to fail gracefully (correct weight shown, nothing to highlight, no
  error) same as before this change.
  Follow-up same day: placeAboveWholeModel() centered on the WHOLE main
  model's XZ footprint, which is usually wider/deeper than the specific
  top plate the part actually sits on (lower plates in a stack are often
  bigger) - looked visibly off-center, sitting toward a corner rather
  than centered on its own plate. Fixed generically: find every mesh
  whose own top edge sits within STACK_GAP of the model's highest point,
  union those into a "top slab" box, and center on THAT instead of the
  whole model - no hardcoded app id for "which part is the top plate."
  Also added rotationX/rotationZ alongside the existing rotationY, since
  a standalone single-part export's orientation is a coin flip relative
  to the main assembly and might need correcting on any axis, not just
  yaw. Exact rotation for LTM 1110's Winch 2* still unresolved as of this
  entry - the person is dialing in the correct "lying flat" orientation
  directly in Onshape's own rotate tool rather than more blind
  trial-and-error rotationX values from here, and may re-export with it
  baked in (or swap the part directly into the main assembly export
  instead of using extraParts at all, in which case this whole mechanism
  won't even be needed for this specific part - it'd just become a normal
  partMap entry like any other plate). Left at rotationY: 90 only (the
  one axis not contradicted so far) pending whichever file arrives next.
  Resolved same day: person swapped Winch 2* directly into the main
  Onshape assembly (as "Part_1") and re-exported, exactly the "won't even
  be needed" outcome above - added 'Part_1': 'p8w' to the regular
  partMap, removed the extraParts entry and the standalone composite GLB
  entirely. Bumped the main model's ?v= (content changed). extraParts/
  placeAboveWholeModel() stay in cwt3d.js unused by any crane right now -
  real, working infrastructure for the same situation recurring on a
  future crane, not speculative code. Verified in-browser: p8w renders
  lying flat, correctly positioned on top of the stack per the person's
  own layout, no console errors. Full crane sweep clean.

10.70 Added Draco decoder support; wired 3D clicks into the existing
      slotGroup cycling behavior
  Draco: the person's next batch of GLBs (LTM 1300/1130 redo, LTR 1220,
  LTM 1250 - new, worked on separately) all failed to load with "No
  DRACOLoader instance provided." Rather than ask for four re-exports
  with compression toggled off (and every future one after that, since
  it's apparently Onshape's default GLB export setting), vendored a
  matching DRACOLoader.js + decoder (JS/WASM) from the same three.js
  release already in use (r185, confirmed against the vendored
  three.core.min.js's own REVISION string) and wired one shared decoder
  instance into loadGLTFAsync()'s GLTFLoader. Decoder files vendored
  locally under vendor/three/draco/, not fetched from Google's CDN (same
  reasoning as every other vendored dependency this app uses - see
  vendor/three's own original header). Added to allOfflineContentUrls()
  alongside the rest of the 3D viewer's own files. Verified: all four
  pending files decode and traverse cleanly now: 15/8/11/10 group nodes
  respectively - full crane sweep re-run clean, no regression on the
  already-shipped (non-Draco) LTM 1130/1300/1110 files either.
  Slot cycling: separately, the person noticed LTM 1110's Winch 2*/
  Replacement Ballast slot (methodology.txt 10.7) didn't cycle in 3D the
  way its 2D hotspot already does, now that Winch 2* has real geometry
  (10.69/10.70's Part_1 swap) to click on. onCanvasClick() previously
  only ever called window.toggleCwtPlate() - added a slotGroup lookup
  per namedPart (built once per load from the app's own component data,
  same reused-signal pattern as `separate`/`explodeTop`) so a click on a
  slot member's mesh now calls the SAME window.cycleCwtSlotGroup()
  already used by the 2D view, instead of a plain toggle. Verified both
  via direct cycleCwtSlotGroup() calls and real canvas clicks on the
  winch mesh - cycles none -> Winch 2* -> Replacement Ballast -> none
  correctly (Replacement Ballast itself still has no 3D geometry, same
  as before - clicking past it just deselects, no error).

10.71 CWT Combinations: LTM 1300/1130 redone with preExploded; added LTR
      1220 and LTM 1250 - first two cranes with 3D models built entirely
      around a preExploded file, no auto-explode debugging at all
  Person redid LTM 1300 and LTM 1130's models in their own already-
  exploded layout (same as LTM 1110, 10.67/10.69) and sent LTR 1220 and
  LTM 1250 as new cranes, all four in the same batch, all Draco-
  compressed (10.70 shipped same day, otherwise none of these four would
  have loaded at all).
  LTM 1300/1130: partMap unchanged for both - same node-naming convention
  as the originals, still a direct lookup. Just preExploded: true added
  and the old explain-the-auto-explode-heuristics comments trimmed down
  (10.63-10.66's rigid-block/bottom-edge-sort/pair-clearance/explodeTop
  fixes no longer apply to either crane now, though the code itself stays
  in cwt3d.js - still real, working infrastructure for whichever future
  crane's export isn't pre-exploded).
  LTR 1220: partMap is NOT a direct lookup here - plate 2/3 are 3 side-by-
  side pairs (not a vertical stack; each pair sits at its own tier,
  tiers stacked on each other), and all 6 CAD bodies are labelled just
  "Counterweight_plate_2"/"_3" with no a/b/c suffix telling which tier is
  which. Inferred the app's own a/b/c ids from Y position instead,
  cross-checked two ways before shipping: (1) matched against this
  crane's own combo progression, where tier a is added first, b second, c
  third, each combo a strict superset of the last - so bottom-to-top by Y
  should read a/b/c in that order if the physical build-up matches the
  combo order, which it should since combos are cumulative; (2) checked
  against the 2D hotspot's own SVG Y-coordinates, where p2c/p3c's hotspot
  sits at the smallest image-Y (= highest up on the page = physically
  topmost), consistent with (1). Verified a third way, empirically, after

  highlighted directly above the receptacle, at the bottom. Selected tier
  c alone - highlighted the very top of the stack. Both matched the
  inference exactly.
  LTM 1250: fully direct, unambiguous partMap - every CAD body's name
  matches an app component name exactly, including "Counterweight_7"
  having no "Plate" in it same as the app's own "Counterweight 7" name
  for p7 (the one component on this crane that isn't itself called a
  "plate"). Hook_Ballast_8 is 2 CAD bodies sharing one name (GLTFLoader
  auto-renames the second to "_1"), matching p8's separate:true side-
  mounted pair.
  Verified in-browser for all four: every component id resolves to its
  correct real weight and highlights the correct mesh, receptacle sits at
  the bottom on both redone cranes and both new ones, LTM 1130's winch
  still on top post-redo, LTM 1250's Hook Ballast pair highlights both
  sides together. Full 7-crane sweep clean, no console errors - 5 of 7
  cranes now have 3D models (1110, 1130, 1220, 1250, 1300).

  Reported: cycling LTM 1110's Winch 2*/Replacement Ballast slot (10.70)

10.73 Outrigger Support Positioning: added ground bearing pads (bog mats),
      with real rectangle-vs-chassis collision detection, not just the
      leg's own point
  Person places 3.5m x 2.4m mats under P1-P4 sometimes to improve ground
  bearing pressure, and wants the MUST MOVE / clash logic to account for
  the mat's own footprint - "the real thing that needs collision
  detection" - not just the leg's single contact point, which is all
  clashesWithFootprint() ever checked. Confirmed before writing any math:
  the mat is centered on the leg's own contact point (not offset to one
  edge), and its 3.5m length always runs inline with the carrier's own
  length axis regardless of which corner it's under (never rotated per-
  leg - it's a physical mat sitting on the ground, not a rotated marker).
  Person also asked for a PER-LEG toggle, not one global on/off - mats go
  down one at a time as they're actually placed on site, not all four at
  once.
  Added padClashesWithFootprint(): a straightforward axis-aligned
  rectangle-vs-rectangle overlap test between the pad (centered at the
  leg's plotted x/y, half-width/half-length from the two new Pad Length/
  Pad Width inputs) and the chassis footprint rectangle - both already
  expressed in the same un-rotated local frame FOOTPRINTS/calcCAD() use
  for everything else, so no new coordinate math needed, just a proper
  AABB test instead of a point-in-box test. checkClash() is the single
  entry point calcCAD() calls for both the "if moved" and "if left in
  place" checks - transparently swaps to the pad-aware test only for
  legs with their own checkbox on (cad-pad-1..4), point-only test
  unchanged for every other leg, so default behavior (all four off) is
  byte-for-byte the same as before this change.
  Drawn on the site plan as a translucent violet rectangle at the leg's
  own final position (drawPadBox(), same rotated-polygon approach as the
  existing chassis footprint box), plus a lighter dashed ghost pad at an
  optional leg's "if moved" target spot so the footprint that WOULD be
  needed there is visible before deciding to relocate it.
  Verified with real numbers before screenshotting anything (LTM 1130,
  1.8m lateral shift): P1's own point sits at relX=1967mm, outside the
  chassis's 1425mm half-width - point-only check correctly says
  "optional." Its 2.4m-wide pad's near edge sits at 1967-1200=767mm,
  well inside that same 1425mm boundary - pad-aware check correctly
  flips it to "MUST MOVE" once P1's pad checkbox is on. Confirmed live in
  the browser: exact same flip, and the screenshot shows the pad
  rectangle visibly overlapping the dashed chassis footprint outline,
  matching the math. Regression-swept the outrigger tab across all 7
  cranes with every pad checkbox off (a real shift applied on each,
  not just the zero-shift case) - identical "optional" results to
  pre-change behavior, confirming the default path is untouched. No
  console errors.

10.74 Outrigger ground pads: recolored to match this tab's own existing
      current/future convention (yellow now, blue if moved)
  10.73's pad rectangles/ghost pad were both violet, a color not used

  everywhere else (compare mode's old-center dot is the same yellow
  #e5a900; the existing ghost target dot/line for an optional leg is

  something already established rather than inventing a third color
  language.
  Current pad (drawPadBox at o.x/o.y): solid yellow outline/fill,
  unchanged from 10.73 otherwise. Its own marker DOT also switches to
  yellow when a pad is present (overriding the leg's must-move green /
  optional amber dot specifically) - the must-move/optional status isn't
  lost, since it's still fully carried by the connecting line's own

  fill changes. Ghost pad (drawPadBox at o.movedX/o.movedY): recolored

  "pad (current)" (solid yellow) and "pad (if moved)" (dashed blue).
