10.75 LTM 1110's winch (p8w) swapped for its own real geometry via STEP
      reimport; discovered and fixed a real Group-vs-Object3D gap in
      cwt3d.js's node matching along the way
  10.69's "Part_1" was a working substitute (person pulled a part from
  LTM 1130's own document, since Onshape allows cross-document part
  reuse) - functional, but not this crane's own actual winch geometry.
  Person went back and did the proper fix on the source Composite Part
  itself: export it to STEP, reimport as a plain solid, swap that into
  the assembly in place of the old composite-linked instance, re-export.
  Confirmed by loading the new file directly: the composite's own
  geometry is now really there, landing as "Replacement_Ballast_<1>",
  visibly more detailed than the substitute (real housing/bolt-hole
  geometry, not a stand-in slab).
  What DIDN'T just work: this node came in as an Object3D, not the Group
  type every other matched node in every crane so far has been - the
  groupsByKey traversal only ever checked `obj.type === 'Group'`, so it
  silently skipped this node entirely (found by broadening a diagnostic
  dump to include Object3D types too, after the Group-only dump came up
  empty for anything winch-sized). Root cause is a structural
  difference in how a STEP-reimported node comes into the scene graph
  vs. a native Onshape occurrence, not a naming quirk - so the fix is
  generic, not a one-off: groupsByKey's traversal now accepts Object3D
  nodes alongside Group, still filtered by name via partMapKeyFor()
  exactly as before. Confirmed this can't misfire for any existing
  crane: the auto-generated "occurrence_of_X" wrapper nodes (Object3D
  type everywhere already, present in every crane's export) never
  coincidentally share a name with a real partMap key - partMap only
  ever lists a part's own name, never its wrapper's - so loosening the
  filter only ever adds a match, never creates a false one.
  partMap updated to 'Replacement_Ballast_<1>': 'p8w' (was 'Part_1').
  Verified in-browser: all 8 of LTM 1110's own ids resolve to correct
  weights, p8w highlights the new, more detailed winch mesh correctly
  positioned on top of the stack. Full 7-crane sweep plus a dedicated
  re-check of every other crane's own component ids (1130, 1220, 1250)
  confirms the loosened type filter didn't disturb anything - identical
  results to before this change. No console errors.
  Also downloaded, this same session: the LTM 1650 carrier (46MB) and
  LTM 1110 carrier (32MB) GLBs, via direct-download Nextcloud links
  (curl straight to disk, bypassing the chat upload size limit) -
  received and validated (real glTF binary headers, not error pages),
  not yet inspected/wired up - that's the next mission's work, not part
  of this fix.

10.76 Outrigger Support Positioning: added a 3D carrier (chassis) preview,
      the "next mission" flagged in 10.75
  New vendor/carrier3d.js module, deliberately much simpler than
  cwt3d.js: no partMap, no click selection/highlighting, no explode
  logic. The carrier is a single rigid whole - its own real assembled
  position from the CAD export IS the intended view (nothing to pull
  apart into an exploded layout), and its materials already carry the
  crane's own Liebherr colours baked in from Onshape (confirmed by
  inspecting both GLBs' material list directly - yellow/red/grey/black
  already present, not flat grey placeholder), so nothing needed
  recolouring either. Reuses the same vendored three.js/GLTFLoader/
  DRACOLoader/OrbitControls modules and shared Draco decoder as
  cwt3d.js - both these carrier exports and the counterweight exports
  come out of Onshape Draco-compressed by default.
  Explicitly scoped as "look and see" only, per the person's own
  framing - a toggle button on the Outrigger tab, NOT wired into that
  tab's own must-move/clash-detection math. That integration (matching
  the pad-collision work from 10.73/10.74) is possible future work, not
  part of this.
  Files moved from the session's scratch download location into
  outrigger/models/ (ltm1110-carrier.glb, ltm1650-carrier.glb) -
  matches the existing counterweight/models/ convention. New
  CARRIER_MODELS map (index.html) keyed the same as
  cadFleetData/COUNTERWEIGHT_DATA ('1110', '1650') - only 2 of the
  outrigger tab's 7 cranes have an exported carrier model so far, so
  the preview card only shows for those two, hidden entirely for the
  other 5 (checked: 1130/1300 correctly hide it, 1110/1650 correctly
  show it).
  Switching crane always resets the toggle back to hidden/off - same
  reasoning as CWT's setCwtView reset, don't try to carry "3D on"
  across a crane that might not have a model. Tracked with its own
  carrier3DModelKey/carrier3DActive state rather than piggybacking on
  calcCAD()'s own modelKey read, since calcCAD() also runs on every
  shift/pad input change - resetting an open preview every time someone
  nudges a shift value would be a real regression, not just noise.
  allOfflineContentUrls() extended to include both carrier GLBs and
  carrier3d.js's own vendor file list, deduped against cwt3d.js's
  existing vendor/three/* entries (both viewers share the same core
  three.js/GLTFLoader/DRACOLoader/OrbitControls files) via a Set - so
  "Cache All Offline Content" on the other tabs picks these up too,
  without listing the same vendored files twice.
  Verified in-browser: card hidden for 1130/1300, shown for 1110/1650;
  toggle shows/hides the canvas and correctly fetches+renders each
  crane's own model (confirmed visually - Liebherr yellow/black LTM
  1110, primarily grey/white LTM 1650 chassis - matches what each
  crane's own material list contains); switching crane while the
  preview was open resets the toggle back to "Show 3D Carrier Model"
  and hides the canvas; no console errors.

10.77 3D Carrier Preview: tied outrigger pad positions to the site plan
  Person's own framing after seeing 10.76 work: "can you try tie them
  to a site plan". Adds leg markers and pad boxes (current position:
  solid; "if moved" target: ghosted) into the 3D scene, positioned
  using the SAME leg/pad data the 2D canvas itself draws from
  (calcCAD()'s own mappedOutriggers/lastFp - x/y/movedX/movedY/pad
  passed through unreinterpreted, including the exact same "only ghost
  when it differs from current" condition the 2D canvas already uses)
  - not a separate parallel calculation that could quietly drift from
  the 2D plan's own numbers.
  The hard part: mapping the site plan's real-world mm coordinates
  (relative to the slew center) onto the CAD model's own arbitrary
  local coordinate space. Chose NOT to rescale the model to fit
  FOOTPRINTS' width/front/rear figures - the CAD export is already
  dimensionally accurate (more so than those OEM-sheet-derived
  approximations), rescaling it would only introduce distortion.
  Instead: measure the model's own bounding box, then use FOOTPRINTS'
  front/(front+rear) as a FRACTION to locate where within that box the
  slew center sits along the model's length axis (Z, confirmed the
  longer axis for both cranes' exports) - this only requires knowing
  which END of the box is the front, not the model's true real-world
  size. That one fact - front-at-min-Z or front-at-max-Z - is the only
  thing that can't be derived from the geometry alone.
  Confirmed LTM 1110's orientation by rendering a straight-down
  (orthographic-style) view and visually identifying the driving cab
  (clear windshield/mirror geometry, unambiguous) - cab sits at min Z,
  so frontAtMinZ: true. Verified this is actually right, not just
  plausible, by rendering the calculated P1-P4 marker positions
  alongside the real model from the same straight-down angle: all four
  land exactly on the tips of the model's own four outrigger beams,
  both front pair and rear pair, with the left/right pairing also
  matching the 2D plan's own P2=front-right/P3=front-left convention.
  Shifted-position test (ghost pads) confirmed too: "must move" legs'
  solid pad relocates to the new spot (matches 2D - no ghost, since
  x already equals movedX there), "optional but clashing" legs show
  the current solid pad plus a distinct blue ghost box at the target
  spot, exactly mirroring the 2D canvas's own two-state behavior.
  LTM 1650 is a different story: its export is far sparser (28 parts
  vs 1110's 289, confirmed back in 10.76) and only has ONE of the two
  outrigger-beam pairs modeled at all - no driving cab geometry either,
  so there was nothing to visually confirm frontAtMinZ against. Used
  the same fraction-based math with a best-effort frontAtMinZ: true
  guess, then checked it the same way as 1110 - rendered the
  calculated markers against the model. The front pair (P2/P3) landed
  exactly on the one modeled beam, which is real confirmation of the
  length-axis and left/right calibration both being correct. The rear
  pair (P1/P4) landed right at the very edge of the model's own
  bounding box, close enough to the tip that the sparse export is more
  likely explanation than a calibration error (this model is plausibly
  just missing some rear-most chassis structure - the same document
  this crane's Composite Part/STEP-reimport saga came from, see
  10.75's methodology entry). Flagged uncertain: true on '1650' in
  CARRIER_CALIBRATION for this reason - the math is consistent and the
  confirmable half checks out, but nothing here should be taken as
  precisely confirmed the way 1110's is until a fuller carrier export
  exists for it.
  Marker/pad colours match the 2D legend exactly (#e5a900 current,
  #38bdf8 ghost) rather than inventing a separate 3D palette. Sync is
  replayed automatically once a still-loading model finishes (toggling
  3D on and calcCAD() firing happen at nearly the same time, so the
  very first sync call almost always arrives before the multi-MB model
  has finished fetching - would otherwise silently do nothing and wait
  for the next unrelated input change). CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped 1->2.
  Card subtitle and in-canvas label text updated to reflect that this
  is no longer look-and-see only, with an explicit "approximate, not
  survey-precision" caveat - the calibration is a real, checked
  alignment, but still built from OEM dimension-sheet figures and a
  bounding-box fraction, not a CAD-to-CAD coordinate match.

10.78 3D Carrier Preview: anchor calibration to the model's own outrigger
      geometry instead of only the FOOTPRINTS formula
  Person's own screenshot, on LTM 1650: the calculated pad marker and
  the model's own real outrigger foot (visible on the CAD geometry
  itself - a small red-marked cylinder at the end of the beam) were
  clearly two separate points, not lined up. "can we get these two
  points to line up thats is what all the p1/4 symbolize the outrigger
  pos." Fair - 10.77's calibration was entirely formula-derived
  (FOOTPRINTS' front/rear as a fraction along the model's measured
  length), and FOOTPRINTS is itself an OEM-sheet approximation, not
  measured off this specific CAD export - close, evidently not exact.
  Fix: when the model actually has its own outrigger beam/foot geometry
  (not guaranteed - LTM 1650's export is missing one whole beam pair,
  see 10.76/10.77), treat that geometry as ground truth and nudge the
  calibration to match it, rather than trusting the formula alone.
  refineCalibrationFromGeometry() finds each of the 4 corners' own
  farthest-reaching point (the foot sits at the very tip of the
  extended beam, so "the single most extreme point in that quadrant"
  finds it directly - no need to identify which named part is "the
  foot," which isn't possible anyway since Onshape's own part names
  are all just "Part N", nothing semantic to match against), matches
  each detected point to its real P-id using the existing frontAtMinZ/
  lateralSign flags, and shifts the WHOLE calibration's
  lateralCenter/slewZ by the average (detected - predicted) offset
  across whichever legs matched. A sanity check (detected distance
  from center must be within 0.5x-1.5x that leg's own known r) stops a
  missing leg's quadrant from grabbing some unrelated far-off part
  (cab, tail block) as a false match - exactly the situation 1650's
  rear pair is in.
  Result, checked the same way as 10.77 (zoomed screenshot of the
  actual rendered corner): LTM 1650's front-pair marker, previously
  visibly offset from the real foot, now sits essentially coincident
  with it. LTM 1110 - already good in 10.77 - stayed good; the
  refinement found a genuine but small correction there too (its
  formula estimate was close, not exact).
  Also flattened the leg marker (cylinder height 0.35->0.05, y-offset
  0.18->0.06): a raised marker reads as visually offset from the real
  foot in any angled (non-top-down) view purely from camera
  perspective, even once the underlying X/Z position is exactly
  right - most of what was left of the visible gap after the geometry
  fix was this, not a calibration error. Confirmed via a pure top-down
  render (matches 10.77's own verification method) that ground-plane
  X/Z alignment was already exact before this - the flattening is a
  legibility fix for the default angled camera, not a second
  correctness fix.
  Calibration is computed once per model and cached (calibrationCache
  keyed by modelKey) rather than redone on every calcCAD() call - the
  geometry scan traverses every mesh in the model, worth avoiding on
  every shift/pad keystroke. baseLegs (each leg's real CURRENT r/x/y,
  fresh from cadFleetData) added as a new argument to
  __carrier3dSyncOutriggers() specifically for this anchoring purpose -
  deliberately not reused from lastMapped's own x/y, which already
  reflect whatever the shift inputs currently show (calcCAD()'s own
  plotX/plotY logic), not necessarily the physical current position.
  CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped 2->3. Verified in-browser: all 5 cranes
  without a carrier model still correctly hide the card; LTM 1110 with
  pads + a shift still renders the ghost pad correctly (10.77's own
  behaviour untouched by this change); no console errors.

10.79 3D Carrier Preview: whole-chassis ghost footprint, gated behind
      Compare mode; Fit View / Top View camera controls
  Person's own request after seeing the per-leg ghost pads working:
  "can we get a ghosting box where the next cranes pos is going to be
  on the 3d model... doesnt need to be the carrier 3d model could be
  something like use used to repersent the bog mats ? just transperent
  dashed line version?" - i.e. don't re-render a whole second carrier
  model at the shifted spot, just reuse the same flat, dashed, ghost-
  pad visual language already built for the per-leg pads, sized to the
  whole chassis footprint instead of one pad.
  Sized/positioned using FOOTPRINTS (the same width/front/rear figures
  the 2D canvas's own drawFootprintBox() rectangle uses), offset by the
  current shift, through the same siteToWorld() calibration transform
  already used for everything else in this feature - no new
  coordinate math needed, just a bigger box.
  Person's own follow-up question caught something the first pass
  missed: "should we have that on a toggle aswell like the 2d version
  show old ver new pos?" - checked the 2D canvas's own logic and
  confirmed: its ghost FOOTPRINT rectangle only draws when
  compareMode is true (see its own `if (fp && compareMode && hasShift)
  drawFootprintBox(...)` call), while the per-leg ghost PADS are
  unconditional (drawn whenever a leg's position differs, regardless
  of compareMode). The 3D side had gotten this wrong - the new
  whole-chassis ghost was unconditional on any shift, not gated by
  compareMode like its 2D counterpart. Fixed: __carrier3dSyncOutriggers
  now also takes compareMode, and the whole-chassis ghost box checks it
  alongside shiftX/shiftY - per-leg ghost pads correctly left
  unconditional, matching 2D exactly in both cases now, not just one.
  compareMode threaded through from index.html's own `compareMode`
  variable (set by toggleCompareMode(), which already calls calcCAD()
  itself, so no extra wiring needed to keep it live).
  Extracted the shared ghost-box building (transparent fill + wireframe
  outline) into addGhostBox(), used by both the per-leg pad ghosts and
  the new whole-chassis one - and upgraded the wireframe from a plain
  solid LineBasicMaterial to an actual LineDashedMaterial (dashSize
  0.15, gapSize 0.1, wire.computeLineDistances() called after
  construction - required or a "dashed" material renders solid) since
  the person specifically asked for "dashed line" and the 2D plan's own
  ghost convention is dashed too - the per-leg ghosts got this upgrade
  for free along with the new footprint ghost, not just the new one.
  Also added, unprompted but a natural fit given how much this session
  has leaned on comparing the 3D view against the model's own geometry
  by eye: Fit View and Top View buttons (a small toolbar row, shown
  alongside the canvas). Fit View just re-runs the existing
  frameCamera(). Top View is new - deliberately NOT a literal straight-
  down camera (0.5° off vertical instead): true vertical is a
  degenerate case for OrbitControls, whose internal orientation math
  breaks when camera "up" and "forward" become parallel. An early
  version of this (used only for this session's own debugging
  screenshots, never shipped) changed camera.up itself to work around
  that, which left dragging behaved oddly afterwards - OrbitControls
  caches object.up at construction time and doesn't notice it changing
  later. The 0.5°-off-vertical approach avoids the degenerate case
  without ever touching camera.up, so drag-to-rotate afterwards behaves
  completely normally - confirmed by scripting a drag right after
  clicking Top View and checking the model actually rotated, not some
  broken/inverted state.
  CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped 3->5 (one bump for the ghost footprint work,
  one for Fit/Top View). Verified in-browser: shift without compare
  mode shows per-leg ghosts only; enabling compare mode adds the
  whole-chassis dashed ghost; Top View gives a clean straight-down
  render (useful for exactly the kind of alignment checking this
  session has been doing manually); drag-to-rotate and Fit View both
  work normally afterwards; no console errors.

10.80 3D Carrier Preview: current pads stay put, ALL legs ghost, linked
      Compare toggle on the 3D panel itself
  Person's own follow-up after 10.79: "can we have all future
  positions ghosted to there they will lay and leave the current bog
  mag pos the yellow ones were they are so i can visually see the
  confilicts." Real bug, not a preference: the solid "current" pad's
  position came from `leg.x/leg.y`, which is calcCAD()'s own plotX/
  plotY - and for a "must move" leg, plotX/plotY is ALREADY the moved
  target, not the true current spot (see calcCAD()'s own isRequired
  logic). That's the right call for the 2D canvas (a "must move" leg
  reads better shown at where it's going), but wrong for this: the
  person's whole point of the 3D ghosting is comparing a mat's TRUE
  current position against ALL the shifted targets at once, and a
  "must move" leg's solid pad silently jumping to its target - with no
  ghost at all, since x already equalled movedX there - hid exactly
  the comparison they were trying to make for those legs.
  Fixed by using baseLegs (already being computed and passed in for
  10.78's calibration anchoring, now used for BOTH purposes) as the
  source of truth for the solid marker/pad position instead of leg.x/
  leg.y - true current position, every leg, unconditionally. Ghost pads
  now key off leg.movedX/movedY instead of the old "only if it differs
  from leg.x" check - movedX/movedY is always the real fully-shifted
  target regardless of required/optional (calcCAD() sets it
  unconditionally, unlike plotX/plotY), so this correctly ghosts all
  four legs now, not just the ones the 2D canvas happens to ghost.
  Second part of the same message: "can we also have a small Compare
  old/new position check box linked to the top 2d one but also on the
  3d panel bar." Added a second checkbox to the 3D toolbar row,
  wired to the exact same `compareMode` flag as the original one atop
  the 2D plan - toggleCompareMode(sourceEl) now syncs BOTH checkboxes'
  checked state to whichever one fired the change event, so they can
  never show a different state from each other. Saves scrolling back
  up to the 2D card just to flip it while looking at the 3D view.
  CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped 5->6. Verified in-browser: solid pads for
  "must move" legs now stay at their real current spot instead of
  jumping (checked directly against the pre-fix screenshot); enabling
  compare mode via the NEW 3D checkbox ghosts all 4 legs and syncs the
  2D checkbox's own checked state too (and vice versa, same code path);
  cranes without a carrier model still correctly hide the card; no
  console errors.

10.81 3D Carrier Preview: fixed the pad/foot alignment, for real this time
  Person's own screenshot on LTM 1110: the calculated pad marker and the
  model's own real outrigger foot geometry were STILL clearly two
  separate points - not a small residual, a real visible gap on all four
  corners. This meant 10.78's original "geometry-anchored calibration"
  claim was wrong, or had regressed. Root-caused properly this time by
  reproducing it locally (confirmed: identical gap, even in a pure
  straight-down Top View where camera-angle parallax isn't a factor) and
  iterating through several wrong theories before landing on the real
  fix - worth recording all of them, since each one looked plausible and
  each one was checked, not assumed:
   1. Single global averaged offset (10.78's actual bug): applying one
      averaged (detected - predicted) offset uniformly to all four legs
      can only correct a constant frame-offset error, not the kind of
      per-leg/scale mismatch this turned out to have. Replaced with an
      independent per-axis least-squares LINE fit (site mm -> world
      metres) through however many legs' geometry could be confirmed -
      genuinely better, but the fitted line still doesn't pass through
      any single leg's own point exactly, so a real (smaller) gap
      remained even after this fix alone.
   2. Farthest-reaching single VERTEX per quadrant: wrong, because a long
      rectangular BEAM's own extreme corner is a real vertex too (unlike
      a round foot's bounding-box corner, which isn't a point on the
      shape at all) - this just found the beam's own tip, not the foot.
   3. Most compact single MESH: also wrong. Dumped every mesh within 1.5m
      of one corner and found 500+ of them - this Onshape export splits
      every real part into hundreds of tiny per-face sub-meshes
      (millimetre-scale bolt heads, decals, fillets), so "compact" just
      grabbed some near-zero-size decal.
   4. What actually works: aggregate sub-meshes back into their own PART
      first (each real Onshape part occupies exactly one parent node in
      this export - confirmed by dumping and aggregating: one 2.7m-long
      part is obviously the beam, one short 0.95m-tall part is obviously
      the jack cylinder, one 0.5x0.5m part sitting right at the model's
      own lowest Y is obviously the foot plate), THEN filter to parts
      within 15cm of the model's own lowest point (the foot plate TOUCHES
      THE GROUND - the cylinder above it, the beam, and nearby hardware
      don't), THEN take the LARGEST footprint among those ground-level
      survivors (the plate itself, not incidental ground-adjacent
      hardware). Also switched from the winning part's bounding-box
      centre to its own vertex centroid, less sensitive to a single
      asymmetric detail (an off-centre boss, a bracket on one side)
      skewing a bounding box away from the part's true visual centre.
   5. Even with (4)'s detection now correctly finding the foot plate,
      there was STILL a real residual gap - because a least-squares fit
      through 4 points doesn't pass through any one of them exactly, and
      that residual was exactly what was still showing on screen. Fixed
      by not routing a leg's own CURRENT position through the fitted line
      at all when its geometry was directly confirmed: legAnchors (leg id
      -> its own exact detected point) is now checked first in
      siteToWorld(), bypassing the fit entirely for that leg's current
      marker/pad. The fitted line is still used for extrapolating to a
      SHIFTED (ghost) position, where there's no real geometry to check
      against anyway - imprecision there is far less visible/important
      than getting a leg's own CURRENT position exactly right.
  Verified by re-testing the exact same corner after each of the 5
  changes above, in order, with cropped/upscaled screenshots each time -
  confirms this wasn't guesswork, each step was checked against the
  previous one before moving to the next theory. Final result: LTM
  1110's marker sits exactly on the real foot geometry at all four
  corners (checked in Top View, no visible gap at any corner); LTM
  1650's front pair (the only pair with real geometry to check against)
  shows the same result; the whole-chassis ghost footprint and per-leg
  ghost pads (10.79/10.80's own features) still work correctly with a
  shift applied and Compare mode on - unaffected by any of this, since
  ghosts deliberately never use legAnchors. CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped
  6->12 across the whole debugging session (each iteration needed its
  own cache-bust to actually test against a fresh browser). Full 7-crane
  regression re-run at the end: cranes without a carrier model still
  correctly hide the card, both 1110 and 1650 still load and render with
  no console errors.

10.82 3D Carrier Preview: added LTM 1130/1160/1250/1300, and a new 1650
      CWT model - person's own overnight batch
  Person sent the rest of their carrier exports (Nextcloud folder share,
  Myslewer/Carrier/*) plus a new low-poly LTM 1650 counterweight model
  (Myslewer/Counterweight/LTM1650CWT-low.glb) and said to take the time
  overnight to work through them. Folder share downloaded as a single
  zip via .../download (redirects to the WebDAV ?accept=zip endpoint,
  confirmed with curl -sIL before committing to the full download) -
  ~200MB total, but that's the person's whole working folder (source
  .dwg/.dxf/.pdf files, CWT models for cranes already wired up). Only
  extracted the specific files actually needed
  (Carrier/LTM{1130,1160,1250,1300}CARRIER.glb,
  Counterweight/LTM1650CWT-low.glb) rather than the whole archive -
  no reason to touch already-integrated CWT models that weren't asked
  for.
  Carrier models: added to CARRIER_MODELS/CARRIER_CALIBRATION following
  the exact same process as 1110/1650 - rendered each in Top View,
  confirmed frontAtMinZ by identifying the driving cab (all four show it
  at the same end/side as 1110's own confirmed orientation, so
  frontAtMinZ: true, lateralSign: 1 for all - not assumed, checked per
  crane). Then ran the SAME per-leg geometry confirmation now used for
  1110/1650 (10.81's fix) against each: 1160, 1250, and 1300 all
  achieved full 4/4 leg confirmation (legAnchors has an entry for every
  leg - checked directly, not inferred from the render alone). 1130 only
  got 2/4 (P1/P3 confirmed; P2/P4's own detected candidate for those two
  legs came out far too close to the chassis centreline to be a real
  outrigger foot - the sanity check correctly
  rejected it rather than anchoring to a wrong point, so those two legs
  fall back to the fitted line, same graceful degradation 1650's missing
  rear pair already relies on. Not a bug - the detection heuristic
  earning less confidence on a differently-structured export is exactly
  what the sanity check exists to catch safely rather than silently
  accept.
  New LTM 1650 CWT model: NOT wired up in this pass. Inspecting it
  (1771 nodes, 882 meshes, 44 top-level "Part N" bodies with no semantic
  naming beyond "winch 2" and two winch-related assembly nodes) made
  clear this needs a real partMap - every other crane's partMap so far
  has had either semantic part names or a small enough part count to
  map by direct visual/positional inspection with reasonable confidence.
  44 anonymous bodies for a crane with 12 real components (1 receptacle
  pair + linked center section, 1 five-tonne pair, 7 interchangeable
  ten-tonne pairs, 2 winches) - roughly 2-4 CAD bodies per real
  component - is a real mapping task, and getting it wrong would show a
  crew INCORRECT weight/composition data, not just a slightly-off pad
  render. That's a different risk category to the carrier work above,
  which is look-and-see plus an approximate pad position - worth being
  honest that this wasn't rushed through overnight alongside the lower-
  stakes carrier work. File is staged at
  counterweight/models/ltm1650-cwt-staging.glb (not referenced by
  anything yet) for the next session to pick up, ideally with the
  person's own input on which part numbers correspond to which
  positions rather than pure geometric inference.
  Verified in-browser: all four new carriers show correctly for their
  own crane and stay hidden for every other crane; full cycle (toggle
  3D, all 4 pads on, Compare mode on, shift applied, reset, pads off,
  3D off) run against all 6 cranes with a carrier model plus 1100 (no
  model) - no console errors anywhere. CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped to 15
  across this investigation.

10.83 3D Carrier Preview: fixed foot-plate selection (largest reach, not
      largest footprint) - LTM 1130 "quite off" report
  Person reported (with two mobile screenshots) that the LTM 1130 3D
  layout was "quite off" - a real, visible regression from 10.81's own
  working fix for 1110. Root cause: 10.81's foot-plate selection took
  the LARGEST FOOTPRINT among ground-level candidates in each quadrant.
  That happened to work for 1110 because the true foot plate was also
  the largest ground-level part nearby, but it isn't a safe rule in
  general - 1130's export has some other ground-level part (closer to
  the chassis centreline, not a real outrigger foot) with a bigger
  bounding box than the actual plate, so it won the footprint contest
  and got wrongly anchored to, exactly matching what the person saw on
  their screen.
  Confirmed by dumping every ground-level part's own reach/footprint/
  groundGap directly (temporary __carrier3dDumpAll debug hook, removed
  again once done) rather than guessing: the real foot plate (e.g.
  Part_8, reach ~4.05m, groundGap≈0, footprint ~0.373 m²) was sitting
  right there in the candidate list, correctly passing the ground-level
  filter, just losing the footprint contest to a closer/bigger part.
  Fix: selection criterion changed from largest-footprint to largest-
  REACH among ground-level candidates - reach is what actually,
  physically distinguishes an extended outrigger foot from anything
  else sitting near the ground (a step, a storage box, incidental
  hardware). Footprint is now only a minimum-size sanity filter
  (0.02 m² / 2cm x 2cm), rejecting decal-scale noise, not the primary
  selector.
  Verified visually against 1130 (screenshot crop: all 4 corners now
  align with real beam/foot geometry) and via a legAnchors-coverage
  regression across all 6 carrier-equipped cranes (1110/1130/1160/1250/
  1300/1650 all confirmed 4/4 legs). 1650 showing full 4/4 confirmation
  initially looked suspicious given 10.76/10.77's own note that its
  export was missing outrigger geometry - re-checked with a proper,
  unclipped Top View screenshot and found that note was itself wrong:
  1650's export DOES have real, symmetric foot-plate-plus-beam geometry
  for all four corners (front and rear pairs both ~0.49 m² footprint,
  ~5.1m reach, near-zero ground gap) - an earlier session's visual
  impression from a clipped/zoomed screenshot led to a mistaken belief
  the rear pair was entirely absent. Worth noting as a reminder to
  verify claims like "this model is missing X" against the actual
  geometry data rather than carrying them forward as established fact.
  CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped to 19; both temporary debug hooks
  (__carrier3dDebug, __carrier3dDumpAll) removed before shipping.

10.84 Header: app version number next to the logo
  Person wanted a version number shown next to the MYSLEWER wordmark,
  same idea as their norwest-forms PWA. Asked whether it should reuse
  the existing CACHE_VERSION (already bumped every deploy, so always
  accurate, but reads as an internal build string like "v51-beta38")
  or be a separate, clean, user-facing version - person chose the
  separate version. Added a plain "v1.0" span next to the h1, small
  and muted (0.6rem, --text-muted, vertical-align:super) so it reads
  as a version tag rather than competing with the wordmark itself -
  static markup, not wired to any constant, since this is meant to be
  bumped by hand on meaningful releases rather than tracked
  automatically like CACHE_VERSION/CWT3D_VERSION/CARRIER3D_VERSION
  are. Checked at both a normal and a narrow (360px) mobile viewport -
  no wrapping or overlap with the update-check button.

10.85 3D Carrier Preview: dropped Top View, made Fit View bird's-eye and
      fit everything drawn, not just the model
  Person asked to get rid of the separate Top View button and have Fit
  View do the bird's-eye framing itself, AND zoom out to fit everything
  currently on screen - not just the carrier model. The old Fit View
  (an angled 3/4 view) only ever measured the model's own bounding box
  (frameCamera(root)), so a ghost pad or the whole-chassis ghost
  footprint (both drawn well outside the model itself once a shift is
  applied - see 10.79/10.80) could sit off-screen entirely with no way
  to bring it into frame besides manually dragging/zooming.
  Removed the Top View button and its window.__carrier3dTopView
  entirely. Fit View now calls a new fitView() that: (1) unions the
  model's own bounding box with outriggerGroup's (the markers/pads/
  ghost boxes built by applySync - see sceneBox()), so anything
  currently drawn is included; (2) uses the same near-vertical (0.5°
  tilt) bird's-eye angle Top View used to, for the same OrbitControls-
  degenerate-case reason (10.79's own explanation still applies
  unchanged); (3) derives camera distance from the camera's actual
  vertical FOV and current aspect ratio, fit against whichever
  horizontal axis (lateral X or longitudinal Z) actually constrains the
  canvas's own shape, rather than the old fixed 1.3x-of-larger-
  dimension guess, which could crop the narrower axis on a canvas
  that isn't roughly square.
  Verified with a big compare-mode shift applied (so the ghost
  footprint sits well outside the model) at both a narrow mobile
  width and a wide desktop width - the whole chassis, current pads,
  AND the shifted ghost footprint all land fully inside frame at both
  aspect ratios, nothing clipped. CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped to 20.

================================================================================

10.86 3D Carrier Preview: removed the permanent "approximate" overlay text
  Person said the "Outrigger pad positions shown below are approximate -
  the CAD model is aligned to..." caption blocked the view - it sat as
  a translucent chip over the top-left corner of the canvas the ENTIRE
  time the preview was open (not just transiently), covering part of
  the model itself, unlike the "Loading 3D model…" / error text it
  shares an element with, which are genuinely transient. Removed it
  outright rather than relocating it - added a setLabel() helper that
  also toggles the element's own display:none when there's nothing to
  show, so an empty caption doesn't leave a stray padded chip visible
  (the div's CSS gives it padding/background/border-radius regardless
  of content, so clearing textContent alone wasn't enough). Loading and
  error states are unchanged. Verified across all 6 carrier-equipped
  cranes: label is empty and display:none once loaded, no console
  errors. CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped to 21.

10.87 Ground Bearing Pads: mirror the P1-P4 toggles onto the 3D toolbar
  Person wanted the per-leg pad toggles (Ground Bearing Pads - P1/P2/
  P3/P4, above the 2D plan) reachable from the 3D toolbar too, next to

  Length/Width) stays only in its original spot above - only the on/

  Added syncPadToggle(legId, sourceEl), the same mirrored-checkbox

  event updates its counterpart and calls calcCAD(). padEnabledFor

  time after already toggling a pad above would show the toolbar's

  ran ON a change event - toggleCarrier3D() now pulls the real current

10.88 LTM 1650 CWT: wired up the real model, real partMap
  Person supplied a NEW export (Myslewer/Counterweight/LTM1650CWT.glb -
  not the earlier "-low" one staged back in 10.82) plus their own
  Onshape instance tree screenshot describing each stack's top-to-
  bottom part order, and asked to confirm the read before uploading
  the model. Cross-checked their description against the file's real
  geometry rather than trusting either alone:
    - Every "Part N" node name in the GLB matches the Onshape tree
      exactly (occurrence-of-Part-N, no collisions anywhere in the
      file) - unlike the earlier "-low" export, this one names
      individual plates, not just "Part N" as a generic default.
    - World position confirms both the left/right split (X identical
      to several decimals within each side) and the exact top-to-
      bottom order the person described, independently, for both
      stacks: Right 6->12->1->2->3->10->7->15, Left 13->9->8->16->4->
      14->5->11.
    - Mesh bounding-box thickness confirms the 5t/10t split: Part 6/13
      (the two "5t" plates) are 0.389m thick vs 0.68m for every 10t
      plate, same footprint (2.192 x 2.192) throughout - consistent
      with a 5t plate being roughly half a 10t one.
    - Person separately confirmed Part 16's <2> instance count in the
      Onshape tree wasn't a hidden duplicate ("I just dragged it") -
      confirmed independently too: exactly one "occurrence of Part 16"
      node exists anywhere in the file, sitting exactly where its
      stack position predicts.
  Wired up model3d on '1650' for real: each 10t PAIR (one plate per
  side, matching the 2D diagram's own "×2" ids p10a..p10g) maps both
  that pair's GLB node names to the same app id, paired by physical
  stack rank; p5x2 the same way for the two 5t plates. Winch 2 is a
  single clean node; Winch 3 is a 6-body sub-assembly under cryptic
  Onshape-generated names (id11__$ etc) - confirmed unique, all 6
  mapped to w3. p1 (All Counterweight Frame Receptacles) deliberately
  left UNMAPPED - its own GLB representation is a large, complex
  nested sub-assembly (hundreds of anonymous sub-parts, a different
  problem than the 16 clearly-identified plates) - shown as background
  geometry, not 3D-clickable; the 2D diagram remains how it's toggled.
  First attempt used cwt3d.js's own auto-explode (no preExploded flag)
  since the raw GLB's plates were only 0.1m apart, which read as
  "real assembled position, needs auto-explode" by the same logic
  that's worked for every non-preExploded crane so far. Wrong: the
  person had already exploded the stack themselves in Onshape (0.1m
  WAS their intended gap, not the real touching thickness) - letting
  auto-explode run on top of that doubled the spacing AND left the
  large unmapped p1 base geometry static while the plates moved away
  from it, which read as visually broken (confirmed by screenshot,
  not just theorized). Fixed by setting preExploded: true once this
  was clarified - trusts the person's own layout untouched, consistent
  with everything staying spatially correct relative to the static
  base. Verified: 3D toggle appears for 1650, click-to-select works
  (confirmed via an actual hit, not just no-console-errors), no
  console errors, CWT3D_VERSION unaffected by this entry (see 10.89
  for the version bump that also landed alongside it).

10.89 CWT 3D viewer: front view + fit-to-model default, fixed a
      perspective depth bug found along the way
  Person asked for the CWT 3D viewer's default camera to be a front
  view with everything fit in frame ("zoom to fit or whatever it's
  called"), after LTM 1650's own default 3/4-angle view (10.88) left
  its left counterweight stack sitting behind the model's own central
  base/winch bulk, only visible after manually dragging to rotate -
  same class of problem carrier3d.js's Fit View already solved for the
  outrigger tab (10.85), applied here to the CWT tab's own viewer.
  Replaced cwt3d.js's frameCamera() 3/4-angle fixed-margin guess with
  a straight-on front view (camera on +Z, default up, looking at the
  model's own bounding-box center) sized from the camera's actual FOV/
  aspect ratio, same approach as carrier3d.js's fitView - fit against
  whichever of X (width) or Y (height) actually constrains the
  canvas's own shape.
  Regression-checked against every other CWT-3D crane (1250, 1130,
  1110, 1300, 1220) before trusting it as a safe default change, not
  just eyeballing the one crane that prompted it - caught a REAL bug
  doing this: LTR 1220's counterweight is nearly as deep (Z) as it is
  tall (Y), and the initial version measured required distance only
  from the bounding box's CENTER depth, which under a perspective
  camera under-fits anything with real thickness - the model's own
  front (near-camera) face sits closer than the "fit" distance and
  gets magnified past the frame edges. Confirmed by comparing computed
  fit distance against the model's own half-depth (halfZ=1.2 against a
  fit distance of only 3.2 before margin - the front face was ~1.3x
  closer than the framing assumed) rather than guessing from the
  screenshot alone. Fixed by adding each axis's own halfZ to its
  required distance, so the fit is valid at the model's NEAREST point,
  not just its average depth - a no-op for a genuinely flat model,
  the actual fix for a thick one.
  Verified all 6 cranes (1650, 1250, 1130, 1110, 1300, 1220): no
  console errors, everything fits in frame with visible margin on all
  sides, nothing clipped, click-to-select still functions (confirmed
  via an actual hit on LTM 1650's stack, not just visual inspection).
  CWT3D_VERSION bumped to 12.

================================================================================

10.90 HOTFIX: LTM 1650 CWT plates were completely unclickable in
      production - GLTFLoader sanitizes spaces out of node names
  Person reported, on the LIVE deployed app, that "the only clickable
  item is winch 3" - a real bug in code shipped in 10.88, not a
  hypothetical. Root-caused with a temporary debug hook dumping
  groupsByKey and namedParts (removed before shipping the fix, same
  as every other debug-hook episode this doc records - never ship
  with one left in):
    - THREE.js's GLTFLoader sanitizes node names on load, replacing
      spaces with underscores - "Part 6" becomes "Part_6", "winch 2"
      becomes "winch_2". Confirmed directly: dumping every Group/
      Object3D name actually present in the loaded scene showed
      "Part_6", "Part_12", "winch_2", etc, not the space-containing
      names the raw glTF JSON (and this app's own partMap, copied
      from that raw JSON) both used.
    - Every other crane's partMap already happened to use underscore-
      style names (Counterweight_Plate_2, Receptacle_Plate_1, etc) -
      not because anyone knew about this sanitization, but because
      those Onshape part names never happened to contain a literal
      space to begin with. LTM 1650's new export (10.88) was the
      first one where real part names DID contain spaces ("Part 6",
      "winch 2"), which is what finally made this pre-existing,
      previously-invisible GLTFLoader behavior matter.
    - Net effect: every "Part N" and "winch 2" partMap entry silently
      matched nothing at all. The ONLY thing that DID match was two of
      winch 3's six sub-parts (id2__$, id5__$ - no spaces in those
      names to sanitize) - and one of those two happens to reference a
      1264-primitive mesh, large enough to visually cover most of the
      model's surface. That combination is exactly what read as "only
      winch 3 is clickable" - not a coincidence, a direct consequence
      of the naming mismatch plus one oversized mesh filling the gap.
      Checked that oversized mesh's own world bounding box before
      assuming it was itself a problem: it sits centered in the
      model's own upper-middle area between the two stacks, not
      overlapping either one - consistent with being real, detailed
      winch mechanism geometry (plausible for a hydraulic winch's
      internals), not an export mistake. Left as-is.
  Fix: changed partMap keys to the underscore form GLTFLoader actually
  produces ('Part 6' -> 'Part_6', 'winch 2' -> 'winch_2').
  Found a SECOND, unrelated bug in the same pass, via the same debug
  dump: 4 of winch 3's 6 sub-parts (id11__$, id8__$, id17__$, id14__$)
  still didn't match even after the naming fix. Cause: a glTF mesh
  with exactly ONE primitive becomes a bare THREE.Mesh on load, not a
  Group wrapping one Mesh child the way every multi-primitive part in
  this app so far has landed - confirmed by checking each of the 6
  sub-parts' own primitive count directly (the 4 broken ones: exactly
  1 each; the 2 working ones: 1264 and 50). The traversal's own type
  filter only ever checked for Group/Object3D, silently skipping
  plain Mesh nodes - a real gap for any future single-primitive part
  on any crane, not an LTM-1650-only fix. Added `|| obj.isMesh` to the
  filter.
  Verified end-to-end after both fixes: groupsByKey now finds all 23
  expected keys (16 plates + winch_2 + all 6 winch 3 sub-parts,
  5104 total meshes correctly distributed across the right appIds,
  not one appId absorbing everything). Clicked an actual plate and
  confirmed both the visual selection AND the Total Counterweight
  readout updated correctly (10.0t for the 5t pair) - not just "no
  console errors," the actual number a crew would rely on. Regression-
  checked every other CWT-3D crane (1250, 1130, 1110, 1300, 1220) -
  unaffected, as expected, since their own partMaps never had a space
  to begin with. CWT3D_VERSION bumped to 13.
  Lesson for future CWT models: if a new Onshape export's own part
  names contain spaces (check the Onshape instance tree directly, the
  same way the person already screenshots it for stack-order
  confirmation), use underscores in the partMap from the start rather
  than copying the space-containing name and finding out the hard way.

================================================================================

10.91 LTM 1650 CWT: made p1 (base/receptacle) clickable in 3D too, as one
      rigid block - person's own suggestion
  10.88 deliberately left p1 (All Counterweight Frame Receptacles)
  unmapped, reasoning that its GLB representation - a large, complex
  nested sub-assembly with hundreds of anonymously-named sub-parts -
  was too risky to guess a per-sub-part mapping for. Person, inspecting
  the file directly in a separate CAD viewer, pointed out this was
  overthinking it: p1 is only ever toggled as ONE unit everywhere else
  in the app (no per-sub-part granularity in the 2D diagram either), so
  it doesn't need per-sub-part mapping at all - just ONE partMap entry
  pointing at the sub-assembly's own top-level container node, treated
  as a single rigid block the same way any other multi-body partMap
  entry already works in this app.
  Confirmed the container node's exact name empirically rather than
  assumed, having just been burned by an incorrect naming assumption in
  10.90: raw name "98037278_002 <1>" loads as "98037278_002_<1>" (space
  sanitized to underscore, "<1>" suffix preserved as-is - a data point
  worth keeping in mind for any future name containing similarly unusual
  characters, not just plain spaces). Note there's ALSO a bare
  "98037278_002" node in the same file - that's the model's own root,
  not the receptacle: mapping that one instead would make the entire
  model - stack, winches, everything - a single clickable p1, wrong.
  Added '98037278_002_<1>': 'p1' to the partMap. Verified both via a
  programmatic toggle and an actual canvas click: the whole base/
  receptacle frame highlights correctly, cleanly excluding the plates
  and winch mechanism above it (screenshotted and visually confirmed,
  not assumed from the click succeeding alone) - Total Counterweight
  shows 25.0t with the OEM-approved checkmark, matching the 2D data
  exactly. CWT3D_VERSION bumped to 14.
  Person separately asked about getting Onshape to export this
  sub-assembly as one true merged part instead, for a cleaner file -
  their first attempt (selecting the sub-assembly and exporting it
  directly) came back still showing every individual part in the tree,
  which is expected: exporting a selection of parts (to STEP or GLB)
  preserves whatever hierarchy already exists, it does not merge
  anything. Onshape's Composite Part tool is what actually performs a
  boolean union into one new solid body - it's a modeling operation
  done BEFORE export, not an export option. Explained the same
  procedure already proven for Winch 2* (10.75): create the Composite
  Part from the selected solids, export THAT specifically to STEP
  (composite parts themselves get silently dropped by Onshape's
  assembly-level GLB exporter, 10.69), reimport the STEP as a plain
  solid, swap it into the assembly in place of the old individual
  instances, rename if possible, re-export the whole assembly.
  UPDATE: person tried the Composite Part / STEP swap and hit friction
  getting it to work cleanly - decided the '98037278_002_<1>' block-
  mapping above is a fine PERMANENT answer for this crane, not a
  stopgap waiting on a cleaner export. Nothing to revisit here unless
  the person independently gets a genuinely merged export later; this
  isn't tracked as outstanding work.

================================================================================

11. HOW TO SOURCE A NEW CRANE'S COUNTERWEIGHT DATA + 3D MODEL  [CONVERSATION]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Use this whenever a crane needs its CWT Combinations tab built or extended -
either from scratch, or adding a 3D model to a crane that currently only has
the 2D diagram. This is the person's own real, repeatable workflow, run the
same way across every crane so far - written down here as one procedure
instead of left scattered across the dozen-plus per-crane entries in section
10 that each describe living through some piece of it. Same relationship to
section 10 that section 9 has to section 5: section 10 is the record of what
actually happened and why (including the false starts); this section is the
current, distilled procedure.

11.1 Two source documents, from two different places
  a) The OEM operator manual PDF for that specific crane, "unlocked" (its
     own password/permission protection removed) before it's handed over.
     Just the Counterweight chapter needs extracting from it (10.1)
     - it's the source of the combination weights/ids/subset-sum data,
     not the 3D geometry.
     Extraction gotcha to check for, not assume away: this fleet's PDFs
     have shown up both plain and with a uniform per-character code-point
     shift applied by an embedded font subset (confirmed on the 1250 doc,
     10.1) - decode one word and confirm it reads correctly before
     trusting a bulk extraction. Tool availability isn't constant session
     to session either (poppler-utils present for some, absent for
     others, 10.1 vs section 9's own toolchain) - don't assume this
     session has what a previous one did.
  b) A GLB export of the vendor-supplied 3D CAD model, prepared in
     Onshape by the person (11.2) - this is the source of the 3D
     geometry/positions, not the weight data. The PDF and the GLB are
     independent inputs; nothing about one is inferred from the other.

11.2 Preparing the GLB in Onshape, before export
  Open the vendor-supplied 3D model in Onshape and:
  - Space the counterweight stack itself apart (~120mm between adjacent
    plates has been the standard gap used everywhere in this app -
    STACK_GAP in cwt3d.js matches it exactly, 10.59) so it reads as an
    exploded view rather than the plates' real touching-flush assembled
    position. This is now done BY HAND in Onshape for every new crane
    (10.71 onward), not left for the app's own auto-explode heuristics
    to figure out - see the UPDATE note on 10.59 for why the workflow
    moved this direction. The resulting model3d config uses
    preExploded: true, which tells cwt3d.js to trust these positions
    exactly and skip its own explode logic entirely.
  - Rename parts to real, meaningful names where Onshape allows it. Note
    a real Onshape UI limitation the person has hit directly: only the
    ASSEMBLY INSTANCE can always be renamed from the assembly tree; the
    underlying PART's own name (the one that actually ends up on the
    exported GLB node, see 11.4) sometimes can't be changed from there -
    it has to be renamed in the part's own Part Studio instead, which
    isn't always practical. When it isn't, the export ships with
    Onshape's own generic "Part N" names instead of anything semantic -
    not a blocker (see 11.4), just means the partMap has to be built by
    cross-referencing an Onshape instance-tree screenshot against real
    geometry instead of reading it straight off descriptive names.
  - Swap out any Composite Parts (an Onshape object type - a merged/
    boolean result, not a plain solid). These get SILENTLY DROPPED by
    Onshape's assembly-level GLB exporter even though they're a normal
    part of the tree (discovered the hard way on LTM 1110's Winch 2*,
    10.69 - confirmed by comparing the Onshape instance count against
    what the GLB actually contained, not assumed). The fix that's stuck:
    export the Composite Part on its own to STEP, reimport that STEP
    file as a plain solid, then swap the assembly's instance of the old
    composite-linked part for the reimported one. The reimported node
    lands with real geometry and survives the main assembly export
    normally after that (10.75) - the earlier approach (exporting the
    composite as its own small supplementary GLB and loading it
    alongside the main one via model3d.extraParts) still works and is
    still in cwt3d.js, but is no longer the first thing to reach for now
    that the STEP-swap fix is known to work directly in the main file.
  - Export the finished, exploded, composite-free assembly as one GLB.
    Two export-setting gotchas that have bitten before and are worth
    checking for on every new file, not just assumed fixed: Onshape
    defaults to Draco compression, which needs a matching decoder on the
    app's own side (already vendored, 10.70 - not a per-export toggle
    the person needs to remember, just worth knowing why an
    un-flagged-off compressed export still loads fine) - and if the
    export instructions above ever change to allow it, watch for literal
    SPACES in real part names ("Part 6", "winch 2") surviving into the
    export: THREE.js's GLTFLoader silently sanitizes spaces to
    underscores on load, so a partMap copied verbatim from the raw file/
    instance names will silently match nothing (10.90 - use the
    underscore form in the partMap from the start).

11.3 What actually gets handed over
  The PDF extract (11.1a) plus the finished GLB (11.2) - and, when part
  names came out generic ("Part N", not renamed), an Onshape instance-
  tree screenshot showing each stack's own physical order, described
  top-to-bottom with at minimum the top and bottom part numbers stated
  explicitly (10.87/10.88's LTM 1650 CWT session is the reference
  example - the person named the top and bottom of each stack and
  confirmed the tree's own listing order was the physical order,
  which turned out to let the middle positions be inferred too). This
  screenshot is cheap for the person to produce and removes almost all
  the guesswork on the receiving end - worth including as standard
  practice going forward, not just when something's ambiguous.

11.4 What Claude does with it
  - PDF: extract the combination chart, verify every printed combo
    reconciles exactly against the subset-sum of a fixed component list
    (10.2's method) - a combo that doesn't reconcile is a transcription
    error worth re-checking before shipping, not a data quirk to shrug
    off.
  - GLB: confirmed node names always carry the real "Part N" (or
    equivalent) identifiers, whether or not Onshape ever let them be
    renamed to something descriptive (10.88) - the ORIGINAL "-low"
    LTM 1650 export was avoided specifically for lacking this. When an
    instance-tree screenshot is available (11.3), cross-check the
    person's stated order against the model's own real geometry
    (world position per axis, mesh bounding-box size/thickness) before
    trusting either source alone - this is what caught that the tree's
    listing order really did match physical stack order, and separately
    confirmed a plate the person flagged as possibly-duplicated (a <2>
    instance count in the Onshape tree) was genuinely a single physical
    part (10.88).
  - Build the partMap from confirmed identifiers only, matching the
    app's own existing component ids (a stack's two matching plates -
    left and right - map to the SAME id, same "×2" pairing the 2D
    diagram already uses, not two separate ids).
  - Ship with preExploded: true (11.2) and verify with an ACTUAL CLICK
    test before calling it done - not just "no console errors." A wrong
    node-name assumption can leave every namedPart wired to a single
    wrong id while the model still loads and renders with zero console
    errors (10.90 - this exact failure mode shipped once and only
    surfaced when the person tried actually using it). Click a plate,
    confirm the Total Counterweight number itself changes correctly,
    not just that something highlights.
  - Regression-check every OTHER crane's 3D CWT view after any change to
    cwt3d.js itself (not just the new crane) - several of the real bugs
    caught this way (10.75's Group-vs-Object3D gap, 10.89's perspective-
    depth fit bug, 10.90's Mesh-type gap) were found specifically by
    sweeping the existing fleet after a change made for one crane, not
    by testing that one crane in isolation.

To ask for this workflow to run on a new crane, the person doesn't need to
re-explain any of the above - just say which crane, and provide (or point
to) the two source documents from 11.1. Nothing more is needed for Claude to
follow this section directly.

================================================================================

12. SOURCING A BRAND-NEW CRANE: WHAT'S PUBLIC VS WHAT NEEDS THE FULL MANUAL
    [CONVERSATION]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Use this to triage what to ask the person for BEFORE starting on a crane
that isn't in the app at all yet, ahead of running section 9 (reeving) or
section 11 (CWT) - it's about which document covers which tab, not how to
process any one of them once in hand.

12.1 The public Liebherr product page (no login, e.g.
     liebherr.com/.../mobile-cranes/ltm-mobile-cranes/ltm-<model>) has a
     Downloads tab with a "Technical Data - Mobile crane <model> [m/t]" PDF
     - the same document TYPE as an OEM dimension sheet (the "Maße" page
     style - overall dimensions, axle loads, outrigger geometry). This
     alone is enough for:
       - Outrigger Support Positioning (P1-P4 figures, always read at
         100% extension - the person's own standing rule, confirmed
         independent of any specific crane).
       - Rope Retentioning (drum length/pull are typically in this sheet
         too).
     This PDF was NOT verified by directly fetching the live page in this
     conversation - WebFetch 404'd on it (Liebherr's site likely blocks or
     requires JS rendering this tool can't do), so what it contains was
     taken from the document TYPE's established pattern, not a live check
     of that specific page. Try fetching it directly when this actually
     comes up; if it 404s again, ask the person for the PDF itself rather
     than assuming the pattern holds.

12.2 What the public page does NOT reliably cover, confirmed by the
     person directly rather than assumed:
       - CWT Combinations: the product page's "ballast systems" section
         may show an exploded-view image (useful for the 2D diagram's own
         geometry/plate layout, same role as the artwork described in
         10.4), but not the actual combination-validity table - which
         specific plate combos are OEM-approved, at what weight. That
         table has come from the full Operator Manual's own
         "Counterweight" chapter for every crane in this app so far (see
         11.1a) - a public summary sheet isn't the same document.
       - Reeving Plan: the EINSCHERPLAN diagrams (section 9's own source)
         are an Operator Manual document too, not something the public
         product page carries.
       - 3D CAD models / GLB exports: never public - always the person's
         own Onshape work (section 11.2), regardless of crane.

12.3 Practical upshot for a brand-new crane
     Ask for the Operator Manual PDF (unlocked, per 11.1a) from the start,
     alongside/instead of just a product-page link - don't wait to
     discover the CWT/reeving gap after already starting on the parts the
     public page DOES cover. The public Technical Data sheet is still
     worth having even so: it can very likely be worked from independently
     for the Outrigger and Rope Retentioning tabs while the fuller manual
     extraction (CWT, reeving) happens in parallel, rather than gating
     everything on one document.

================================================================================

13. DRIVING W/ EQUIPMENT: FINDING THE OUTRIGGER EXTENSION % STAGES
    [CONVERSATION]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
For the Driving w/ Equipment tab's own request: showing each chart row's
required outrigger extension as a percentage (100/75/50/25/0) alongside the
raw length, matching the crane's actual PINNED support-base positions - the
ONLY positions Liebherr outriggers can physically be operated at (person's
own confirmed domain fact, not an assumption).

13.1 Best source: the manual has a table for this directly - check here
     FIRST
  Confirmed live on LTM 1160-5.2's operator manual, exact navigation path
  (from the PDF's own contents panel, not guessed): Operating Instructions
  -> 1 Description of Crane -> 1.03 Technical Data -> 3 Support ->
  3.1 Support Base. The table there ("Support base" / "Extension condition
  [c1 to c4]" / "Length [a]" / "Width [b]") gives the percentage DIRECTLY,
  already labeled - no derivation, no interpolation, no risk of confusing
  it with an unrelated dimension. LTM 1160-5.2's confirmed values:
    0%   ->  9.18m x 2.45m
    50%  ->  9.18m x 5.38m
    75%  ->  9.18m x 6.84m
    100% ->  9.18m x 8.30m
  Only 4 stages exist for this crane (no 25%) - confirmed two independent
  ways: this table, AND a separate place elsewhere in the same manual the
  person found listing only 100/75/50/0. Don't assume every crane has all
  five round-number stages; read what's actually there. Note Length [a]
  stays fixed at 9.18m across every stage here - only Width [b] changes
  with extension - worth checking whether that holds for every crane or
  is specific to this one's beam layout (e.g. a crane with front AND rear
  telescoping beams, not just left/right, could vary both dimensions).
  "1.03 Technical Data" (with this same "3 Support" numbering) is
  reasonably likely to be a STANDARD chapter/section structure across
  this manual series, not a one-off - worth checking this exact path
  first on any future crane's manual before falling back to 13.2.

13.2 Fallback: derive it from the general dimension sheet if 13.1's table
     isn't present
  The overall min-max range printed on the crane's dimension sheet (e.g.
  "2446-8306") gives the 0% and 100% endpoints directly. Intermediate
  values printed on the SAME dimension stack can be checked against
  simple linear interpolation - value = min + (max-min) x fraction -
  BEFORE trusting them as a stage: confirmed working (exact match) for
  LTM 1160-5.2's own 50%/75% figures cross-checked this way, matching
  13.1's table to within a few mm (rounding only).
  Real trap hit and caught during this exact exercise: a THIRD number in
  the same dimension stack (4270) did NOT fit the linear pattern as 25%
  (expected ~3911) - turned out to be the width of the CWT slabs, an
  entirely unrelated dimension that happened to sit in the same visual
  area of the drawing. A number that doesn't fit the linear check is a
  signal to ask what it actually is, not to force it into the pattern
  or discard the check. This is exactly why 13.1 (a table that states
  the percentage outright) is the preferred source whenever it exists -
  this derivation method is a fallback, not the first move.

13.3 Confirmed fleet-wide table (all 7 relevant cranes checked)
  Sourced from each crane's own operator manual via the 13.1 navigation
  path ("1.03 Technical Data -> 3 Support -> 3.1 Support Base"), confirmed
  standard across the whole LTM series - present and at that exact path
  in every LTM manual checked. Width [b] at each extension %, Length [a]
  fixed per crane (varies by crane, doesn't vary with extension %) unless
  noted otherwise:

    Crane          0%      25%     50%     75%     100%
    LTM 1110-5.1   2.4m    -       *5.0m   *7.0m   *7.0/8.3m
    LTM 1130-5.1   2.40m   -       5.00m   -       7.50m
    LTM 1160-5.2   2.45m   -       5.38m   6.84m   8.30m
    LTM 1250-5.1   2.70m   -       5.50m   6.90m   8.30m
    LTM 1300-6.2   2.68m   -       5.60m   7.06m   8.53m
    LTM 1650-8.1   2.62m   4.36m   6.10m   7.84m   9.58m

  LTM 1130-5.1 and 1160-5.2 only have 3-4 stages, not 5 - don't assume
  every crane has 25% and 75%; read what the table actually lists.

  * LTM 1110-5.1 is NOT symmetric front/rear like the rest of the fleet -
  its own manual table gives named positions instead of one clean %
  column, because front and rear support width differ at the same
  nominal stage:
    Retracted     -> rear 0%  / front 0%,   both 2.4m
    Reduced       -> rear 50% / front 55%,  rear 5.4m / front 5.0m
    Reduced wide  -> rear 75% / front 100%, rear 6.85m / front 7.00m
    Wide          -> rear 100%/ front 100%, rear 8.30m / front 7.0m
    Variable      -> continuously adjustable 0-100% both sides
  RESOLVED: the app's existing Driving w/ Equipment chart data for this
  crane (DRIVING_EQ_DATA['1110']) was sourced from a genuinely separate
  document - a per-crane "Driving with the equipment in place" book
  (chapter 15.05, own BAL number, own folder:
  Driving_with_the_equipment_in_place/, sibling to Operating_instructions_
  Crane/, NOT inside the main operator manual PDF at all). Downloaded and
  scanned it directly: every chart in that book's 122 pages uses a
  "Support width (min.B)" column with only two values across the entire
  crane, 2.45m and 5.00m - never 5.4m. Since 5.00m matches the Technical
  Data table's FRONT designation exactly (not the rear's 5.40m), the
  chart data already reflects the front-axle number, so it maps cleanly
  to 55%, not 50%. app's OUTRIGGER_STAGE_TABLE['1110'] = [[0,2.40],
  [55,5.00]] - no front/rear split needed after all, because the source
  chart itself never varies the number by boom direction, only the
  Technical Data summary table does.
  General lesson: when a crane's own "Support Base" table (13.1) looks
  asymmetric or ambiguous, check whether the SAME manual has a separate
  "Driving with the equipment in place" book/chapter before assuming the
  ambiguity carries through to the Driving w/ Equipment feature - the
  driving chart may already have picked a single number for you.

  LTR 1220 was checked and confirmed OUT OF SCOPE for this feature
  entirely, not a data gap: it's a crawler crane, riding on fixed-width
  crawler tracks rather than extendable outrigger beams. Its manual has
  no "Support Base" chapter at all - the closest analog is a separate
  "1.03.10 Outrigger pads" section, which covers ground-bearing mats for
  load distribution, an unrelated concept. Confirmed directly by the
  person too ("it uses tracks not outriggers") - don't go looking for a
  support-base % table on this crane again.

13.4 Renamed the app's outrigger corners from P1-P4 to C1-C4 to match
     Liebherr's own numbering
  The app's outrigger-pad code (cadFleetData, the Outrigger Pad Positions
  tab's toggles) had its own P1-P4 naming from before this session,
  already correctly assigned to physical corners: P1=Rear Right,
  P2=Front Right, P3=Front Left, P4=Rear Left. Checked this against
  Liebherr's own numbering, found directly in LTM 1130-5.1's manual,
  chapter 3.05 "Crane at the job site", section "1.2 Numbering of
  support" (confirmed as standard chapter numbering, same place other
  cranes' manuals define it): "1 Support right rear / 2 Support right
  front / 3 Support left front / 4 Support left rear" - this is the same
  numbering shown on the crane's own BTT/LICCON display. It matches the
  app's existing P1-P4 assignment exactly, corner for corner, which is
  also why the Technical Data "3.1 Support Base" table's "Extension
  condition [c1 to c4]" column groups c1/c4 as "Rear" and c2/c3 as
  "Front" (see 13.3) - same underlying numbering, just Liebherr's own
  "c" shorthand for it in that chapter. Because the assignment was
  already identical, this was a pure relabel (P1->C1, P2->C2, P3->C3,
  P4->C4: object keys P1_RR/P2_FR/P3_FL/P4_RL -> C1_RR/C2_FR/C3_FL/
  C4_RL across all 7 cranes in cadFleetData, the "name" field inside
  each, the two craneObj.P*_* usages in syncCarrier3DOutriggers/
  calcCAD, and the 8 checkbox labels on the Outrigger Pad Positions /
  3D Carrier Preview toolbar) - not a remap, no risk of assigning the
  wrong physical corner. If a future crane's numbering ever turns out
  to run the other rotation direction (some manufacturers do
  front-first), verify against that crane's own "Numbering of support"
  section before assuming this same C1=RR/C2=FR/C3=FL/C4=RL pattern
  holds - it's confirmed standard across the LTM series checked so far,
  not assumed universal.

13.5 LTM 1110-5.1's Driving w/ Equipment display now shows both sides
     of its asymmetric "Reduced" stage
  Per 13.3's front/rear resolution, the Driving w/ Equipment chart's
  5.00m figure for LTM 1110-5.1 is the C2/C3 (front) reading at 55% -
  C1/C4 (rear) sits at 50%/5.40m for that same physical pinned position,
  which the chart's own single "Support width (min.B)" column never
  states. A bare "(55% extension)" label would wrongly read as if all
  four corners were at 55%. Fixed by adding OUTRIGGER_ASYMMETRIC_PAIR
  (keyed by crane + the chart's own quoted width) so the render checks
  for a known asymmetric stage before falling back to the plain
  percentage - when present, shows both readings explicitly: "5.00 m -
  C2/C3 (front): 55% · C1/C4 (rear): 50%, 5.40 m". Only 1110 has an
  entry today, since it's the only crane whose own Support Base table
  splits front/rear at all (13.3) - every other crane in the fleet
  keeps the plain single-percentage line.

13.6 Follow-up: 13.4's P->C rename missed the Visual Site Plan canvas +
     its results table
  13.4 renamed the underlying data (cadFleetData keys, checkbox labels)
  but missed two places that build their OWN "P${...}" label text from
  the numeric item.id rather than reading the renamed object keys: the
  Visual Site Plan canvas (drawCADCanvas - the on-canvas leg labels,
  the tap-for-info box's id/type strings, and the dashed "if moved"
  ghost target label) and the "All Four Legs - Reference Frames" table
  underneath it (both its zero-shift and shifted-state row templates).
  Person caught this directly ("the remaining p1-p4 being used to
  label the out riggers"). Fixed all 8 sites (7 in drawCADCanvas/
  clickableTargets, 1 in the results table). Lesson: a rename pass
  needs to search for the LITERAL STRING PATTERN the display code
  builds from (here, the template `P${o.id}` / `P${item.id}`), not just
  the data structure's own field/key names - display code that
  reconstructs a label from a raw id rather than reading a stored name
  string won't show up in a search for the old key names.

13.7 Added LTM 1110-5.1's remaining T-only Driving w/ Equipment boom
     configs (T-22.6, T-33.8) under Advanced
  Person asked for "all T boom 445/525 tire Driving with equipment
  charts, non-fully-retracted ones under Advanced" - excluding TK,
  TVK, TNZK, TK-2.9 (folding-jib/lattice variants) entirely, plain
  telescopic "T" boom only.
  Source: the same crane's separate "Driving with the equipment in
  place" book (chapter 15.05, see 13.6's discovery of this being a
  genuinely separate document/folder from the main operator manual),
  re-downloaded and read directly rather than assumed from the table
  of contents alone.
  Mapped the book's 24-section table of contents against actual page
  content (chapter-page-N in the footer maps to PDF page N+27 in this
  specific file) and found only 3 sections are plain "T" (not
  TK/TVK/TNZK/TK-2.9):
    - Sections 1/14/15: T-11.5 (0/0/0/0/0/0) - FULLY RETRACTED. Section
      1 ("Description") is only 2 illustrative example rows, not a
      real chart; sections 14/15 are the actual full front/rear chart
      and were confirmed to be the exact source the app's existing
      baseline boomConfig was already built from (23/78 rows matched
      exactly) - nothing new to add here.
    - Sections 2/3: T-22.6 (46/46/46/0/0/0) - not fully retracted.
    - Sections 4/5: T-33.8 (46/46/46/46/46/46) - not fully retracted.
  Added T-22.6 and T-33.8 as new boomConfig entries with advanced:
  true (68/48 and 63/22 front/rear rows respectively), parsed
  programmatically from the extracted PDF text (grouped into 6-field
  row chunks: weight/hookBlock/boomAngle/supportWidth/axleA/axleB)
  rather than transcribed by hand, given the row counts involved -
  verified row-count parity (raw line count divisible by 6, no
  leftover fields) before writing anything into the app.
  Tire clarification: every page in all three T-only sections lists
  ALL three tire sizes (385/95, 445/95, 525/80 R25) against the SAME
  single data table - the tables are tire-agnostic in this specific
  book, they don't split into a separate 445/525-only table the way
  the person's framing implied. This differs from how the OTHER
  cranes' sourceNotes are worded ("chapter 15.05 Charts for 445/95
  R25 and 525/80 R25 tires") - 445/525 there likely reflects the
  fleet's actual fitted tires (525/80 R25 confirmed elsewhere as the
  fleet standard, see the CAD tab's footprint width sourcing) rather
  than a genuine per-tire chart split in every crane's own book. Not
  something to assume holds for every future crane either way -
  check each book directly.

13.8 Fleet-wide check: 1110 was the only gap, everyone else already
     complete
  After 13.7, checked every other crane's own "Driving with the
  equipment in place" book directly (not assumed) for the same thing -
  plain T-boom (excluding TK/TNZK/TVK/TVNZK/TS/TNZF-jib variants),
  not-fully-retracted configs missing from the app. Result: nothing
  else needed adding anywhere in the fleet.
    - LTM 1130-5.1: only ONE T-boom length exists in the entire book
      (T-12.7, already fully retracted) - already in the app.
    - LTM 1300-6.2: all three plain-T sections (T-14.7 front, T-14.7
      rear, T-19.6 front-only) already in the app, verified row-for-
      row exact against the 445/525-tire sub-chapter.
    - LTM 1650-8.1: person explicitly scoped this to "T3 only" (not
      the dozen-plus T3Y/T3N/T3NH/T3YVEN guying-variant sections this
      crane's huge 1213-page book also has for the SAME T-16.7 boom
      length) - both T3 sections (with/without winch 2) already in the
      app, verified exact.
    - LTM 1160-5.2 / LTM 1250-5.1: both use "Outer radius of
      counterweight" labeling instead of boom-length notation because
      their own "T operation" section is a SINGLE boom length
      (T-13.1) with counterweight-radius as the only variable - both
      radius variants already in the app, verified exact.
  LRT 1100-2.1 explicitly excluded from this whole feature, per the
  person: it's a rough terrain crane that generally always drives WITH
  its counterweight fitted - the "driving with the equipment in place"
  concept this feature is built around doesn't apply to it the way it
  does to the LTM (mobile crane) fleet. Not a data gap - don't go
  looking for a Driving w/ Equipment book for this crane.
