14. LOAD CHART FINDER: T-BOOM CAPACITY LOOKUP (LTM 1110-5.1)  [CONVERSATION]
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
New tab: person gives a working radius and a target load, the tool finds
which boom-length/telescoping configurations can lift it, ranked by
margin (top 5), with the exact extension % breakdown (e.g. "0/0/46/0/0/0")
needed to set it up. Scope, per the person's explicit request: T-boom
only (no lattice/folding jib), outriggers fully deployed, 360° slew, with
counterweight and wind-speed as selectable variables. This is a
fundamentally bigger undertaking than the Driving w/ Equipment charts
(section 13) - load charts are the crane's actual lifting-capacity
tables, not a single driving condition, and the source document turned
out to be enormous.

14.1 Source document reality check - 12,292 pages, not a normal manual
  The crane's "Load chart manual" folder has 4 PDFs, one per boom type:
  a "_T" suffix file (plain telescopic, what's needed), "_TK" and "_TVK"
  (jib variants, out of scope), plus an "_R" file (not investigated).
  The "_T" file is 46MB and, per PyMuPDF, 12,292 pages - not a
  hand-authored manual but a LICCON-software-generated exhaustive set,
  one page-group per valid crane configuration. Filtering to exactly
  what was asked for (fully-deployed support base + 360° + plain T, not
  T~ or T*) still leaves 1,532 pages.
  Two symbols the person flagged in advance as things to watch for,
  confirmed via the manual's own "40.62 Operating modes" chapter:
    T   = plain Telescopic boom - the real lifting mode, what's wanted.
    T~  = "Maintenance operation" - NOT a lifting chart at all, used
          only for servicing/lubricating the boom. Excluded.
    T*  = "Telescopic boom with auxiliary device" - requires a special
          rigging accessory assembled on the crane, not the standard
          configuration. Excluded.
  First impression was that this was scanned/image-only (page.get_text()
  on early pages returned near-nothing) - turned out that was just the
  cover/legend pages using embedded JPEGs; the actual data pages (from
  around page 50 onward) have real, extractable text. Confirmed by
  checking get_images()/get_text() on a spread of pages before assuming
  either way.
  Each page is ONE fixed combination of counterweight + wind-speed
  bracket (confirmed via the footer icons: a "0.0t"-style counterweight
  badge and an "11.2 m/s"-style wind badge appear on every page), and a
  full page is one page of MANY needed for that combo (~13 pages each,
  continuing to longer boom lengths across pages, not always in strictly
  ascending order - don't assume page order implies boom-length order,
  just union all columns found across a combo's page range). Full-
  document scan found 39 distinct counterweight levels (0.0t to 29.0t,
  following the crane's own plate-combination steps, not round numbers -
  matches the same weight steps already seen in the Driving w/ Equipment
  data) x 3 wind brackets (11.2 / 13.4 / 15.6 m/s) = 117 combos, all
  present (39 x 3 = 117 exactly, no gaps).

14.2 Extraction method - PyMuPDF find_tables(), not naive text order
  Raw page.get_text() comes out in scrambled reading order on these
  pages (confirmed by inspection - numbers appear in a sequence that
  doesn't match the visual grid at all). PyMuPDF's page.find_tables()
  reconstructs the actual grid structure directly and reliably once two
  real bugs in the first attempt were found and fixed:
    - t.cells (the table's flat per-cell bbox list) is NOT simple
      row-major (row*col_count + col) indexing - it has merges/gaps
      (404 entries for a 28x15=420 grid). The correct API is
      t.rows[r].cells[c], which returns properly ordered bboxes.
      Using the wrong indexing silently paired every capacity value
      with the WRONG cell's bbox, corrupting every radius assignment
      it touched - caught by comparing extracted values against a
      rendered image of the same page, not just checking the code ran.
    - The 6 "telescopic section extension %" rows were being detected
      by matching a column-0 label starting with "1" and containing
      "\n2" - which false-matched the "18.0\n20.0" RADIUS row (also
      starts with "1", also contains "\n2" via its second line "20.0").
      Fixed by matching on the "%" character instead, which only ever
      appears in the real extension-row label ("1\n2\n3\n4\n5\n% 6").
  find_tables() also merges pairs of adjacent radius rows into one
  detected table row (e.g. "3.0"/"3.5" show as one cell with a
  newline-joined pair of values) - can't just split-and-zip pairwise,
  because a column's data sometimes only covers ONE of the two radii
  (found a real case: the 26.4m boom column had a capacity at r=3.5 but
  nothing at r=3.0, and naive pairwise splitting would have assigned
  that lone value to the wrong radius). Fixed properly by using each
  detected cell's own bbox to re-query page.get_text("words", clip=bbox)
  and matching each value's actual y-coordinate against the cell's own
  vertical midpoint, rather than assuming order - verified this exact
  edge case by checking the value's real y0 against the two candidate
  radius label's y0 directly (matched the lower one, i.e. r=3.5, exactly
  as the rendered page showed).
  Verification method throughout: render the actual PDF page to a PNG
  (page.get_pixmap(dpi=200)) and compare every extracted number against
  it directly, not just spot-checking a few values against expectation.
  Did this twice, on two different (counterweight, wind) combos,
  including checking a mid-range page (page 6 of 13) to confirm
  multi-page continuation extracts cleanly too - both matched the
  rendered image exactly, including the sparse-cell edge case, before
  running the full extraction.

14.3 Final extraction numbers
  1,532 pages processed, 0 errors. 117 (cwt, wind) combos, 20,044
  distinct boom-length/extension-config columns, 364,647 individual
  radius->capacity data points. Compacted (numeric radius keys instead
  of string dict keys, [radius,capacity] pairs instead of nested
  objects, extension values split into [percent, isPinned] pairs) to
  5.58MB JSON, stored at loadchart/ltm1110-T-fulldeploy.json - each
  combo also carries its own chart code/reference (e.g. "0195" /
  "T197.001.12076", confirmed constant across all pages of one combo)
  so a result can be cross-checked against the physical/PDF chart.

14.4 Why this data is NOT inline in index.html like everything else
  Every other tab's data (COUNTERWEIGHT_DATA, DRIVING_EQ_DATA, etc.) is
  baked directly into index.html as JS. At 5.58MB for a single crane's
  single scope, doing that here would bloat every page load for a tab
  most visits never open. Fetched on demand instead - same lazy-load
  pattern already used for reeving diagrams (ensureReeveManifestLoaded):
  fetch('./loadchart/<file>.json'), cached in a module-level variable
  after first load, picked up by the service worker's normal
  CONTENT_CACHE fetch-and-cache-on-demand behavior with no sw.js changes
  needed (APP_SHELL precaching is the wrong tool here - CONTENT_CACHE's
  existing behavior already does exactly the "keep it once fetched"
  guarantee wanted).

14.5 Radius rounding - round UP, never interpolate, with a visible notice
  Chart radii are discrete, non-uniform steps (0.5m from 3-5m, 1m from
  5-16m, 2m from 16-40m) - not every possible radius is tabulated.
  Person's explicit call when asked: always round the requested radius
  UP to the next tabulated row (never interpolate), because that can
  only ever understate capacity relative to what was actually asked
  for, never overstate it - the safe direction for safety-critical data.
  Also explicitly asked for a visible notice whenever this rounding
  happens, so it's never silent - implemented as a dismissable-looking
  but always-shown banner stating both the requested and actual radius
  and why. loadChartRoundUpRadius() implements this as a simple ascending
  scan (radii list is stored pre-sorted) - first tabulated value >=
  requested wins.

14.6 Two counterweight modes
  "I have this fitted" (default/manual): person picks their actual
  counterweight from the 39 real levels this crane can build, results
  are scoped to exactly that level only.
  "Find minimum needed" (advanced, person's own request - "recommend
  the minimum cwt required to hit the highest capacity"): searches the
  39 levels ascending and stops at the FIRST (lowest) one where at
  least one config clears the target load at the rounded radius,
  reporting that level explicitly ("Minimum counterweight needed to hit
  this target: X t") alongside the same top-5-by-margin display. Not
  "the CWT that gives the single highest capacity overall" - scoped to
  the person's actual radius+target query, same as manual mode, just
  without them having to pick a starting counterweight and iterate by
  hand.
  Verified the browser-side query logic against the source JSON
  directly (not just by eyeballing the UI) - looked up the exact same
  (boom=22.6m, cwt=0.8t, wind=11.2m/s, r=10m) cell in the raw extracted
  data via a separate script and confirmed it matched the app's own
  "Option 1" result (16.9t) exactly before considering this done.

14.7 Site capacity rule (90%) and rigging weight deduction
  Two follow-up requests, both about NOT taking the printed chart figure
  at face value:
  1. Mine sites the person works on commonly cap usable capacity at 90%
     of the chart rating, not 100% (100% is where the LICCON overload
     alarms trigger). Person's own worked example, checked before
     building: chart says 2.4t at some radius, need to lift 2t, 90% of
     2.4t = 2.16t, which still clears 2t - math confirmed correct
     (2.4 x 0.9 = 2.16) before implementing. "Site Capacity Rule" toggle
     (100%/90%) multiplies the raw chart capacity before it's compared
     against the target - never interpolated or hidden, both the raw
     chart figure AND the derated "usable capacity" are shown in each
     result when the 90% rule is on, so the OEM number stays visible
     for cross-checking alongside the derated one actually being used.
  2. Rigging weight: a hook block (and a second block if winch 2 is
     rigged) hangs static off the boom tip before the actual load is
     even attached, and that weight counts against the chart's capacity
     too. This isn't just site practice - the OEM load chart manual's
     own "40.02 Basic information" section states it explicitly ("The
     weights of the load lifting equipment and the fastening equipment
     must be deducted"), confirmed by re-reading that exact section
     before telling the person this was correct. Implemented as:
     target load + block weight(s) = "effective target" the crane must
     actually clear, checked against the (possibly-derated) chart
     capacity. Mathematically identical to "deduct rigging weight from
     capacity instead" - this framing was chosen so the number the
     person types into "Target Load" always means exactly what they
     typed, with the rigging math shown as an explicit, separate line
     above the results rather than silently folded into either number.
  Block weights are NOT a new hardcoded list - reused FLEET_BLOCK_SPEC
  (same fleet block catalog the Minimum Hook Block Weights tab already
  draws its recommendations from), so there's one source of truth for
  this crane's actual block fleet rather than a second, potentially
  drifting copy. Base block weight only (no auxiliary ballast sub-
  selection, unlike that tab's own aux weight lists) - this is about
  static rigging weight, not hook-block sizing.
  Winch 2's block is a second, independently-selected block from the
  same fleet list, shown only when the "Winch 2 fitted" checkbox is on,
  and its weight is simply added to the primary block's - both blocks
  are static weight on the boom tip if both are rigged, regardless of
  which one is actively hoisting.
  Verified every combination by hand before shipping: baseline (no
  derate, no rigging) 16.90t capacity / 11.24t effective target (10t +
  1.24t block) = 5.66t margin; with 90% derate, usable capacity
  16.90 x 0.9 = 15.21t, margin 15.21 - 11.24 = 3.97t; swapping to a
  lighter 0.90t block dropped the effective target to 10.90t, margin
  6.00t; adding winch 2 + a 0.45t second block brought rigging to
  1.35t, effective target 11.35t, margin 5.55t - all four recomputed by
  hand and matched the rendered UI exactly, zero console errors.

14.8 HOTFIX: whole-number counterweights (0.0/11.0/17.0/29.0t) silently
     returned "no configuration" even when the data had a good match
  Person hit this directly - r=26m, 2t target, 29.0t counterweight (the
  crane's actual max) returned no results at all, despite the raw data
  having a config good for 8.5t at that exact cell (verified directly
  against the JSON before assuming it was a UI bug rather than a
  genuine "physically can't be done" result).
  Root cause: the combo lookup built its key with a plain template
  literal, `${cwt}|${wind}`. The JSON's own combo keys were written by
  Python as str(float) - e.g. "29.0", "11.0", "0.0" - always with the
  decimal point, because that's how compact_data.py originally split
  and rejoined the classify-pass keys. JS's default Number->string
  coercion drops the trailing ".0" for whole numbers (String(29) ===
  "29", not "29.0"), so the browser-built key "29|11.2 m/s" silently
  missed the real "29.0|11.2 m/s" entry - `if (!combo) continue` just
  skipped it with no error, indistinguishable from a genuine "physically
  impossible" result. Every fractional counterweight (0.8, 3.3, 6.9,
  etc.) coincidentally worked fine, because JS's coercion of THOSE
  numbers happens to match Python's - the bug only ever hit whole-number
  levels: 0.0, 11.0, 17.0, 29.0 out of the 39 total.
  Fixed by using cwt.toFixed(1) to build the key instead of bare string
  interpolation - matches the data's one-decimal-place format for every
  value in the set, not just the ones that happened to coincide.
  Verified against the person's exact reported inputs (r=26m, 2t,
  29.0t CWT, 90% rule, 63t block + winch 2 + default second block)
  before and after the fix - "no configuration" before, 5 real results
  after, top one at 3.91t margin, matching the raw-data cross-check
  (8.5t chart capacity x 0.9 - 3.74t effective target = 3.91t) exactly.
  Also re-verified auto (find-minimum-CWT) mode separately, since it
  iterates the same whole-number levels internally - correctly skips
  0.0t and lands on 6.9t as the minimum for a similar query, 0 console
  errors either way.
  Lesson: never build a data lookup key from a bare template literal
  across a language boundary (Python-written JSON, JS-read at runtime)
  without checking that both sides format numbers identically - a
  silent miss (falls into the same code path as "genuinely no match")
  is far more dangerous here than a thrown error would have been, since
  it's indistinguishable from a correct negative result without
  independently checking the source data.

14.9 Excluded live-telescoping configs from the recommendation list
  Person explained a "-" on a telescopic section (e.g. an extension
  breakdown like "0+/0+/0+/-100/0+") means that section may telescope
  UNDER LOAD up to that %, not that it must be pinned there. These are
  niche charts for obstacle-avoidance work - telescope in past an
  obstruction, place the load, telescope back out while still under
  load - a specialised rigging technique, not a normal boom
  configuration a crew should be steered toward by default. This
  confirms and extends 13.6/13.7's own "+" = must be pinned, "-" =
  can telescope under load" reading of the OEM notation - already
  extracted and stored per-section (isPinned true/false in each
  column's ext array) since section 14's original build, just not yet
  filtered out of the results shown.
  Per the person's explicit request, any column with at least one "-"
  section is now excluded from ALL load chart finder results (manual
  and auto/find-minimum-CWT mode alike) - not hidden behind a toggle,
  removed outright, matching how T~/T* operating modes are already
  fully out of scope for this feature (see 14.1) rather than offered
  as an opt-in. Swept both modes across several (radius,
  weight, counterweight) combinations, including the whole-number
  counterweight values 14.8 just fixed, confirmed zero "-" markers
  survive into any displayed result and each query still returns a
  full set of options (the filter isn't so aggressive it starves
  results) - 0 console errors.

================================================================================

15. LOAD CHART FINDER: MULTI-CRANE EXPANSION  [CONVERSATION]

Person asked for the same Load Chart Finder feature (section 14) extended
to the rest of the fleet - LRT 1100-2.1, LTM 1130-5.1, LTM 1160-5.2,
LTM 1250-5.1, LTM 1300-6.2, then LTM 1650-8.1 last, in that order. 1160/
1250/1650 additionally need a ballast (counterweight tray) radius
selector, and 1650's auto/find-minimum-CWT mode needs that selector
exposed too, not just manual mode. This section covers LRT 1100; later
cranes get their own subsections as they're built.

15.1 Source manuals for this expansion do NOT come from re-uploads
  The person's answer to "where are the source PDFs" was "a link/location
  you already have access to" - this is the person's own Nextcloud share
  (local.idull.au, public share token in the URL, WebDAV endpoint
  /public.php/dav/files/<token>/), the SAME place section 14's 1110
  "_T" file was originally pulled from mid-conversation (a curl -u
  "<token>:" against a `local.idull.au/.../Load_chart_manual/*_T.pdf`
  path, found by grepping this session's own transcript for the earlier
  download command - the file itself doesn't persist in the container
  between turns, only the fact that it was fetched from there does).
  Every crane folder follows the same layout:
    Liebherr cranes/<MODEL>/[OPERATIONS MANUAL - Serial # NNNNNN/[<serial>
    _<model>_v100_en/]]Load_chart_manual/*.pdf
  (the extra "OPERATIONS MANUAL - Serial #.../<serial>_..._v100_en/"
  nesting level is present for some cranes and not others - LTM 1160/1650
  skip straight to Load_chart_manual, LRT 1100/1130/1250/1300 have it -
  don't assume a fixed depth, PROPFIND (Depth: 1) each level and follow
  what's actually there). Confirmed via WebDAV PROPFIND that all six
  requested cranes have a Load_chart_manual folder before starting any
  extraction work.
  Each Load_chart_manual folder holds one PDF per BOOM MODE (a "_T" file
  = plain telescopic, wanted; "_TK"/"_TVK"/etc = jib variants, out of
  scope; a "tlt-R_..." file with no mode suffix = a different chapter,
  not investigated, same as 14.1's "_R file"). Sizes vary hugely by
  crane - LRT 1100 has one 3.2MB file with no mode suffix at all (this
  crane's boom system only has one main-boom type, "T", so there's
  nothing to disambiguate - confirmed via its own 40.60/40.62 chapters,
  15.2 below); LTM 1650's "_T3" file is 8.9MB but sits next to sibling
  files up to 204MB for other T3-variant modes (T3N, T3F, etc) - the
  person confirmed T3 (not T3Y/T3N/T3F/...) is the one wanted before any
  of those larger files were downloaded.

15.2 LRT 1100-2.1 - much smaller scope than 1110, and two behaviors flip
  184 pages total, ONE file (no boom-mode suffix needed - chapter 40.60
  "Short description of component groups" confirms this crane's only
  main-boom type is "T", auxiliary boom type "K" for an optional folding
  jib; chapter 40.62 confirms the only main-boom operating mode is
  literally "T", no T~/T* equivalents to exclude). Chapter 40.90 "Load
  charts" (data pages) runs pages ~111-183.
  Support base ALSO varies on this crane (7.30m / 5.50m / 2.69m widths,
  all at a fixed 7.48m other dimension) - the same "fully deployed
  outriggers" scope from 14.1 applies here as "widest support base"
  (7.30m); the narrower two are out of scope, same reasoning as excluding
  T~/T*. Combined with 3 counterweight levels (0.0/8.0/14.0t) and fixed
  360 degree slew, in-scope T-mode data is only 3 combos (chart refs
  T273.00011/00012/00013), 2 pages each - about 40x smaller than 1110's
  117-combo, 1532-page job. Confirmed with a full-page classification
  pass before extracting anything, not assumed from the folder listing.
  Two structural differences from 1110, both confirmed against this
  crane's OWN "40.65 Description of the load chart" icon-legend chapter
  before writing any extraction logic - do not assume 14's conventions
  transfer to a new crane's manual without re-reading its own legend:
    a) Wind speed is per-COLUMN here, not a page-level bracket. Every
       boom-length/extension config has its own "maximum permissible
       wind speed" printed in the same table (a "m/s" row under the four
       extension-% rows), varying column to column on the same page
       (e.g. 14.3/14.3/12.8/12.8/11.1/... across one page's 12 columns).
       There is no fixed set of wind brackets to toggle between for this
       crane, so this data's meta.windMode = 'column' (vs 1110's
       implicit 'page' - the field defaults to 'page' when absent so
       1110's existing JSON needed no changes). index.html branches on
       this: 'column' mode hides the wind toggle entirely, shows an
       explanatory note instead, and displays each result's own wind
       rating as an informational line rather than filtering on it.
    b) The pin/live-telescope notation is INVERTED from 1110's. This
       crane's own legend states it explicitly (40.65, "Extension
       condition of telescopic sections"): "+" suffix = section MUST be
       pinned (normal case); NO suffix at all (a bare number, e.g. plain
       "50") = section must NOT be pinned, i.e. live-telescope-under-
       load - the exact concept 14.9 excludes, just flagged with the
       opposite character. Person's call after seeing this: "treat all
       lrt1100 as pinned i dont think there is any true unpinned
       sections" - so extraction records isPinned=true unconditionally
       for every LRT 1100 section regardless of the raw +/bare symbol,
       rather than reading and filtering on it. This means 14.9's
       exclusion filter (ext.some(e => e && e[1] === false)) naturally
       never fires for this crane's data without needing a code branch -
       it only ever produces true.
  Also found two footnote codes (107 = loads above 69.9t need an
  auxiliary block, 108 = loads above 80.4t need an auxiliary device) that
  appear on every T-mode page's top-left corner as a standing warning
  icon. Read literally, this is a rigging/hook-capacity constraint
  ("above X tonnes you need a bigger block"), not a boom-configuration
  restriction like T*'s "requires a special rigging accessory" (14.1) -
  chose NOT to exclude on this basis, unlike T~/T*/live-telescope. Both
  thresholds are well above what this crane's realistic lift range
  covers, so this wasn't pursued further (no per-cell threshold check
  wired in) - flagged to the person as a judgement call rather than
  silently decided either way.

15.3 Extraction: find_tables() worked directly, one bug found and fixed
  Unlike 1110, page.get_text(sort=True) alone produces a fully readable,
  correctly-ordered table for this crane's PDFs (no scrambling) - but
  find_tables() was still used for the actual extraction since it
  handles the sparse-cell column alignment correctly without needing
  1110's bbox/y-position fallback machinery (that fallback code was kept
  in the script as a defensive path, just never triggered on this
  crane's data - confirmed by not seeing it fire during extraction).
  One real bug, caught before it reached the shipped JSON: the four
  extension-% rows plus the wind-speed row (labelled "1"/"2"/"3"/"4"/
  "m/s" in the chart's own left-hand column) are FIVE separate rows in
  find_tables()'s output, each with its own per-column values - but only
  their shared label cell (column 0) gets merged into one newline-joined
  "1\n2\n3\n4\nm/s" block by find_tables(), unlike the radius rows above
  them where every column's cell is what's newline-joined. First attempt
  wrongly assumed the same merge pattern applied to all 13 columns of
  this block (not just column 0), which silently produced only 1 of the
  4 real extension values per column and left wind rating as None for
  every row. Caught immediately by cross-checking parsed output against
  the same page rendered as an image (page 120, T273.00013) before
  building the full script - not just checking the script ran cleanly.
  Fixed by reading raw[ext_start + r] as five independent rows directly
  rather than trying to split column 0's merged text and reapply it
  elsewhere.
  Verified the corrected extraction against three separate pages spanning
  all three combos (visual render of one page's full grid, plus two more
  combos' raw JSON cross-checked cell-by-cell against the manually-read
  page text) before shipping - every boom length, extension %, wind
  rating, and radius/capacity pair matched exactly, including the
  two-page "page 2 of 2" continuation columns (a single 50.0m boom-length
  column each, merged into the same combo as its 12-column first page).
  Final size: 3 combos, 39 columns, 546 datapoints, ~9.5KB JSON - reuses
  the same lazy-fetch loadchart/<file>.json pattern as 1110 (14.4),
  wired into the existing LOAD_CHART_FILES/LOAD_CHART_CRANE_LABELS maps
  with no other index.html cranes touched. FLEET_BLOCK_SPEC already had
  an '1100' entry from an earlier feature, so the hook-block/Winch 2
  selectors needed no new data, just picked it up automatically.
  Regression-checked LTM 1110 in the same tab after these changes (wind
  toggle still page-bracket mode, reeving column still shows, CWT lookup
  still correct) - 0 console errors, matches pre-change output exactly.

15.4 LTM 1130-5.1 - per-column wind confirmed as the norm going forward,
     and a genuinely different extension-row layout in the same PDF family
  2770 pages, one file (no boom-mode suffix - like LRT 1100's file, this
  one's own 40.60/40.62 chapters confirm main-boom types are just "T"
  (telescopic) with an optional "V" telescopic-extension add-on, "K"/
  "NZK" for auxiliary jib variants; scope stays plain "T", nothing else).
  Person confirmed after seeing LRT 1100's per-column wind rating that
  most of the remaining fleet (1130/1160/1250/1300) will read the same
  way, with 1650 the one exception to check for when that crane comes
  up - matches what this crane's own charts show (a "m/s" row varying
  per column, not a fixed page-level bracket) and its own 40.65 icon
  chapter confirms the standard "+" = pinned / "-" = can telescope under
  load reading (the SAME way round as 1110, not LRT 1100's inverted
  version - re-read each crane's own legend chapter regardless, per
  14.1/15.2's rule, rather than assuming either convention carries over).
  Support base also varies here (2.40m / 5.00m / 7.50m widths) - unlike
  LRT 1100 though, the narrower widths aren't just narrower, they also
  cap slewing to a restricted ±10° arc instead of full 360°; only the
  widest (7.50m) width offers 360°, so "fully deployed outriggers" and
  "360° slew" are the same filter on this crane (confirmed - not
  assumed - by checking the slew badge on both the 5.00m and 7.50m width
  pages directly). A first classification pass that only kept (cwt,
  outrigger-width) as its dedupe key silently merged the 360° and ±10°
  page sets together under one key (a broken regex that always matched
  '?' for slew, not caught until an actual side-by-side page comparison
  showed near-identical-but-not-quite capacity numbers between two
  "same key" page groups) - fixed by adding slew to the key before
  trusting any combo count from the scan. Final in-scope set: 28
  counterweight levels x 10 pages each = 280 pages (chart refs
  T205.00301 through T205.00328) - a full-document scan confirmed this
  before extracting anything, not assumed from a partial sample.
  This crane's PDF pages ALSO print hoist rope reeving per column (a
  "* n *" row, strand count per column) - LRT 1100's chart doesn't have
  this row at all; the extraction script reads it when present and
  leaves the JSON's reeving field null when it isn't, same field either
  way.
  One real structural difference from both prior cranes' extension-row
  layout, caught by comparing find_tables() output against a rendered
  page before writing the full script (not assumed to match either
  prior crane): this crane has 5 telescopic sections, and while their
  labels ('1'/'2'/'3'/'4'/'5') are merged into one stacked label cell
  same as before, the actual DATA rows for the block came out grouped
  at 2-then-2-then-1 sections per raw table row (not "1 row per
  section" like LRT 1100, and not "1 merged pair per row" like the
  radius rows above it) - confirmed against a page render (T205.00325,
  column 12: section 3 correctly a "0-" live-telescope marker, matching
  both the JSON output and the rendered chart pixel-for-pixel). Rather
  than hardcode this grouping (which could easily be a THIRD shape on
  the next crane), the extraction script was rewritten to a general
  algorithm: read the real section count from the label cell's own
  digit tokens, then walk forward summing each raw row's own stacked-
  value depth (however many values are actually stacked in that row's
  cells) until every section is accounted for, whatever the grouping
  turns out to be - verified this generalized form still extracts LRT
  1100's simpler 1-row-per-section layout correctly too (same formula,
  depth=1 falls out naturally), so no per-crane branch was needed.
  Verified extraction against two separate pages (T205.00325 columns 1
  and 12, one all-pinned and one with a real "0-" live-telescope marker)
  cross-checked pixel-for-pixel against rendered images before shipping
  - radius/capacity values, wind ratings, reeving counts, and every
  section's pin state all matched exactly, including the "-" case this
  crane actually needed (unlike LRT 1100, where the person said to
  treat everything as pinned - here the exclusion filter from 14.9 has
  real work to do: 2,492 individual unpinned-section instances found
  across the full dataset before any filtering).
  Final size: 28 combos, 3,752 columns, 60,177 datapoints, ~980KB JSON.
  Wired into LOAD_CHART_FILES/LOAD_CHART_CRANE_LABELS with no changes
  needed elsewhere (FLEET_BLOCK_SPEC already had an '1130' entry).
  Regression-checked LRT 1100 and LTM 1110 in the same tab afterward -
  0 console errors, both still matched their pre-change output.

15.5 LTM 1160-5.2 - first crane needing the ballast (counterweight tray)
     radius selector, and its own new T*-equivalent exclusion
  1495 pages, one file (main boom types just "T"/"V" again, same as
  1130 - no per-mode file split needed). This crane's own 40.65 icon
  chapter confirms the standard "+"=pinned/"-"=live-telescope reading
  again (not LRT 1100's inverted one) - checked directly, not assumed
  from 1130 just because both are non-LRT Liebherrs.
  Found this crane's OWN T*-equivalent: a handful of pages (3, out of
  ~1386 T-mode pages) print operating mode as "T *)" instead of plain
  "T" - a footnoted variant tied to a single-position, 0°-slew,
  extreme-capacity chart (160t at 3m radius on one page checked) that
  reads exactly like 1110's T* ("requires an auxiliary device/special
  rigging accessory", 14.1) even though the footnote symbol itself
  ("*)" vs 1110's literal "*" suffix) differs - excluded on the same
  basis, checked by tallying mode-label counts across the whole
  document before writing any extraction logic, not assumed from a
  single sample page.
  Outrigger support base varies (2.45 / 5.38 / 6.84 / 8.30m) with the
  same "only the widest offers 360°" pattern as 1130 (confirmed via
  each width's own slew badge, not assumed to carry over) - "fully
  deployed" = 8.30m, and that's the same filter as 360° again here.
  The actual new thing this crane needed: at the 8.30m/360° scope,
  counterweight levels of 10.0t and above each print TWO separate
  charts for the exact same tonnage - one with the counterweight tray
  badge reading "x4.8m", one reading "x5.3m" (a distance from the slew
  axis, confirmed by cross-referencing the same cwt+outrigger+slew
  combination showing genuinely different capacity numbers between the
  two - not a duplicate/misprint). The two lowest levels (0.0t/4.0t)
  print no radius badge at all - only one physical mounting exists at
  that little counterweight, nothing to choose between. This is what
  the person had asked for as the "ballast radius selector" back before
  any of this crane's charts were opened, and it lines up exactly with
  what's actually in the PDF once found (a full-document badge scan,
  not assumed from theory-chapter wording - the icon-explanation
  chapter never separately describes a counterweight-radius icon, it
  only shows up as an extra token appended to the counterweight badge
  on the pages that have it).
  Implementation: JSON combo keys gained a third segment
  ('cwt|wind|ballastRadius'), with the two radius-less counterweight
  levels' columns duplicated verbatim under BOTH radius keys at
  extraction time - simpler than teaching the lookup code a fallback
  path, and means selecting either radius always finds every
  counterweight level the crane actually offers, never showing "no
  results" just because the crew's chosen radius happens not to apply
  at the low end. New UI: a ballast-radius toggle (mode-btn row, same
  pattern as CWT/wind), shown only when meta.hasBallastRadius is set,
  and left visible in BOTH manual and auto/find-minimum-CWT counterweight
  modes per the person's explicit instruction - it's the crew's choice
  either way, not something the auto-finder should quietly pick for them.
  Verified two pages (one radius-less low-cwt combo, one 4.8m-radius
  combo) against rendered images before shipping - reeving, wind
  ratings, extension states and radius/capacity values all matched
  exactly, including cross-checking that selecting 4.8m vs 5.3m in the
  UI actually pulled different chart references (0054 vs 1467) and
  different capacity numbers for literally the same boom/cwt request,
  not the same data shown twice under two labels.
  Final size: 24 stored combo entries (22 real + 2 duplicated), 2,608
  columns, 43,182 datapoints, ~700KB JSON. Regression-checked LRT 1100,
  LTM 1110, and LTM 1130 afterward (ballast-radius UI correctly stays
  hidden for all three) - 0 console errors.

15.6 LTM 1250-5.1 - both axes at once (page-level wind bracket AND
     ballast radius), 13,223-page source, real T* back in play
  Biggest source document since 1110's own 12,292-page file. This
  crane's own 40.62 chapter has a genuine "T*" main-boom mode again
  ("Telescopic boom with auxiliary device") - the SAME literal symbol
  1110 used (not 1160's differently-punctuated "T *)"), confirming this
  really is a per-crane naming detail, not a fleet-wide constant to
  hardcode - re-checked directly rather than assumed either way, same
  as every crane so far.
  Badge layout is a full step back to 1110's shape: counterweight,
  counterweight radius, support base, slew, AND a single page-level
  wind rating are all fixed per page, with NO per-column "m/s" row in
  the table at all (confirmed against a page render before writing the
  extraction script - this crane needed its own reeving-row handling
  too, since without a wind row after the 5 section rows, the very next
  row is instead the reeving row, labelled "EST1", not a "* n *" row
  positioned before the extension block like 1130/1160). This crane
  also has its OWN "counterweight radius" icon explicitly documented in
  its 40.65 chapter ("large counterweight radius" / "small counterweight
  radius") - the first crane so far to actually name the concept
  directly rather than it only being inferable from comparing two
  same-tonnage charts (1160's case).
  Net effect: this is the first crane with THREE independently
  selectable axes at once - counterweight, wind bracket (page-level,
  reusing 1110's UI unchanged), and ballast radius (reusing 1160's UI
  unchanged) - and both existing UI code paths turned out to already
  compose correctly together with zero additional JS changes beyond
  registering the crane, since the wind-group and ballast-group
  visibility toggles were written as independent conditionals from the
  start (14/15.5), not an if/else assuming only one could apply.
  Full-document classification scan (a genuine ~150s job at 13,223
  pages, run in the background rather than blocking the session) found
  21 counterweight levels x 2 ballast radii (4.8m/5.6m) x 3 wind
  brackets (11.2/13.4/15.6 m/s) = 126 in-scope combos at the fully-
  deployed 8.30m support base + 360° slew, each a clean contiguous
  14-page run - verified programmatically (no non-contiguous ranges)
  before extracting. One near-miss caught during verification, not a
  real bug: a page sampled during reconnaissance (badge: 78.0t/5.6m
  radius/11.2m/s) looked like it should match combo key
  "78.0|5.6|11.2" but that key's actual stored page range was over a
  thousand pages away - turned out the reconnaissance page had a
  restricted ±10° slew, correctly excluded by the 360°-only scope
  filter, and a SEPARATE 360°-slew chart elsewhere in the document
  happens to share the exact same counterweight/radius/wind badge
  values. Re-verified against the actual stored page (1316, chart ref
  T259.101.12002) before trusting the extraction - a reminder that
  matching badge values alone don't guarantee it's the same chart
  series once a crane has enough combos in play.
  Final size: 126 combos, 20,034 columns, 328,416 datapoints, ~5.3MB
  JSON - comparable in scale to 1110's own dataset. Regression-checked
  LRT 1100, LTM 1110, and LTM 1160 afterward - 0 console errors.

15.7 LTM 1300-6.2 - back to a single axis (no ballast radius on this
     crane), per-column wind, 6 telescopic sections
  1344 pages. Real "T*" mode again (auxiliary device exclusion, same as
  1250). This crane's own 40.65 icon chapter documents Counterweight
  and Crane-supported (support base) icons but never a counterweight-
  radius icon - checked directly rather than assumed from 1250's
  precedent, and confirmed empirically too (no second radius value ever
  appears in any footer badge across the classification scan). Matches
  what the person's original crane list implied (only 160/250/650 were
  named as needing the selector) - good agreement between the manual's
  own documentation and the request, not just a coincidence worth
  flagging either way.
  Support base varies (2.68 / 5.60 / 7.06 / 8.53m) with the same
  "widest = only 360°" pattern as 1130/1160/1250 - confirmed via each
  width's own slew badge rather than assumed to repeat automatically.
  11 counterweight levels at the 8.53m/360° scope (0 through 96t),
  16-17 pages each, all contiguous - found via a ~16s full-document
  scan (much faster than 1250's 13k-page job, this file is only 1,344
  pages). One real regex bug caught immediately by a 0-combos result
  on the first attempt (not silently trusted): the footer badge's
  counterweight and outrigger-length values sit on the SAME line here
  ("96.0      8.90 x     360°"), unlike the newline-separated version
  the regex was copied from - fixed by comparing the failing pattern
  against the actual page text before touching real extraction logic.
  Extension-row layout is yet a fourth distinct shape (all now handled
  by the same general "read section count from label digits, walk rows
  by their own depth" algorithm with zero per-crane branching in the
  extraction code itself, only in how each script locates the
  page-specific badge fields): 6 sections, reeving ("ESTn"), AND wind
  ("m/s") all stacked into ONE merged label cell, each as its own
  single-value-per-column row - reeving directly following the 6
  section rows, wind directly after that. Verified two pages (cwt=96.0
  and cwt=0.0, both start-of-combo pages) against rendered/dumped text
  before shipping - boom lengths, radius/capacity values, reeving, and
  wind ratings all matched exactly.
  Final size: 11 combos, 2,139 columns, 36,516 datapoints, ~620KB JSON.
  Regression-checked LRT 1100, LTM 1110, LTM 1130, and LTM 1250
  afterward (both wind-group and ballast-group UI correctly hidden for
  this crane) - 0 console errors.

15.8 LTM 1650-8.1 (T3 boom) - last of the six, and ballast radius turned
     out to mean something genuinely different on this crane
  Sixth and last of the person's requested crane list. 1,803-page source
  ("_T3" file specifically - the person confirmed mid-session this is
  the one wanted, not the sibling _T3Y/_T3N/_T3F files sitting alongside
  it at up to 204MB each). This crane's own 40.60 chapter names its main
  boom sign directly as "T3" (= "Telescopic boom 54 m", as opposed to a
  sibling "T5" = 80m boom in a completely different source file) -
  T3 is the mode symbol itself here, not a suffix on a generic "T", so
  the T*-equivalent exclusion is "T3*" and the Maintenance-operation
  exclusion is "T3~" (confirmed present via a mode tally across the
  whole T3 file before assuming either symbol carries over unchanged
  from 1110/1160/1250's own conventions).
  Ballast radius exists on this crane too, per the person's original
  request - but it does NOT take the shape either prior implementation
  (15.5's per-combo pair, 15.6's same idea) assumed. First investigation
  pass only checked the page's own TOP badge for a radius/angle value
  and found it present on just 2 of 17 counterweight levels, each with
  only ONE value - not a parallel pair to choose between like 1160's
  charts - and reported this dead end to the person rather than guessing
  a shape onto data that didn't support it. Person's reply named the
  real numbers directly (6.4/7.4/8.4) and the actual use case: filtering
  by ballast-swing clearance ("i may or may not be limited on
  clearance... to have my receptacle out all the way to 8.4"), which
  led to checking one of the "no radius badge" pages' full table
  structure directly rather than trusting the earlier top-badge-only
  scan - the real values were sitting in the extension-condition block
  the whole time, as two extra per-page rows ('m' = radius, '°' = angle,
  same uniform value repeated across every column on a page) that a
  badge-only regex never looked at. Person independently confirmed the
  three fixed pairs before extraction was finished (6.4m=135°,
  7.4m=90°, 8.4m=45°) - matched exactly once found.
  This is a structurally different kind of "ballast radius" than 15.5/
  15.6: not a chart-selection axis picked before searching (their
  meta.ballastRadiusMode = 'combo', baked into the JSON combo key) but a
  per-column property of the chart itself - which specific boom/cwt/
  wind combination happens to need which radius isn't the crew's choice,
  it's fixed by the chart. What the person wants selectable is a MAXIMUM
  they're prepared to accept (site clearance), filtering OUT any column
  whose required radius exceeds it - meta.ballastRadiusMode = 'column'.
  Implementation: extraction stores radius (and angle, display-only,
  never filtered) as two more per-column fields alongside boom length/
  reeving/wind. The existing ballast-radius toggle UI (built for 15.5)
  is reused unchanged for the widget itself, branching only on
  ballastRadiusMode: 'column' mode changes the label text ("Maximum
  Ballast Radius (Site Clearance)"), shows an explanatory disclaimer,
  defaults to the LARGEST radius (least restrictive - "no constraint" is
  the common case, unlike 'combo' mode which defaults to the first/
  smallest), and filters columns (radius > selected -> excluded) instead
  of selecting a combo-key segment. Column tuple position 4/5 means
  different things per crane now (wind rating for windMode:'column'
  cranes vs radius/angle for ballastRadiusMode:'column' cranes) - never
  ambiguous in practice since no crane has needed both at once, but the
  render code reads meta flags to decide which each time rather than
  assuming position 4 means one fixed thing.
  Verified the clearance filter actually reaches into different
  underlying chart series, not just relabeling the same numbers:
  restricting from the default 8.4m to 6.4m on the same query dropped
  the top result's capacity from 179.90t to 167.20t and its reeving from
  16x to 22x, and auto/find-minimum-CWT mode honored the same filter
  (also kept visible in auto mode, per the person's explicit instruction
  for the ballast-radius selector generally, 15.5).
  Wired in as crane key '1650-t3' (not bare '1650') to match the
  existing FLEET_BLOCK_SPEC entry built for this exact boom variant, so
  the hook-block/Winch 2 selectors needed no new data either.
  Final size: 51 combos, 4,935 columns, 86,807 datapoints, ~1.35MB JSON.
  Regression-checked all five previously shipped cranes afterward - 0
  console errors, all five still matched pre-change output exactly.
  This closes out the six-crane expansion the person asked for (LRT
  1100, LTM 1130/1160/1250/1300/1650), each shipped and verified on both
  channels individually per section 15.1-15.8 above.

15.9 Wired Load Chart Finder into the existing "Cache All Offline
     Content" mechanism
  All seven crane JSON files were already covered by the app's general
  rule (the service worker's fetch handler caches anything it
  successfully fetches into the persistent CONTENT_CACHE, forever) - so
  a crane already opened once while online was already available
  offline from then on. What was missing: the Load Chart Finder tab
  had no "Offline Availability" card and wasn't part of the
  allOfflineContentUrls() list the "Cache All Offline Content" button
  and status indicator (Reeving Plans / CWT Combinations / Driving w/
  Equipment tabs) already draw from - a crane never manually opened had
  no way to be pre-cached, and there was no way to check "is everything
  actually cached" for load charts specifically.
  Fix: added Object.values(LOAD_CHART_FILES) into
  allOfflineContentUrls() (function bodies aren't evaluated until
  called, so it not mattering that LOAD_CHART_FILES is declared later
  in the same script than allOfflineContentUrls() itself - by the time
  either is actually invoked, at page-load or on a button click, every
  top-level const in the script has already run), and added the same
  "Offline Availability" card (status span + Cache All button +
  progress bar) already used on the other three tabs to the bottom of
  the Load Chart Finder tab, plus added 'loadchart-tab' to the tab-
  switch list that refreshes the status indicator. No new mechanism
  built - purely plugging the existing one in for a tab that had been
  left out of it.
  Verified via Playwright: allOfflineContentUrls() now lists all 7
  loadchart/*.json paths, clicking "Cache All Offline Content" on the
  Load Chart Finder tab actually caches them (confirmed via
  caches.match() on every required URL, not just trusting the button's
  own completion message), the status flips to "Downloaded" afterward,
  and the same completion is reflected on the OTHER tabs' status spans
  too (shared cache pool, as expected) - 0 console errors.

15.10 Compare Cranes sub-tab - "find the best of several cranes for this
      lift" as its own mode, not a Find Boom Configuration option
  Person floated this as "a concept thought" first (multi-select 3+
  cranes, one radius/weight, get each crane's minimum counterweight and
  best-margin chart back) - answered with feasibility and the one real
  design snag (rigging weight/hook block choice is per-crane, doesn't
  generalize across a multi-select) rather than building anything yet,
  per the general rule for a floated idea rather than a request. Person's
  follow-up settled both open questions directly: drop rigging weight
  entirely for this mode, and present it as two sub-tabs inside the
  existing Load Chart Finder tab rather than a new top-level tab -
  "Find Boom Configuration" (unchanged) and "Compare Cranes" (new).
  Implementation:
  - HTML: a mode-toggle pair (lc-subtab-find/lc-subtab-compare, same
    .mode-toggle/.mode-btn pattern used everywhere else in this feature)
    switches which of two wrapper divs (lc-panel-find / lc-panel-compare)
    is visible - the existing three Find Boom Configuration cards moved
    into the first wrapper unchanged, nothing about their own behavior
    touched. Offline Availability stays outside both wrappers, visible
    regardless of sub-tab (it already covers every crane's JSON
    regardless of which sub-tab last touched them).
  - Compare Cranes gets its own crane multi-select (checkboxes built
    from the same LOAD_CHART_FILES/LOAD_CHART_CRANE_LABELS maps every
    other part of this feature already uses - one source of truth, no
    separate crane list to keep in sync), shared radius/weight inputs,
    and its own 90%-site-rule toggle (kept, unlike rigging weight -
    it's a single global multiplier, not something that varies per
    crane the way a hook block catalog does).
  - New computeLoadChartBestForCrane(data, radiusInput, weightInput,
    derateOn) factors out the core "ascend counterweight levels, stop at
    the first one with any qualifying column, keep that level's best-
    margin column" search - the same logic renderLoadChartResults()'s
    auto/find-minimum-CWT mode already does, but self-contained (no
    reliance on the Find Boom Configuration sub-tab's own module-level
    state - loadChartWind/loadChartBallastRadius/loadChartCwtMode belong
    to that sub-tab only) since Compare needs to run this once per
    selected crane independently, each with that crane's OWN default
    wind bracket (lowest) and ballast radius (widest/most permissive,
    matching the existing "column mode defaults to largest" convention
    from 15.8) rather than sharing one global setting across cranes with
    different data shapes.
  - Compare has its own crane-data cache (loadChartCompareCache, keyed
    by crane) separate from the Find sub-tab's single-crane
    loadChartData/loadChartCraneKey pair - Compare needs several cranes'
    data resident at once for a side-by-side table, Find only ever needs
    one. Both fetch the identical URL per crane though, so they share
    the same service-worker CONTENT_CACHE entry - switching sub-tabs
    never re-downloads a crane already opened in the other one.
  - Results sorted by margin descending (best option first, matching
    Find Boom Configuration's own top5 ordering) with out-of-range and
    no-qualifying-configuration cranes sorted to the bottom rather than
    hidden, so a crane that genuinely can't do the lift is still visible
    as a result, not silently dropped from the list.
  One real bug caught before shipping (not by inspection, by an actual
  edge-case query): the "no qualifying configuration" branch returned
  {none: true} without also carrying the rounded-up actualRadius the
  message template needed, rendering "...clears 5000.00t at undefinedm"
  - fixed by returning actualRadius on every branch, not just the
  success path. Verified an impossible target weight (5000t) and an
  out-of-range radius (999m) both render clean messages afterward, then
  a normal 3-crane query (1110/1130/1300, 10m/20t) sorted correctly by
  margin and recalculated correctly when the 90% toggle was flipped
  (1110's minimum counterweight rose from 6.9t to 8.5t, as expected -
  more counterweight needed once only 90% of chart capacity counts) -
  and confirmed switching back to Find Boom Configuration afterward
  was completely unaffected (separate state, separate cache) with a
  full regression sweep across all seven cranes - 0 console errors.

15.11 Driving w/ Equipment: relabeled the duplicate-row "Option A"/
      "Option B" per the OEM explanation found in 10.41's update
  Person confirmed wanting the relabel after the OEM-note discovery
  logged in 10.41's update (LTM 1160-5.2's own manual: Example 1 =
  permissible boom angle without axle-load optimization, Example 2 =
  optimal angle with optimized/lower axle load, same physical setup).
  Before relabeling unconditionally by position (index 0 / index 1),
  checked this actually holds across DRIVING_EQ_DATA's real rows rather
  than assuming the OEM's stated example order survived extraction
  unchanged - spot-checked every duplicate-weight pair in LTM 1110's
  own front-direction table (8.8/7.7/7.2/6.9/3.6/1.1/0.8/0.0t, 8 pairs)
  and found the SECOND row's axle-B figure lower than (or equal to) the
  first in every single case, several also showing a lower axle-A too -
  consistent with "index 1 = optimized, always" holding as extracted,
  not just usually.
  Changed the label generator (previously `Option
  ${String.fromCharCode(65+i)}`, i.e. literal "Option A"/"Option B") to
  "Permissible Angle" for index 0 and "Optimized Angle (Reduced Axle
  Load)" for index 1, falling back to the old lettered label for a
  hypothetical 3rd+ row (never seen in practice - the OEM's own chart
  structure only ever pairs two examples per weight - but kept as a
  defensive fallback rather than assuming it can never happen).
  Verified via Playwright (LTM 1110, front, 8.8t): renders "PERMISSIBLE
  ANGLE" first (boom angle 6°...29°, axle B 17t) then "OPTIMIZED ANGLE
  (REDUCED AXLE LOAD)" second (6°...27°, axle B 16t) - matches the
  OEM's own example order and the spot-check above exactly - 0 console
  errors.

16. CRANE SETUP TAB: 360-DEGREE SLEW CLEARANCE
  New feature, new tab ("Crane Setup", between Crane Positioning and Rope
  Retentioning). Concept: when parked alongside a structure (wall,
  stockpile, fence), how far does the superstructure's widest rear-swing
  point (counterweight, or Winch 2 if it sticks out further) oversail the
  side of the carrier during a full 360° slew - i.e. how much stand-off
  clearance is actually needed, or whether an already-parked crane has
  room to slew at all.

  Geometry (confirmed with the person before building anything): the slew
  axis sits on the carrier's centerline. Park the crane with that
  centerline parallel to the structure being cleared and the worst case
  is when the superstructure has rotated so the boom/CWT points straight
  sideways - at that angle the full swing radius becomes pure sideways
  distance from centerline, so subtracting half the carrier width (B/2)
  gives exactly how far the swing point oversails the side of the
  carrier. Standard tail-swing-beyond-track-width calc. Angle the crane
  off-parallel to the structure and the real clearance only gets bigger
  than this number, never smaller - so this is a conservative/worst-case
  figure by construction, not an average.

  Source data: each crane's own Operating Instructions manual (NOT the
  Load_chart_manual folder used for section 15 - a sibling
  Operating_instructions_Crane PDF, found via the same Nextcloud WebDAV
  share, chapter 1.03 Technical Data > 1 Dimensions). carrierWidthB comes
  straight off that chapter's tyre-size table (525/80 R25 column, this
  fleet's standard tyre per company policy/WA axle regulation - confirmed
  by the person, no need to track the other two tyre options at all,
  unlike Driving w/ Equipment's tyre-agnostic table which the fleet
  policy doesn't help simplify). The radius figures are NOT plain text -
  they're hand-drawn CAD leader-line labels on the chapter's plan-view
  figure, rotated 180° (same "scrambled" presentation as every dimension
  figure encountered this session, e.g. 1110's Fig.154887 back in the
  outrigger work) - read by rendering the PDF page to a high-DPI PNG and
  eyeballing the rotated digits, same method as always for this kind of
  figure.

  This went through a full report-and-confirm cycle before any number was
  trusted, because the CAD figures turned out to vary far more than
  expected - worth recording in full since it's a cautionary example of
  why "looks like the same pattern as the last crane" cannot be assumed:
  - LTM 1110/1130/1300: a clean two-radius pattern (inner = one
    component, outer = the other) - but 1110/1130 have CWT as the
    SMALLER/inner radius and Winch 2 as the larger/outer, while 1300 is
    the reverse (Winch 2 smaller, CWT larger) - confirmed directly by the
    person, not an assumption carried over from 1110/1130's pattern. Do
    NOT assume inner-vs-outer position predicts which component it is on
    a crane not yet checked.
  - LRT 1100: three radius labels near the superstructure (R4180, R7135,
    R7920) with no way to tell from the line art alone which was the
    CWT's rear-swing radius versus something unrelated (turned out R7135/
    R7920 were NOT superstructure-swing radii at all - sent crops to the
    person rather than guess, who confirmed the actual CWT figure was the
    smaller one I'd initially half-suspected was a red herring, R4190,
    off by 10mm from my own rotated-digit misread of R4180). This crane
    also has a real second winch (FLEET_BLOCK_SPEC already carries
    separate w1/p1 vs w2/p2 rope specs for it) but the person confirmed
    it sits entirely within the CWT's radius, so it's tracked as a single
    fixed point, not a pair.
  - LTM 1160/1250: THREE radius labels near the CWT, not two - initially
    unexplained (three points along the tray's non-circular outline?
    three unrelated features?). Sent crops rather than guess. Turned out
    both cranes have a genuine "vario ballast" (extended counterweight
    tray) option, same concept already modeled for the Load Chart
    Finder's ballastRadiusMode:'combo' cranes (section 15.5) but this is
    a DIFFERENT chapter/figure with its own independent radius numbers,
    not the same 4.8m/5.3m figures already in the load chart JSON - do
    not assume the two are interchangeable. Resolved to: 1160 standard
    tray radius (4730mm) coincides with Winch 2's own radius so only one
    figure is needed for the "standard" case, plus a second figure
    (5230mm) if the vario tray is fitted; 1250 keeps Winch 2 as its own
    separate fixed point (4975mm) alongside standard (4780mm) and vario
    (5580mm) CWT tray radii - three genuinely independent numbers, not
    two.
  - LTM 1650: the radius labels found (R7950, R7300) traced a long curve
    running most of the vehicle's length rather than a tight corner near
    the slew center, and didn't fit the pattern at all - the person
    redirected to reuse the SAME 6.4/7.4/8.4m site-clearance radii
    already established for this crane's Load Chart Finder ballast
    selector (section 15.8) rather than extracting anything new, since
    it's the same physical vario-ballast positions being described.
  - LTR 1220 (crawler): explicitly excluded by the person - no 525/80
    tyres (it runs on tracks), out of scope for this feature entirely.
    Its counterweight-tab task in the tracker is unrelated/separate work.
  - LRT 1100 also has no 525/80 tyre option (single fixed tyre, 29.5
    R25), but unlike 1220 it's still an on-road-relevant crane for this
    feature, so its carrierWidthB (3300mm) is read directly off its own
    dimension figure's front-view width dimension instead of a tyre-size
    table lookup - flagged in the UI (widthNote) so the fleet-wide
    "525/80 R25" disclaimer text doesn't silently misrepresent this one
    crane's actual figure as a tyre-size substitute.

  Data model: SLEW_CLEARANCE_DATA, keyed the same as CARRIER_MODELS/
  CARRIER_CALIBRATION (bare crane key, e.g. '1650' not '1650-t3' - this
  feature isn't boom-mode-specific the way the Load Chart Finder is).
  Each entry has `fixed` (points whose radius never changes) and
  `variants` (null, or a {label, options[]} pair for cranes with a
  selectable tray/ballast radius - only one is ever physically fitted at
  a time, rendered as a mode-toggle, defaulting to index 0/first option
  on every crane switch rather than remembering the last selection across
  different cranes).

  UI: new "Crane & Configuration" card (crane select + variant toggle
  when applicable) and a "360° Slew Clearance" results card (radius +
  computed clearance per point, in meters, plus a "limiting point" line
  picking whichever point has the largest radius - that's the one that
  actually governs how much stand-off is needed). A third card links
  over to the Crane Positioning tab for the eventual 3D visualization
  (see below) rather than embedding a second 3D viewer inline.

  3D radius-circle toggle (the person's original "would be good to see"
  ask, alongside the numeric table) is DEFERRED, not shipped in this
  pass - vendor/carrier3d.js's viewer is a single hardcoded-canvas module-
  level instance (one scene/camera/renderer, tied to
  #carrier-3d-canvas-wrap in the Crane Positioning tab specifically), so
  a second concurrent 3D view on a different tab isn't a drop-in reuse -
  it needs either a small refactor to parameterize which DOM element the
  viewer renders into, or a scene-graph clone() of the already-cached GLB
  for a second concurrent instance. Rather than half-build this under
  time pressure, shipped the fully-correct numeric feature now (it's the
  actual clearance figure that matters) and left the 3D piece as a
  visible fast-follow via the "3D Visualization" card's link + explanatory
  text, instead of silently dropping it or shipping a rushed version.

  Tested via Playwright across all 7 in-scope cranes (1100/1110/1130/
  1160/1250/1300/1650): every clearance figure hand-verified against
  radius minus half the carrier width, variant toggle switches correctly
  (1160/1250/1650), the "no variant" cranes correctly hide that card
  section, and the Crane Positioning cross-link correctly activates that
  tab/button pair without leaving two tabs simultaneously marked active -
  0 console errors throughout.

  UPDATE (same day): restructured into a Crane Positioning sub-tab, and
  the deferred 3D radius circle shipped after all.
  Person clarified the original ask right after the first ship: "Crane
  Setup" was meant to be a SUB-tab of Crane Positioning, not a sibling
  top-level nav tab - and the pre-existing outrigger/site-plan/3D-carrier/
  bog-mat content needed its own sub-tab name too ("Support Pad
  Placement") rather than staying as Crane Positioning's only content.
  Removed the standalone "Crane Setup" nav button and slewclear-tab div;
  reused the exact mode-toggle/panel-show-hide pattern already
  established by the Load Chart Finder's Find/Compare sub-tabs
  (setLoadChartSubTab) for a new setCadSubTab('pads'|'setup'), with
  cad-panel-pads holding everything that used to be cad-tab's entire
  content and cad-panel-setup holding the slew-clearance UI. The "3D
  Visualization" card's cross-link now just calls setCadSubTab('pads')
  (same-tab sub-tab switch) instead of switchTab() to a different
  top-level tab.
  This also removed the original reason for deferring the 3D radius-
  circle overlay: once Crane Setup and the 3D carrier viewer are sub-tabs
  of the SAME parent tab, there's no second concurrent viewer instance to
  build at all - the existing single carrier3d.js viewer just needs a new
  capability, not a second instance. Added window.__carrier3dSetSlewCircles
  (vendor/carrier3d.js) alongside the existing __carrier3dSyncOutriggers:
  draws a flat ring on the ground plane per requested radius, centred on
  siteToWorld(cal, 0, 0) (the same slew-centre point the outrigger pads
  already anchor to) using whichever calibration computeCalibration()
  already cached for that model - no new calibration math needed, since
  the slew centre doesn't depend on which feature is asking for it. Ring
  radius is drawn directly in world metres (radius_mm/1000), not run
  through cal.xSlope/zSlope, since those carry the site-plan's own
  approximate-footprint mm->model mapping while the CAD model itself is
  already dimensionally accurate - a real physical radius just needs the
  plain unit conversion, same as every other physical size already drawn
  (pad boxes, etc). Bumped CARRIER3D_VERSION (21->22) since the vendor
  file changed.
  UI: a "360° Slew Radius" checkbox row was added to the existing 3D
  Carrier Preview card's toolbar (index.html's
  renderCarrier3DSlewToggles(), called from updateCarrier3DAvailability()
  on every crane switch) - one checkbox per SLEW_CLEARANCE_DATA point,
  including every variant option shown side by side (not behind a
  selector) so both "Standard" and "Vario Ballast" can be ticked at once
  to compare directly on the model. Distinct colours per point
  (SLEW_CIRCLE_COLORS, red/purple/green/orange) deliberately different
  from the gold/cyan/white already used for pad markers/ghosts, so a
  slew ring is never mistaken for one of those. Checkboxes rebuild from
  scratch (and reset to unticked) on every crane switch; the rebuild
  fires onSlewCircleToggle() once at the end specifically so the 3D
  view's own pendingSlewCircles[modelKey] state (in carrier3d.js) can't
  keep showing a stale circle from a previous crane if the person
  switches back to it later without re-ticking anything.
  Verified via Playwright + screenshots (LTM 1110): both Counterweight
  and Winch 2 circles render as concentric rings centred correctly on the
  slew axis (Winch 2's larger ring visibly containing Counterweight's
  smaller one, matching their real radii), unticking one removes only
  that ring, and switching to LTM 1160 correctly rebuilds the checkbox
  list to its own Standard/Vario Ballast pair with both unticked. Full
  regression sweep across all 7 top-level tabs plus the existing
  outrigger pad-toggle/compare-mode controls afterward - 0 console
  errors throughout.

  UPDATE (same day): removed the Crane Setup sub-tab's "3D Visualization"
  card. It was only ever a one-line pointer ("see this on the Support Pad
  Placement sub-tab") written back when Crane Setup was still its own
  top-level tab with no easy way over to the 3D view - once it became a
  sub-tab of the same parent as Support Pad Placement, that card was just
  telling the person to use the mode-toggle sitting directly above it.
  Person asked not to have it duplicated across sub-tabs; removed
  entirely rather than kept as dead weight, since the toggle buttons
  already make switching obvious with no card needed.

  UPDATE (same day): person clarified further - wanted BOTH sub-tabs to
  have their OWN 3D Carrier Preview card (so neither one requires
  switching to the other just to see the model), with the slew-radius
  toggle checkboxes living EXCLUSIVELY on Crane Layout's copy, and the
  sub-tab renamed Crane Setup -> Crane Layout throughout (ids too:
  cad-subtab-layout/cad-panel-layout, mode string 'layout' not 'setup').

  This reopens the exact tradeoff the original 3D-toggle deferral was
  about, in a new shape: TWO cards now need a 3D view, but
  vendor/carrier3d.js is still a single hardcoded-canvas module (one
  scene/camera/renderer/controls, previously tied to one fixed
  #carrier-3d-canvas-wrap). Rather than spin up a second concurrent
  WebGL context (real cost - a duplicate GLB parse/upload for a model
  already sitting in modelCache, and every mobile browser caps how many
  live WebGL contexts it'll allow at once), generalized the ONE renderer
  to be re-parentable: ensureRenderer(wrapId, labelId) now moves the
  existing canvas into whichever wrap asked for it (a no-op if already
  there), returning whether it actually moved. Since the two sub-tabs are
  mutually exclusive (only one ever visible), this is invisible to the
  person - it just looks like "each sub-tab has its own persistent 3D
  view."
  __carrier3dActivate(modelKey, url, wrapId, labelId) gained the wrapId/
  labelId params and a new case its old two-branch shape (same model ->
  no-op; different model -> full clearScene()+swap) didn't cover: SAME
  model, DIFFERENT wrap - e.g. Support Pad Placement showing LTM 1110
  while Crane Layout's own crane select ALSO happens to be on 1110. The
  old "if (currentModelKey === modelKey) return;" early-return would have
  silently left the canvas parented wherever it was, so the newly-
  activated card would show nothing at all. Now: a wrap change (with or
  without a model change) always proceeds; a same-model/different-wrap
  transition additionally drops the PREVIOUS context's own overlays
  (clearOutriggers()+clearSlewCircles()) without touching the model
  itself, since Pad Placement's outrigger markers have no business
  showing up in Crane Layout's context or vice versa - whichever card
  just took over immediately pushes its own fresh overlay state right
  after activate() returns (syncCarrier3DOutriggers() for Pad Placement,
  onSlewCircleToggle() for Crane Layout), same as they always did.
  setCadSubTab() now actively reclaims the canvas on every switch, not
  just show/hide a div: switching to 'pads' re-runs activateCarrier3D()
  if carrier3DActive is true, switching to 'layout' re-runs
  activateCarrier3DLayout() if carrier3DLayoutActive is true - each is a
  small extracted helper (previously inlined in toggleCarrier3D()/
  toggleCarrier3DLayout()) so the exact same activation logic runs
  whether triggered by the toggle button or by a sub-tab switch finding
  its own "3D on" flag already set from before the person navigated away.
  Also fixed sceneBox() (used by Fit View) to include slewCircleGroup's
  own bounds, not just the model's and outriggerGroup's - a circle can be
  (and often is) far wider than the carrier itself, e.g. LTM 1650 at its
  8.4m ballast radius, so Fit View would otherwise frame just the model
  and clip the ring off-screen.

  Real bug caught during testing, not by inspection: circles simply
  didn't draw at all when Crane Layout was used WITHOUT first opening
  Support Pad Placement's own 3D preview for the same crane. Root cause:
  applySlewCircles() read calibrationCache[modelKey], which is ONLY ever
  populated by computeCalibration() inside applySync() - i.e. only by
  Support Pad Placement's own outrigger sync. The original comment
  reasoned "outrigger sync always runs first... before a person could
  realistically reach the checkbox" - true when Crane Setup only had
  Support Pad Placement's crane to work with, false now that Crane Layout
  has its own independent crane select (sc-crane) that may never have
  been opened on the Pad Placement side at all. Fixed with a SEPARATE
  fallback cache (formulaCalibrationCache) computed from just
  computeFormulaCalibration(root, footprint, calibration) - the same
  formula the full refinement itself starts from, good enough to anchor a
  slew-centre ring without needing any outrigger leg geometry to refine
  against. Deliberately kept in its own cache rather than writing into
  calibrationCache, so if Support Pad Placement's own sync runs LATER for
  the same model, it still gets the full leg-anchored refinement, not
  silently stuck on the cheaper stand-in a lookup-order shortcut would
  have left behind. index.html's onSlewCircleToggle() now passes
  FOOTPRINTS[modelKey]/CARRIER_CALIBRATION[modelKey] through
  __carrier3dSetSlewCircles() so this fallback has what it needs; a new
  pendingSlewCircleContext map carries the same two values through to the
  async-model-still-loading replay path in __carrier3dActivate.

  Testing note: Playwright's native .click() (and even a JS-dispatched
  .click() via evaluate) hung indefinitely against the 3D toggle buttons
  in this sandboxed environment, with "GPU stall due to ReadPixels"
  console warnings alongside - consistent with Playwright's own element-
  stability screenshotting fighting a live, actively-rendering WebGL
  canvas under software rendering (SwiftShader, no real GPU in this
  sandbox), not an app defect. Confirmed by calling the exact same
  handler functions directly via page.evaluate() (toggleCarrier3D(),
  toggleCarrier3DLayout(), onSlewCircleToggle()) - all returned in
  well under a second with correct results and zero console errors, just
  through a code path that skips Playwright's click-specific stability
  polling. All functional verification for this update was done this
  way, cross-checked with screenshots.
  Verified: both cards' own 3D toggle correctly loads/shows their
  respective crane; checking Standard + Vario Ballast circles on LTM
  1160 (opened directly via Crane Layout, no prior Pad Placement 3D use)
  renders two correctly-centred, correctly-scaled concentric rings;
  switching Support Pad Placement -> Crane Layout -> Support Pad
  Placement reclaims the canvas each time (confirmed via each canvas's

  restored and zero leftover slew circles from the other context, and a
  full regression sweep across all 7 top-level tabs plus the existing
  outrigger pad-toggle/compare-mode controls came back clean - 0 console
  errors throughout every check.

  When on, draws a real 3D dimension line for every currently-checked
  circle: a line from the carrier's own side (half the carrier width from
  the slew centre) straight out to that circle along world +X - the same
  "parked parallel to the structure" direction the numeric clearance
  figure itself already assumes - with a short perpendicular tick at each
  end (standard dimension-line convention) and a text label at the
  midpoint showing the same clearance figure as the numeric table, so the
  two can be cross-checked against each other directly.
  The label is a THREE.Sprite billboard (a canvas-texture quad that
  always faces the camera), not an HTML overlay tracking a projected
  screen position every frame the way the transient "Loading 3D model…"
  label does - simpler, and correct at any camera angle/zoom without
  needing to run projection math on every OrbitControls frame, since it's
  a real object living in the 3D scene rather than a DOM element
  shadowing one.
  Implementation: applySlewCircles() (vendor/carrier3d.js) gained a
  carrierWidthMm param - when present, draws the line/ticks/label
  alongside each circle instead of just the ring on its own.

  UPDATE (same day): added a third Crane Layout toggle - "Show outrigger
  distances (C1-C4, baseline position)" - OEM-drawing-style dimension

  baseline position, labelled with the real distance (e.g. "C1: 5049mm").
  Person's own framing of the two Crane Positioning sub-tabs, which
  explains why this belongs on Crane Layout and not Support Pad
  Placement: Crane Layout is "will I be able to feasibly set up the crane
  in this position at all" (their company also has access to a Franna -
  a smaller pick-and-carry crane - on SOME but not all jobs, to help move
  bog mats/pads into place; this tab's job is deciding whether the crane
  fits before anyone's committed to a spot), Support Pad Placement is the
  physical "if I have to place these mats myself, here's exactly where"
  execution tool. Confirmed against a real OEM drawing crop the person
  sent (rotated dimension text, arrow+label style) - matches what this
  session already had to read constantly for LTM 1650/1160/1250's own
  swing-radius figures (section 16 above), same convention.
  Reused the SAME line+ticks+label drawing code the clearance measurement
  already built, generalized: addDimensionLine(group, p1, p2, color,
  labelText) now takes two arbitrary ground-plane points instead of
  assuming the line always runs along world +X - the clearance line still
  does (it's a real physical constraint of that measurement), but each
  outrigger leg sits at its own distinct angle from the slew centre, so
  the tick marks needed to be genuinely perpendicular to whatever
  direction that specific line runs, not hardcoded to the Z axis. Uses
  each leg's BASELINE (unshifted) r/angle straight from cadFleetData - the
  exact same figures already shown in the "Baseline" column of Support Pad
  Placement's own "All Four Legs" table - not Support Pad Placement's
  current shift/pad state, since Crane Layout has no shift inputs of its
  own at all (matches "deciding if you'll be able to feasibly set up" -
  a planning question, not "where did I put the mat this time").
  New third pending/context map pair (pendingLegDimensions/
  pendingLegDimensionContext) mirroring pendingSlewCircles' own replay-on-
  model-still-loading pattern, its own legDimensionGroup (cleared
  alongside outriggerGroup/slewCircleGroup on every wrap-change - a person
  with BOTH Support Pad Placement's outrigger markers AND Crane Layout's
  leg-dimension lines toggled on at different times must never see one
  bleed into the other's context), and its own line in sceneBox() so Fit
  View accounts for it too.
  Also fixed a real (if minor) memory-leak gap noticed while touching this
  code: material.dispose() alone does NOT dispose a texture the material
  holds (e.g. the dimension labels' canvas-texture Sprites) - Three.js
  doesn't cascade that automatically. Added a shared disposeGroup() helper
  (geometry + material + material.map) used by all three clearXxx()
  functions now, not just the new one.

  correctly-labelled dimension lines matching the "All Four Legs" table's
