  UPDATE (same day): added a fourth Crane Layout toggle - "Show ground
  layout marks (paint-out dimensions)" - genuinely different from the
  "outrigger distances" toggle directly above, despite both being C1-C4
  dimension lines on the same tab. The outrigger-distances toggle draws
  each leg's diagonal ("as the crow flies") distance from the slew centre
  - exactly what's needed once the crane is already parked, to place
  support pads (Support Pad Placement's own job). This new toggle is for
  BEFORE the crane arrives: the figures a crew uses to mark the crane's
  whole footprint out on bare soil with a tape measure and line-marking
  paint, so they know exactly where to park it. Person walked through
  their own real-world procedure step by step with worked numbers (find
  the outrigger's own edge-of-carrier line via (span-B)/2, walk that line
  the leg's longitudinal offset, mark the leg's centre; separately confirm
  the slew centre by measuring in from that same line) and provided an
  annotated OEM drawing screenshot showing the convention: a line
  projected from the slew centre out to meet the straight line between two
  outriggers. A diagonal alone can't be marked out on soil with a tape
  measure and no other reference - there's no way to know what bearing to
  swing the tape at, you'd be guessing (person's own words) - hence
  needing the offsets decomposed into two independently-measurable,
  right-angle components instead.
  Data model (GROUND_LAYOUT_DATA, index.html): per crane, rearSpan/
  frontSpan (the OEM's own printed total support width between that
  pair's two outriggers, fully extended) plus each leg's own longitudinal
  offset (C1-C4, mm, along the carrier's centreline from the slew centre
  to that leg's own "station", read directly off the OEM Dimensions
  sheet's vertical witness-line dimension chains). Lateral position is
  DERIVED, not stored separately: full distance from centreline = span/2;
  distance from the carrier's own edge (what a crew actually measures in
  the field, since it's a physical reference locatable without knowing
  where the slew centre is) = (span - carrierWidthB) / 2, reusing
  FOOTPRINTS[modelKey].width for B rather than duplicating it (confirmed
  identical to SLEW_CLEARANCE_DATA's own carrierWidthB for all 7 cranes).
  Genuinely asymmetric left/right per leg (not a mirrored pair) - same as
  the existing "as the crow flies" r/angle data already was, confirmed
  against the real OEM drawings rather than assumed symmetric.
  Extraction (LTM 1110-5.1, 1130-5.1, 1160-5.2, 1250-5.1, 1300-6.2,
  1650-8.1, LRT 1100-2.1 - all 7 slew-clearance cranes except LTR 1220,
  a crawler crane with no outriggers in this sense, per explicit "not the
  LTR" instruction): read from each crane's own cached OEM Operating
  Instructions PDF (Nextcloud WebDAV source, Load_chart_manual's sibling
  Operating_instructions_Crane folder), "1.03 Technical Data > 1
  Dimensions" figure, same rotated-text/high-zoom-crop reading technique
  established earlier in this session for the swing-radius figures.
  Cross-validated every single leg on every crane via Pythagoras against
  cadFleetData's own trusted r values (longitudinal^2 + (span/2)^2 should
  equal r^2) BEFORE trusting any reading - this caught two real reading
  mistakes during extraction (LTM 1160: misread a decimal-adjacent digit
  producing a ~40-60mm-off prediction that was still directionally
  consistent enough to trust once re-verified via a tighter crop; LRT
  1100: initially grabbed the SIDE-ELEVATION view's own vertical dimension
  chain instead of the PLAN view's - the two numerically overlap for one
  side by coincidence, since the side view happens to align with one
  specific track, which made a wrong reading look plausible until the
  actual plan-view crop was found and gave a much better cross-check
  match). Match quality varied by crane but every leg on every crane
  landed within 66mm of the trusted r value (most within 1-30mm, several
  cranes - LTM 1250/1300/1650 - within 2mm on every leg), confirming both
  the readings and the leg-pairing convention (page-left bracket =
  vehicle-right legs, page-right = vehicle-left; upper chain value = rear
  leg, lower = front leg - consistent across all 7 cranes checked).
  Drawing (vendor/carrier3d.js): two dimension lines per leg, not one -
  deliberately mirrors the two numbers a crew actually uses, not a single
  combined figure they'd have to do their own subtraction on.
    1. Slew centre -> station point on the centreline (0, stationYMm) -
       the longitudinal figure, labelled "C1 fwd/back: 3059mm" etc.
    2. Carrier edge (edgeXMm, stationYMm) -> leg (legXMm, stationYMm) -
       the lateral figure, labelled "C1 out from edge: 2733mm" etc. Starts
       at the carrier's own edge, not the centreline - the short gap
       between centreline and edge is deliberately left undrawn, since
       it's just half the carrier's own known/fixed width, not something
       to remeasure on site.
  Own fourth pending/context map pair (pendingGroundLayoutMarks/
  pendingGroundLayoutContext) and groundLayoutGroup, mirroring the
  outrigger-distances toggle's own pattern exactly - cleared alongside the
  other three overlay groups on every wrap-change, included in sceneBox()
  for Fit View, replayed from the pending map if the model was still
  mid-fetch when the toggle was flipped. index.html's onGroundLayoutToggle()
  computes each leg's stationYMm/edgeXMm/legXMm/lonMm/latMm from
  GROUND_LAYOUT_DATA + FOOTPRINTS and hands them to
  window.__carrier3dSetGroundLayoutMarks(); reused GROUND_LAYOUT_COLORS
  (same 4-colour cycle as the slew circles) purely so each leg reads as a
  visually distinct line when several toggles are on at once.
  Also noticed while touching activateCarrier3DLayout() that the existing
  "outrigger distances" toggle's own checked state wasn't being replayed
  on Crane Layout <-> Support Pad Placement sub-tab switches (only
  onSlewCircleToggle() was called there) - harmless in practice, since
  switching sub-tabs always runs through renderCarrier3DSlewToggles()
  first anyway (via initSlewClearanceTab -> onSlewClearanceCraneChange ->
  renderSlewClearanceResults -> updateCarrier3DLayoutAvailability), which
  rebuilds the whole toggle panel from scratch and unchecks everything
  regardless - but added onLegDimensionToggle()/onGroundLayoutToggle()
  calls alongside onSlewCircleToggle() there anyway for consistency, so
  all three toggles are replayed the same way if that rebuild-on-every-
  switch behaviour is ever changed later.
  Verified via Playwright + screenshots (same direct-function-invocation
  workaround as every other 3D toggle test this session, since native
  .click() hangs once a WebGL canvas is actively rendering in this sandbox
  - see earlier entries in this section): LTM 1110 renders all 8 lines
  (2 per leg x 4 legs) with labels matching the extracted data exactly
  (C3 fwd/back 4680mm, C4 fwd/back 3235mm, C1/C4 out from edge 2733mm,
  C2/C3 out from edge 2082mm - matches the (7013/2-1425)=2081.5mm figure
  confirmed earlier against the person's own field-measured number,
  rounding to 2082 here); LTM 1650 (the Y-outrigger design, structurally
  different beam geometry from the other 6 cranes) also renders correctly
  with all four "out from edge" labels reading the expected 3239mm
  (9578/2-1550); unchecking clears the group cleanly with 0 console errors
  throughout both runs.

  UPDATE (same day): two people flagged the same real problem within a
  minute of the ship above landing, both about the longitudinal ("fwd/
  back") line specifically - it ran straight down the carrier's own
  centerline (X=0) from the slew centre to each leg's station, same as an
  OEM drawing's own vertical witness line does on paper, but on a 3D model
  viewed from above that line sits directly UNDER the carrier body, so its
  label was unreadable; and because all four legs' longitudinal lines
  shared that one centerline, a right-side leg's own figure was visually
  indistinguishable from a left-side one stacked on the same line.
  Fixed by moving the longitudinal dimension line off the centerline
  entirely - drawn at X = that leg's own full lateral offset (legXMm, the
  same X the leg itself sits at) instead of X = 0, running from a point
  level with the slew centre out at that offset, straight down/up to the
  leg's own position (which is exactly where legXMm/stationYMm already
  point - no separate "station on the centerline" concept needed any
  more). This puts each leg's own longitudinal line beside the carrier on
  its own respective side - matches how a real OEM drawing actually lays
  the vertical chain out (offset beside the plan view, not through it) -
  clear of the carrier body the whole way, and visually separates left
  from right since the two sides no longer share a line.
  Added a new addWitnessLine() helper (thin, dashed, no end ticks, no
  label - LineDashedMaterial, deliberately styled differently from
  addDimensionLine's solid+tick+label look) so the short reference
  connector from the true slew centre out to each leg's own offset line
  reads as "this is where it's measured from", not as a third measurement
  a crew would need to go verify - same reasoning that already justified
  leaving the centerline-to-edge half-carrier-width gap undrawn in the
  lateral line.
  Verified via Playwright + screenshots (LTM 1110): all four legs now
  render on their own respective side (C1/C2 right, C3/C4 left), every
  label fully clear of the carrier body and readable, dashed witness lines
  visible connecting back to the slew centre; 0 console errors.

  same inside-edge/outside-edge-from-the-crane concept the ground layout
  marks toggle already uses on Crane Layout, but applied here to a pad's
  own CURRENT (possibly shifted) position instead of the crane's fixed
  baseline, since this is the execution tab. Person's own worked example:
  a leg whose own edge-to-pad-centre distance is 4m, with a 2m-wide pad,
  marks as inside edge (closer to the crane) at 4 - 2/2 = 3m out, outside
  edge at 4 + 2/2 = 5m out - lets a crew square a mat up on bare ground
  with just a tape and paint, without needing to first find the pad's own
  centre point.
  New renderMatMarking() function, called at the end of calcCAD() (so it
  stays live across every shift/pad-toggle/pad-size change, same as every
  other calcCAD()-driven result on this tab): for each leg with a pad
  currently toggled on, edgeToCenterMm = abs(leg.x) - carrierWidthB/2
  (leg.x is whatever position the rest of the tab is already showing for
  that leg - baseX, or the shifted newX once a "must move" shift makes
  that the real target - same value the solid "current" pad rectangle is
  already drawn from), then inside/outside = that +/- half the pad's own
  WIDTH (not length - Pad Width is the lateral dimension per its own
  label, "runs inline with carrier length" is Pad Length instead). New
  card (cad-mat-marking-card), hidden whenever no leg currently has a pad
  toggled on, a 4-column table (Outrigger / To Pad Center / Inside Edge /
  Outside Edge) matching the existing "All Four Legs" table's own style -
  deliberately its own separate compact card rather than two more columns
  bolted onto that existing table, which is already dense enough on a
  narrow mobile viewport.
  Verified via Playwright (LTM 1110): single-leg (C2) and all-four-legs
  cases both render correct figures (C2: 2.89m to centre, 1.69m inside,
  4.09m outside, matching abs(4312mm x) - 1425mm halfB = 2887mm exactly);
  unchecking every pad toggle hides the card entirely; 0 console errors.

  UPDATE (same day): Crane Layout is now the sub-tab that opens by default
  when landing on Crane Positioning (was Support Pad Placement). Swapped
  which button carries the "active" class and which panel's inline style
  starts as display:none (cad-panel-layout now visible by default,
  cad-panel-pads now display:none by default) - switchTab()'s own cad-tab
  handling already checked cad-subtab-layout's own "active" class to
  decide whether to lazy-init the Crane Layout sub-tab's crane selector,
  so no logic changes were needed there, just the two static defaults.
  Verified via Playwright: landing on Crane Positioning now shows Crane
  Layout's panel immediately (pads panel display:none, layout panel
  unset/visible), sc-crane already populated with all 7 cranes; 0 console
  errors.

  UPDATE (same day): person caught a real accuracy bug in the Bog Mat
  Marking table above, within minutes of it shipping - LTM 1110's C2 and
  C3 read meaningfully different "to pad center" distances (2.89m vs
  2.10m) despite being a mirrored pair with an identical physical
  outrigger span, so they should always read the same when unshifted.
  Root cause: the table was built from leg.x, which comes from
  cadFleetData's r/angle (r*sin(angle)) - fine for its EXISTING jobs
  (clash detection, canvas/3D drawing, the "All Four Legs" table), all of
  which only care about the overall reach, but cadFleetData's angle is
  only stored to one decimal place, which isn't precise enough to isolate
  the LATERAL component alone - exactly the same class of imprecision this
  session already worked around once before, when GROUND_LAYOUT_DATA had
  to be extracted fresh from each crane's own OEM dimension chain instead
  of being derived from r/angle (see the "ground layout marks" entries
  above).
  Fix: added a second, accurate lateral-only position (accurateX) to each
  mappedOutriggers entry, computed via a new accurateLateralX() helper -
  side * (span/2), where span is GROUND_LAYOUT_DATA[modelKey].frontSpan or
  .rearSpan (exact, OEM-sourced, identical for both legs of a mirrored
  pair) rather than r*sin(angle). Deliberately does NOT touch leg.x itself
  or any of its existing consumers - kept as a parallel field so the pad
  drawn on the canvas/3D view is unchanged, only the marking table's own
  calculation moved onto the more accurate figure. Mirrors the exact same
  required-to-move/shift logic plotX already uses (accuratePlotX =
  isRequired ? accurateBaseX + shiftX : accurateBaseX) so it still
  responds correctly to a lateral shift - verified a +3m shift moves only
  the two legs actually required to move (by the full 3m, exactly), while
  the two legs that aren't required stay at their own unchanged figure,
  same as the "All Four Legs" table's own optional-vs-required behavior.
  renderMatMarking() updated to read leg.accurateX instead of leg.x; card's
  own on-page disclaimer text corrected to describe this instead of
  claiming it matches the drawn pad's own position.
  Verified via Playwright (LTM 1110, all four pads on): unshifted, C2 and
  C3 now both read 2.08m/0.88m/3.28m exactly (previously 2.89m vs 2.10m to
  centre), C1 and C4 both read 2.73m/1.53m/3.93m exactly; a +3m lateral
  shift correctly moves only the two required legs by the full 3m each,
  leaving the other two unchanged; 0 console errors.

  UPDATE (same day): two more requests landed together - person asked
  whether an earlier message about the Visual Site Plan card had come
  through (it hadn't been actioned yet), plus a new request for a 3D
  toggle showing the bog mat inside/outside edge marks.
  First: Visual Site Plan (the 2D canvas card on Support Pad Placement) is
  now hidden by default behind a "Show Visual Site Plan" button, same
  pattern as the 3D Carrier Preview card right below it - keeps the panel
  shorter to scroll past when the canvas isn't wanted. New toggleSitePlan()
  wraps the toolbar/canvas/legend in a single #cad-siteplan-wrap, toggling
  its display and re-running calcCAD() on reveal - needed because
  drawCADCanvas() sizes itself off canvas.parentElement.clientWidth/Height,
  which reads 0 the whole time the wrap is display:none, so a plain
  re-show without a fresh draw would leave the canvas blank until the next
  unrelated input change.
  Second: added a "Show mat edge marks" checkbox to Support Pad
  Placement's own 3D toolbar - the 3D counterpart of the Bog Mat Marking
  table, drawing both the inside-edge and outside-edge dimension lines
  directly on the model for every leg that currently has a pad toggled
  on. New computeMatMarkingData() helper factored out of renderMatMarking()
  so the table and the new 3D toggle read from exactly one shared
  calculation - can't quietly drift apart from each other. Wired through
  the same fifth pending/context map pair pattern as every other toggle on
  this shared canvas (pendingMatEdgeMarks/pendingMatEdgeContext,
  matEdgeGroup, clearMatEdgeMarks() - included in clearScene(), sceneBox(),
  both branches of __carrier3dActivate()), plus a self-heal call in
  activateCarrier3D() alongside syncCarrier3DOutriggers() so switching
  away to Crane Layout and back doesn't leave the marks blank even though
  the checkbox still shows checked.
  First attempt drew both lines starting at the SAME point (the carrier's
  own edge) running out along the exact same ray - inside line fully
  contained within outside line's own span - with each label placed at its
  own line's midpoint, same as every other dimension line in this app.
  Looked right in isolation but wasn't: screenshotted and found the two
  labels overlapping into unreadable stacked text at any reasonable zoom,
  since the label sprites are wide relative to how close together their
  two midpoints land when collinear. Fixed by offsetting the INSIDE line
  onto its own separate parallel line (400mm toward the vehicle's own
  centre, sign-flipped so it's genuinely "inward" for both front and rear
  legs) rather than collinear with the outside line - matches how the real
  OEM dimension chains already read throughout this app: nested
  measurements drawn as separate parallel lines, not chained end to end
  along one. A thin dashed addWitnessLine() connects the true edge point
  to where the offset inside line begins, same visual language as the
  ground layout marks' own witness connector.
  Verified via Playwright (LTM 1110, all four pads on): Visual Site Plan
  starts hidden, canvas is correctly sized (non-zero) once shown; 3D mat
  edge marks render for all four legs with both labels per leg fully
  legible and non-overlapping, figures matching the 2D table exactly
  (C3: 3282mm outside / 882mm inside); 0 console errors throughout.

  UPDATE (same day): person reported ground layout marks "leaking" from
  Crane Layout into Support Pad Placement's own 3D view - reproduced
  exactly: open Crane Layout's 3D, turn on ground layout marks, switch to
  Support Pad Placement, open ITS 3D (mat edges unchecked, no pads
  toggled) - the ground layout lines showed up anyway.
  Root cause, found in __carrier3dActivate() (vendor/carrier3d.js): the
  function's own comment already said the right thing - "nothing needs
  replaying from here for the synchronous case; only the pendingX replay
  ... for a model still mid-fetch is this function's own responsibility" -
  but the CODE didn't match the comment. The `if (cached)` branch (i.e.
  the model's already loaded, no async fetch needed) unconditionally
  replayed every pendingX map right after the "different card took
  ownership" branch had JUST cleared those exact groups a few lines
  earlier - directly undoing its own clearing. Whichever overlay the
  PREVIOUS card had toggled on would silently reappear on whichever card
  just took over, even though that card owns no matching checkbox and its
  own toggle read unchecked. A genuinely old bug (same shape existed for
  slew circles/leg dimensions since the very first dual-3D-card build,
  see the "single-3D-card misunderstanding" fix earlier in this file) -
  just not surfaced/reported until ground layout marks made it visible.
  Fix: removed the pendingX replay entirely from the `if (cached)` branch
  (all five: sync, slewCircles, legDimensions, groundLayoutMarks,
  matEdgeMarks), keeping it ONLY in the async loadModel() callback branch,
  where it's genuinely needed - a model still being fetched over the
  network when a toggle fires can't apply immediately (each toggle
  setter's own `if (!root) return` early-exit confirms this), so must be
  replayed once loading finishes. Confirmed safe to remove from the cached
  branch because every one of the five toggle setter functions
  (__carrier3dSyncOutriggers, __carrier3dSetSlewCircles,
  __carrier3dSetLegDimensions, __carrier3dSetGroundLayoutMarks,
  __carrier3dSetMatEdgeMarks) already has its own "apply immediately if
  root is cached and currentModelKey matches" fallback built in - which is
  exactly what each card's own activate() wrapper in index.html calls
  synchronously right after __carrier3dActivate returns. The replay in the
  cached branch was therefore not just wrong but entirely redundant.
  Verified via Playwright, both directions: Layout's ground layout marks
  no longer appear on Pad Placement's 3D view after switching (unchecked
  mat-edges box, no pads toggled, screenshot clean); Pad Placement's own
  outrigger pad markers + mat edge marks likewise don't leak onto Layout's
  view when switching the other way; switching back to Layout afterward
  still renders its own toggles correctly when re-checked - 0 console
  errors throughout.

  UPDATE (same day): three smaller fixes landed together.
  1. "All Four Legs — Reference Frames" table is now hidden by default
  behind a "Show All Four Legs" button, same collapsed-by-default pattern
  as Visual Site Plan and the 3D Carrier Preview cards. New
  toggleLegsTable() - simpler than toggleSitePlan() since the table
  content itself (#cad-results) is plain HTML already kept correctly
  up to date by calcCAD() regardless of visibility, so no recalc-on-show
  step is needed, just a display toggle.
  2. Fixed a real staleness bug: changing Pad Width or Pad Length only
  updated the 2D Bog Mat Marking table - the 3D mat edge marks kept
  showing the OLD figures until the 3D view was closed and reopened
  (which happened to mask the bug, since activateCarrier3D()'s own
  self-heal call re-triggers onMatEdgeToggle()). calcCAD() already called
  syncCarrier3DOutriggers() on every recalc to keep the pad MESHES
  themselves live, but never called onMatEdgeToggle() to keep the
  DIMENSION LINES live too - added that call alongside it.
  3. Bog Mat Marking table now reads in mm instead of m - person's own
  reasoning: a tape measure in Australia reads in mm, and this table
  exists specifically to be read directly off a tape on site. Matches the
  unit already used by every other 3D dimension label in the app (ground
  layout marks, mat edge marks, clearance measurement) - only the 2D
  table itself was still in metres.

  3582mm" / "C2 inside: 582mm") without touching the 3D toggle; table

17. HEADER SHARE BUTTON + QR CODE
  New icon button in the header, beside the existing update-check refresh
  icon - opens a small panel with a QR code, the app's own URL as plain
  text, a "Copy Link" button, and a native Share… button (only shown when
  navigator.share exists) - lets a crew member hand the app straight to
  someone else on site (open camera, scan, tap the link) without them
  having to type a URL in.
  Person asked for "a share button like you did for my norwest forms
  pwa" - that other project isn't in this session's repo scope, so rather
  than guess at its exact implementation, asked two quick clarifying
  questions instead: how to source the QR image (their own pasted image,
  which this session has no file-path access to and no tool to extract
  raw bytes from a chat-inline image; vs generating a fresh one), and what
  the button should actually do. Answered: generate the QR code fresh
  (encoding the app's own URL, not a static image), header icon opening a
  small panel with QR + Copy Link + native share.
  QR generation: vendored vendor/qrcode.js - Kazuhiko Arase's
  "qrcode-generator" (MIT-licensed, dependency-free, ~56KB, no build
  step) - same "no CDN, no external service" approach already used for
  three.js/GLTFLoader/DRACOLoader elsewhere in this app, for the same
  reason: the app needs to keep working offline once cached, and a QR
  code generated locally from window.location is always correct - it
  can't silently point at a stale image someone forgot to regenerate
  after the URL changed, the way a static uploaded QR image could.
  Loaded lazily via a manually-created <script> tag (not dynamic
  import() - qrcode.js is a plain classic script, not an ES module) the
  first time the share panel opens, rather than paying its ~56KB on every
  page load for a feature most visits never touch - same lazy-load
  reasoning as carrier3d.js/cwt3d.js, just via a different mechanism since
  the underlying file isn't a module.
  qrcode(0, 'M') - type 0 lets the library auto-pick the smallest QR
  version that fits the URL (confirmed by reading the library's own
  make() - it only auto-detects when typeNumber < 1, tries versions 1-39
  until the data fits), error-correction level M (15% recovery, the
  standard middle-ground default). createSvgTag({ scalable: true }) emits
  a viewBox-only SVG (no fixed pixel size) so the panel's own CSS
  (#share-qr-wrap svg { width: 180px; height: 180px }) controls the
  on-screen size instead.
  shareUrl() strips any query string/hash from window.location - the
  app's own canonical URL is always the meaningful thing to share, not
  whatever incidental state (a scroll anchor, a future deep link) happens
  to be in the address bar at the moment the panel opens.
  Copy Link reuses navigator.clipboard.writeText() where available,
  falling back to the classic hidden-textarea + document.execCommand('copy')
  trick for older/unusual browsers that lack the Clipboard API entirely -
  and reuses the app's own existing showToast() (originally built for the
  update-check button) for the "Link copied." confirmation, rather than
  inventing a second toast mechanism.
  Panel is a simple fixed-position card with a click-to-dismiss backdrop
  (#share-backdrop), not a full modal library - consistent with how
  small, occasional-use UI elsewhere in this app (the update-check toast,
  the update-available banner) is built directly rather than reached for
  a dependency.
  Verified via Playwright: panel opens with a valid QR SVG rendered,
  correct URL shown as text, native-share button correctly hidden in a
  browser context without navigator.share, backdrop click closes the
  panel; Copy Link (tested with clipboard permissions granted) copies the
  exact URL and shows the "Link copied." toast; header layout checked at
  a 360px-wide viewport - QR button, refresh button and the wordmark all
  sit clear of each other with no overlap; 0 console errors throughout.

18. NAV REORDER + NEW CHAIN SHORTENING TAB
  Two requests: move Crane Positioning to sit between Load Chart Finder
  and Driving with Equipment in Place (was between Minimum Hook Block
  Weights and Rope Retentioning); add a brand new tab, Chain Shortening,
  between Minimum Hook Block Weights and Rope Retentioning.
  Nav order is purely a DOM order of the .nav-tabs <button> elements -
  switchTab() shows/hides tab-content divs by id regardless of button
  order, and confirmed the tab-content divs themselves were ALREADY in a
  different physical order than the nav buttons even before this change
  (rope-tab's own <div> sits earlier in the file than hook-tab's, despite
  hook-tab appearing first in the nav) - so reordering the buttons alone
  was sufficient, no risk of breaking anything by leaving the content
  divs' own file order untouched.
  Chain Shortening: person provided their own reference spreadsheet
  (Chain_Length_Calculator.xlsx) to match exactly. Read it directly via
  openpyxl (both the formulas themselves and their computed example
  values, to cross-check): a lookup table of diameter (mm) -> pitch per
  link (mm) - 8->24, 10->30, 13->39, 16->48, 19->57, 22->66, 26->78 (every
  row happens to be exactly 3x the diameter for this chain range, but kept
  as an explicit table matching the source sheet rather than assumed as a
  general multiplier, in case that stops holding for a diameter added
  later) - plus the shortening formula itself: Amount to Shorten =
  Current - Desired; Links to Remove = ROUND(Amount to Shorten / Pitch per
  Link, 0); Actual Finished Length = Current - (Links to Remove x Pitch);
  Difference from Target = Actual Finished Length - Desired. The rounding
  step means a chain often can't land exactly on the requested desired
  length (can't remove a fractional link) - Difference from Target is
  surfaced directly in the result table rather than hidden, so a person
  isn't surprised the finished chain is a few mm off what they typed in.
  Person's own two example phrasings ("I need to remove X amount, how
  many links" and "I have an 8m chain, I need it to be 5.6m, how much to
  remove") both reduce to the exact same current+desired -> links flow the
  reference sheet already implements, so built the one mode that matches
  the sheet exactly rather than two separate input paths.
  New CHAIN_PITCH_TABLE constant + calcChainShortening()/initChainTab()
  functions, in the "TAB 2B" spot in the JS (mirroring the "TAB 3B: CRANE
  SETUP" naming precedent already used for the earlier Crane Layout
  sub-tab, since this is also a small satellite feature bolted on beside
  an existing numbered tab rather than a completely new "TAB 8"), wired
  into the page's own init block alongside the other tabs' startup calc
  calls.
  Verified via Playwright: nav renders in the exact requested order
  (Reeving Plans, CWT Combinations, Load Chart Finder, Crane Positioning,
  Driving with Equipment in Place, Minimum Hook Block Weights, Chain
  Shortening, Rope Retentioning); diameter dropdown lists all 7 sizes;
  default inputs (8mm, 6000mm, 5500mm) reproduce the reference sheet's own
  worked example exactly (21 links, 5496mm actual, -4mm difference); the
  person's own 8m->5.6m example at 8mm gives a clean 100 links / 5600mm
  exact / 0mm difference; a full click-through of all 8 tabs afterward
  came back with 0 console errors throughout.

18. 3D CARRIER PREVIEW: FIT VIEW SCROLL + BOG MAT GHOSTING/DIMENSIONS
  Batch of small 3D-view refinements, iterated with the person over several
  rounds before landing, held as local commits (not pushed) until the
  whole batch was settled - see each commit's own message for the
  blow-by-blow.
  Fit View (both 3D cards - Support Pad Placement and Crane Layout) now
  smooth-scrolls the card itself to the vertical centre of the viewport in
  addition to reframing the camera, via document.getElementById(
  currentWrapId).scrollIntoView({block:'center'}) - carrier3d.js already
  tracks currentWrapId internally (which of the two cards currently owns
  the shared canvas), so no new plumbing needed. Confirmed via Playwright:
  wrap's own bounding-box midpoint lands exactly on the viewport's
  midpoint after clicking Fit View.
  Bog mat pads (the "current" pad mesh drawn per leg in applySync) went
  from a near-solid 0.9 fill opacity down to 0.12, with the outline
  switched from solid to dashed (matching the "if moved" ghost pad's own
  established dashed convention) - the mat edge dimension lines drawn
  through the pad's own footprint were getting buried under the old
  near-opaque fill. The dashed outline itself then needed its own opacity
  set explicitly (0.45) in a follow-up round - it had no transparent/
  opacity property at all, so it defaulted fully opaque and was
  out-competing the dimension lines for attention even after the fill
  itself was ghosted down.
  Mat edge dimension lines (inside/outside per leg) went through three
  attempts before landing: (1) offset onto parallel lines by a flat
  400mm to stop their labels overlapping near the mat's inner corner: (2)
  misread a follow-up sketch as asking for the whole line to run vertical
  along the mat's outer edge - corrected by the person: "I want the txt
  moved not the line showing what the txt represents", reverted to the
  original line geometry with only the label's own position along it
  biased via a new labelT param on addDimensionLine (0=start, 1=end,
  default 0.5); (3) person's actual reference, an OEM outrigger dimension
  sheet photo showing two lines offset onto the object's own two opposite
  ends for separation, not chained through the same station - final
  version offsets inside/outside onto their own parallel lines at roughly
  the mat's own near/far ends (0.4x pad length each direction, leaving a
  small margin off the true corner) rather than a flat 400mm or a label
  trick - real geometric separation, matching the reference photo's own
  style.
  Verified via Playwright/screenshot at each round; final state: 0 console
  errors across a full click-through of all 8 tabs, dimension lines
  visually confirmed sitting at the mat's own two ends with a clean gap,
  ghost pad fill and outline both visibly faded below the dimension
  lines' own full opacity.

19. BUG FIX: "MUST MOVE" STATUS FLIPPING BASED ON PAD CHECKBOX STATE
  Person's own report: with a shift entered and only C4's pad toggled on,
  then Compare old/new turned on, then C1/C2/C3 toggled on - the mat edge
  marks for C1/C2/C3 showed the FUTURE (shifted target) position instead
  of the current one.
  Root cause: calcCAD()'s own isRequired/clash determination
  (checkClash(relX, relY, fp, legPad) and checkClash(newX, newY, fp,
  legPad)) was passed legPad - which is null whenever that SAME leg's own
  pad checkbox happens to be unticked, falling back to a bare-point clash
  test (clashesWithFootprint) instead of the real pad-sized rectangle test
  (padClashesWithFootprint). Toggling a leg's own pad checkbox ON could
  therefore flip that same leg from "optional" to "MUST MOVE" with every
  other input unchanged - confirmed directly: shiftX=1.867m, shiftY=0 on
  the LTM 1130 put C1 exactly in the boundary zone where its bare contact
  point sits clear of the chassis footprint but a 2.4x3.5m pad centred on
  that same point would reach into it - C1 read "optional" with its pad
  off and "MUST MOVE" with it on, no other change. Since accuratePlotX
  (what Bog Mat Marking reads) jumps to the shifted target position
  whenever isRequired is true, this pad-toggle flip was exactly what
  surfaced as the person's bug.
  Fix: the clash tests now always use sharedPadDims() (the shared pad
  size entered above, same for all four legs) rather than legPad -
  whether a mat's own footprint would clash with the chassis is a
  physical fact about the shift and the shared pad size, not something
  that should depend on which leg's checkbox happens to be ticked at the
  moment. legPad itself is untouched for its other job (what's actually
  drawn/shown per leg).
  Verified: reproduced the exact flip on the pre-fix code (C1 optional ->
  MUST MOVE from toggling only its own checkbox, shiftX=1.867m), then
  confirmed identical, order-independent results after the fix - same
  Bog Mat Marking figures for C1-C4 whether pads are toggled on before or
  after Compare old/new, and the same MUST MOVE status regardless of that
  leg's own pad checkbox state. Full click-through of all 8 tabs
  afterward: 0 console errors.

20. TARGET MAT MARKS (SECOND TOGGLE, "IF MOVED" MAT DIMENSIONS)
  Follow-on from the previous entry's bug fix. Person's own reaction to
  seeing shifted-position figures leak into the single existing toggle:
  "I actually wouldn't mind these but it would need to be toggled under
  another button like current mats marks and future mats marks". Renamed
  the existing toggle "Show mat edge marks" -> "Mat Marks" -> (this round)
  "Current Mat Marks", and added a genuinely new second toggle, "Target
  Mat Marks", showing the same inside/outside dimension lines computed
  against each optional leg's TARGET (if-moved) position instead of its
  current one.
  calcCAD() gained accurateTargetX (accurateBaseX + shiftX, unconditional -
  unlike accuratePlotX/accurateX, which only jumps to the shifted spot
  once a leg is actually required). computeMatMarkingData() took a
  useTarget parameter: false reads leg.accurateX/leg.y (unchanged,
  existing behaviour); true reads leg.accurateTargetX/leg.movedY and
  filters out any leg where target equals current (i.e. required legs -
  their current position already IS their target, so showing it twice
  under both toggles would be pure duplication) AND any leg flagged
  leg.clash (the "All Four Legs" table's own NOT POSSIBLE) - moving a pad
  there would clip the chassis, and its own edgeToCenterMm can come out
  smaller than half the pad's width, making insideMm go negative. That's
  technically correct (the pad really would overlap the crane) but not a
  real marking figure a crew could ever act on, so it's excluded outright
  rather than shown with a confusing negative number. Confirmed via
  Playwright against LTM 1130 at two different shifts: one where a
  target's own inside figure came out -742mm before this filter (leg
  correctly excluded after); one with a genuinely valid target (C2,
  optional, not clashing) that renders correctly.
  carrier3d.js: matEdgeGroup (current) and a new targetMatEdgeGroup
  (target) are two fully independent groups/clear functions/pending-
  replay maps, mirroring every other toggle already on this shared canvas
  (slew circles, leg dimensions, ground layout marks) - each checkbox
  clears and redraws only its own group. addDimensionLine gained an
  optional `dashed` parameter (LineDashedMaterial instead of
  LineBasicMaterial for the line itself; the tiny end ticks stay solid
  regardless - dashing something that small reads as broken, not
  "future") - Target's lines render dashed, Current's stay solid, same
  dashed = "if moved" convention already established by the ghost pad
  boxes elsewhere in this app, so both toggles read as visually distinct
  even when on at once for neighbouring legs.
  Verified via Playwright: a scenario with one required leg (target
  correctly absent, current already shows its shifted figure) and one
  valid-target optional leg (both Current and Target render, solid vs
  dashed clearly distinguishable, labels land in genuinely different
  screen positions since the leg's own current vs target Y differ);
  live-updates confirmed by changing Pad Width with both toggles open -
  target marks correctly disappeared once the wider pad made that same
  leg go from optional to required; switching to Crane Layout and back to
  Support Pad Placement with both toggles left on self-heals correctly (no
  duplicate/leaked groups); 0 console errors across the full scenario set
  and a full click-through of all 8 tabs.

21. TARGET MAT MARKS: EMPTY-STATE HINT
  Person's report: with a large shift ("+4 right"), Current Mat Marks
  showed the shifted figures, then unchecking it and checking Target Mat
  Marks "does nothing". Reproduced on LTM 1130 with shiftX=4m, shiftY=0:
  C1/C2 both come out required (their current position already IS their
  target, correctly excluded from Target per the previous entry's design),
  and C3/C4 - optional - both come out leg.clash=true (their own "if
  moved anyway" spot, accurateTargetX around 250mm against a chassis
  half-width of 1425mm, would land the mat well under the chassis), also
  correctly excluded. So at this particular shift, every single leg
  legitimately has nothing valid to show under Target - not a broken
  toggle, just a real "nothing possible right now" outcome that happened
  to be silent.
  Added a small hint div (#carrier-3d-target-empty-hint, same
  cwt-zoom-hint styling already used for the "drag to rotate" hint under
  the 3D canvas) that appears specifically when Target Mat Marks is
  checked AND buildMatEdgeMarks(true) comes back empty - explaining why,
  rather than leaving an unexplained blank toggle a person can't
  distinguish from a bug. Hidden the rest of the time (Target unchecked,
  or Target checked with real marks to show).
  Verified via Playwright: hint hidden with only Current checked; hint
  visible (confirmed via getComputedStyle) with only Target checked at
  the +4m/0m shift that produces zero valid targets; hint disappears
  again once the shift changes to one with a genuinely valid target
  (1.5m/1.5m, C2's own case from the previous entry); full click-through
  of all 8 tabs afterward: 0 console errors.

22. MAT MARKS REDESIGN: CURRENT NEVER JUMPS, TARGET COVERS EVERY LEG
  Person's clarified spec, after seeing Current Mat Marks jump to the
  shifted figures for a required leg: "current mat marks... show were
  they current or should sit from its current position and never change
  just being able to toggle it on and off" - and for Target: "if im
  moving +4 to the right I want this to show me from the current edge of
  crane where the inside and outside will be of the C1/C2 mats" (C1/C2
  being required legs in their own example, not just optional ones) -
  "if the mats will land in the future pos underneath the current pos of
  the carrier there is no need to give dimension as they wont be
  useful". Purpose, confirmed with the person: helps with using a tape
  measure and line marking paint on the ground.
  This superseded both the original Bog Mat Marking design (deliberately
  showed a required leg's shifted position under "current", reasoning
  being that's where the crew should actually place it) and the previous
  entry's Target filter (excluded every required leg, since under the OLD
  design current==target for them).
  calcCAD(): accurateX (the field Current Mat Marks reads) no longer ever
  jumps to the shifted spot for a required leg - it's now unconditionally
  accurateBaseX, the leg's true physical position, full stop. Added
  currentY (unconditionally baseY, same "never jumps" treatment for the Y
  component - leg.y/plotY themselves are untouched, since those still
  correctly drive where the leg/pad marker itself gets DRAWN on the
  canvas/3D view, a separate concern from what the marking figures
  should read). accurateTargetX was already unconditional from the
  previous entry and needed no change.
  computeMatMarkingData()'s target filter dropped the "required legs
  never appear" exclusion entirely (that was only ever a side effect of
  the old jumping accurateX) - now only excludes a leg with literally no
  shift of its own (target trivially equals current) or one flagged
  leg.clash (NOT POSSIBLE - would land under the current chassis).
  Confirmed via Playwright on LTM 1130, shiftX=4m/shiftY=0 (C1/C2
  required, C3/C4 optional-but-clashing): Current Mat Marks' own numbers
  for all 4 legs came back byte-identical between shift=0 and shift=4m/0
  (proving it truly never changes); Target now correctly includes C1 and
  C2 (previously excluded), still correctly excludes C3/C4.
  New clutter this surfaced: a leg's target Y equals its current Y
  whenever the shift is pure sideways (very common - the person's own
  "+4 right" example), which put Current's and Target's inside/outside
  lines for the SAME leg on the exact same row, garbling their labels
  together (screenshotted: "C2 outside: C2 outside: 7525mm", genuinely
  unreadable overlapping text). Fixed in carrier3d.js's drawMatEdgeMarks:
  Target's whole inside/outside pair now gets pushed an extra 0.9x pad
  length further out along Y before applying its own inside/outside
  split, unconditionally (simpler than only applying it when the two
  actually coincide, and harmless when they already differ) - Current
  and Target now always land in clearly separate bands. Re-screenshotted
  to confirm: fully legible now, no character overlap.
  Full click-through of all 8 tabs, plus a spot-check at an extreme
  10m shift (nothing required or clashing - target correctly non-empty
  for all 4 legs, hint correctly hidden): 0 console errors throughout.

23. LTM 1160 UK VARIANT: REAL TAP-DIAGRAM, BUILT FROM THE PERSON'S OWN DXF
  Person wanted the UK-market counterweight variant of the 160t crane
  added. No OEM combination chart exists for it (the document that would
  say which plate subsets are actually approved) - only a photo of the
  exploded-view diagram with weights labelled per piece (5t/6.5t/5t top
  plates, 10t/14t/10t base blocks, 3.5t base spacer - 54t total across 7
  pieces), and a DXF the person traced themselves in AutoCAD LT (a closed
  polyline around each piece's own silhouette, over that same photo).
  Went through several false starts before landing on the right shape:
  (1) first read "map to total weight calculator" as wiring into Load
  Chart Finder - wrong, person clarified "nothing to do with load
  charts, im only trying to build a CWT combo"; (2) built a plain tick-
  list (no diagram) reasoning that no approved-combination chart meant no
  diagram was possible - also wrong, person asked "why isn't there an
  image I can click", clarifying the DXF boxes were always meant to
  BECOME the clickable diagram, not just back a total-weight tally;
  (3) the DXF's own linked reference image (an IMAGE entity pointing to
  a local Windows path) never transferred, and pasting the photo inline
  in chat doesn't give file access either - only an @-attached file does,
  or a Nextcloud share fetched via WebDAV (see the next entry) - that's
  how the actual photo file finally arrived.
  The payoff: the DXF's own IMAGE entity records the EXACT insert point
  and per-pixel u/v vectors used when the photo was placed in AutoCAD
  (confirmed image_size (532,458) matches the photo's real pixel
  dimensions exactly), so every traced vertex converts to an exact photo-
  pixel coordinate with zero guessing: px = (wx-insert.x)/u_pixel.x,
  py = image_size.y - (wy-insert.y)/v_pixel.y. Parsed via ezdxf: all 158
  LINE entities on the 'boxes' layer form exactly 7 closed loops (all
  158 vertices degree-2 in the adjacency graph - simple disjoint
  polygons, no branching), one per piece. Rendered all 7 outlines back
  over the actual photo at their computed coordinates as a verification
  step before writing any hotspot data - every outline landed exactly on
  its own piece's silhouette, and reading the weight label already
  printed inside each outline gave an unambiguous, inspection-verified
  id->weight mapping (no manual matching or guessing which shape is
  which).
  Reused the diagram/hotspot-tap/zoom machinery every other crane already
  has here, rather than a bespoke UI: COUNTERWEIGHT_DATA['1160'] gained a
  ukVariant block with the same shape as a top-level crane entry
  (diagramImage/diagramWidth/diagramHeight/components[with hotspots]),
  but combos: [] (deliberately empty - no OEM chart exists, so nothing
  is ever flagged as an approved combination, matching the pre-existing
  "not an OEM-approved combination" warning path every crane already has
  for an unmatched selection). New cwtData(modelKey) helper is the single
  point every CWT function now reads crane data through - resolves to
  ukVariant's own components/diagram when cwtVariant==='uk', the regular
  top-level entry otherwise - so cwtComponentMap/cwtFindMatchingCombo/
  populateCwtComboSelect/cycleCwtSlotGroup/renderCwtTab etc. all work
  unmodified for the UK variant. populateCwtComboSelect() hides the
  "Jump to Known Combination" card whenever combos is empty (UK), rather
  than showing a pointless empty dropdown. Global | UK toggle only
  appears when the selected crane's data carries a ukVariant key
  (currently just 1160) - person's own request to keep it inside the
  existing crane entry, not a new crane in the dropdown.
  Diagram image saved as counterweight/img/ltm1160-cwt-uk-exploded.png
  (the person's own reference photo, not OEM artwork - flagged as such
  in figRef, same convention as the 1300 global variant's own re-drawn
  diagram).
  Verified via Playwright: toggle only appears for 1160; all 7 hotspot
  aria-labels read the correct name/weight; tapping all 7 (via direct
  toggleCwtPlate() calls, since a synthetic bounding-box-center click
  landed in the spacer piece's own concave notch between its two pins -
  a test-script artifact, not a real interaction, since real SVG polygon
  hit-testing follows the actual fill shape, not its bounding box) totals
  exactly 54.0t matching the photo; "Jump to Known Combination" correctly
  hidden for UK, correctly shown for Global; switching Global<->UK and
  crane<->crane always resets to Global with no stale selection; existing
  Global/EU diagram-tap flow for 1160 (and a spot-check across every
  other crane) completely unaffected; 0 console errors across a full
  click-through of all 8 tabs.

24. PERSON'S NEXTCLOUD - HOW TO PULL A FILE FROM "MY NEXTCLOUD"
  Person's own reference point whenever they say "my Nextcloud" or "same
  link I've pasted in the past": local.idull.au, the same host already
  referenced in sections 14/15/18/20 for the original carrier/counterweight
  exports and OEM manual PDFs. A share link looks like
  https://local.idull.au/index.php/s/<TOKEN> - the token is a public
  share token, used as the WebDAV basic-auth username with an empty
  password: `curl -u "<TOKEN>:" https://local.idull.au/public.php/webdav/`
  lists (PROPFIND) or downloads (GET) whatever that particular share
  exposes - a single file, or a whole folder tree (browse with `curl -u
  "<TOKEN>:" ... -X PROPFIND -H "Depth: 1"`, fetch a specific path by
  appending it, URL-encoding spaces as %20). This session's own token
  (Twn8N4Z7xb3QiEk) exposed the 1160 UK reference photo directly at the
  share root plus Carrier/ and Counterweight/ sibling folders - each new
  share the person sends will have its own fresh token, but the pattern
  (extract token from the /s/ URL, WebDAV GET/PROPFIND with that token as
  the basic-auth username) is the constant part worth remembering. This
  particular share's token was scoped narrowly to just what that
  conversation needed - a later, broader share (section 25) turned out to
  expose the person's whole Nextcloud root instead, so "my Nextcloud"
  resolves to whatever token they most recently sent, not a fixed token
  value - always re-derive from the latest /s/<TOKEN> link they give.

25. LTM 1300-6.2 UK VARIANT - REAL OEM DATA, NOT AN ESTIMATE
  Same starting point as 1160 UK (methodology.txt 23): person supplied a
  DXF (10 piece outlines traced in AutoCAD LT over their own reference
  photo, boxes layer) plus the photo itself (824x442, GB flag "96 t"
  badge, on Nextcloud). Person's explicit scope instruction: move the
  existing (at the time PROVISIONAL) COUNTERWEIGHT_DATA['1300'] entry to
  become the "Global" variant as-is, and add this DXF+photo data as a new
  "UK" ukVariant sibling - same cwtData()/Global|UK toggle architecture
  1160 UK already built, no JS changes needed, just data.
  Loop extraction hit a new failure mode 1160 didn't have: a naive
  vertex-based or edge-based walk of the 254 LINE segments only found
  9 loops instead of 10, because the receptacle (13t) and counterweight-7
  (3t) outlines share a real boundary vertex where they touch - any
  single-path walk that greedily picks "the first unvisited edge" at that
  vertex can wander from one piece's outline into the other's instead of
  closing its own loop. Fixed by using shapely's polygonize() (planar
  subdivision from the full set of line segments, via
  shapely.ops.unary_union + polygonize) instead of a hand-rolled graph
  walk - correctly resolves all 10 closed faces regardless of shared
  vertices, no special-casing needed. World->pixel transform (DXF's own
  IMAGE entity: insert (0,0,0), u_pixel/v_pixel 0.2646202699126753,
  image_size 824x442, exact match to the real photo's pixel dimensions)
  reused unchanged from the 1160 method. Rendered all 10 polygons over
  the actual photo as a verification step (same as 1160) - every outline
  landed exactly on its own piece, confirming the shapely fix.
  Then the person asked to look up "the ltm1300 operator manual, chapter
  4.07 counterweight and combinations" on their Nextcloud - a different,
  broader share this time (a whole personal Nextcloud root, not a scoped
  folder - see the note appended to section 24), containing a full
  2167-page Liebherr operating-instructions PDF for this exact crane
  under CRANES/Liebherr cranes/LTM1300-6.2/071845_LTM_1300-6.2_v100_en/
  Operating_instructions_Crane/. Chapter 4.07 "Counterweight" (pages
  931-940) turned out to be the SAME chapter the already-shipped Global
  entry's manualUrl/manualPage:931 was already pointing at - but nobody
  had actually read it against the Global entry's own (admittedly
  PROVISIONAL) combo data before now. Reading it: "1 Overview of
  counterweight" lists the exact 10 physical pieces (1x receptacle plate
  1, 1x counterweight 7, 1x counterweight plate 2, 2x counterweight plate
  3, 1x counterweight plate 4, 2x auxiliary ballast 5, 2x auxiliary
  ballast 6) matching the person's 10 DXF loops and photo one-for-one,
  and "3 Counterweight combinations" / "3.1 Special ballast 66 t and
  special ballast 86 t" gives the full confirmed tier table: 0/13/16/26/
  36/46/56/66/76/86/96t, where 66t and 86t are "special ballast" tiers
  that use three spacer pins in place of the second counterweight-plate-3
  copy rather than an actual plate. This is a genuinely different base
  pair (receptacle 13t + counterweight-7 3t = 16t combined) than the
  existing Global entry's own base pair (receptacle 6t + Plate 5 10t =
  16t combined, per that entry's own comments "Unique to this
  (global/export) variant") - not a contradiction, just different
  physical hardware between the export/Global crane and this UK one, so
  the Global entry was left completely untouched as instructed.
  ukVariant.combos is therefore NOT empty like 1160 UK's was (no chart
  existed there) - it's the manual's own tier table, transcribed exactly,
  with manualUrl/manualPage:931 carried over onto the ukVariant block
  too since it's confirmably the same document/page. sourceNote reflects
  this is confirmed OEM data, not an estimate.
  Inner-vs-outer wing pairing (aux5 = inner pair closer to the main
  stack, aux6 = outer pair) follows the same left+right-symmetric
  convention as the Global entry's own aux5/aux6, matched by stack
  position since the manual's chart doesn't distinguish inner/outer by
  name, only by weight (all 4 wing pieces are 10t).
  Image saved as counterweight/img/ltm1300-cwt-uk-exploded.png, added to
  sw.js APP_SHELL. model3d intentionally omitted from ukVariant - no 3D
  model exists for this variant's actual hardware - which correctly
  keeps the 2D/3D view toggle hidden via renderCwtTab()'s existing
  `data.model3d ? '' : 'none'` gate, no code change needed.
  Verified via Playwright: Global|UK toggle appears for 1300 only;
  ukVariant has no model3d (view toggle hidden); all 8 components/10
  hotspots present with correct aria-labels; direct toggleCwtPlate()
  calls across all 8 components total exactly 96.0t; all 11 combo tiers
  compute to 0/13/16/26/36/46/56/66/76/86/96t exactly matching the
  manual; a real (non-force) click on the visible receptacle-plate area
  correctly resolves to "Receptacle Plate 1" with the "OEM-approved
  combination" checkmark, confirming the nested 3t Counterweight-7
  hotspot (listed after the 13t receptacle in the components array, so
  it paints on top in the SVG) doesn't swallow clicks meant for the
  surrounding 13t piece; switching Global<->UK leaves the Global entry's
  own data/3D model completely unchanged; full regression sweep across
  every crane's CWT tab plus the other 8 app tabs, 0 console errors.

26. 360 SLEW CLEARANCE - "BEYOND REAR OF CARRIER" (mirrors the existing
    "beyond side" feature, plus a tool box add-on toggle)
  Person's request: the existing 360° Slew Clearance card only ever
  measured how far a swing-radius point oversails the carrier's SIDE
  (radius - carrierWidthB/2) - wanted the same idea for the REAR (radius
  - some rear reference), since a crew backed up close to something
  behind the crane needs that figure just as much as the side one.
  Complication: most of the LTM fleet has a rear-mounted "combi box"
  accessory that sticks out further back than the bare chassis - wanted
  a toggle to include or exclude it from the rear figure.
  Turned out the base rear reference didn't need sourcing at all - it
  already existed. FOOTPRINTS[modelKey].rear (methodology.txt 4.5,
  "distance from slew center to the rear tip of the chassis body") is
  exactly the same measurement already used for the chassis-footprint
  collision check, sourced and cross-validated per crane back when that
  feature was built. Every crane in SLEW_CLEARANCE_DATA already has one.
  The tool box depth needed real sourcing though. Confirmed 810mm from
  TWO independent OEM manuals, not one: LTM 1300-6.2's own operator
  manual (chapter 1.03 Technical data > 9.16 Combi box, fetched from the
  person's Nextcloud - 0.81m depth x 2.50m width x 1.03m height) and LTM
  1110-5.1's own manual (same chapter, "16 Combi box", 0.81m x 2.50m x
  1.02m) - same physical part, independently dimensioned in two separate
  manuals, exact match on the depth that matters here. Checked all 7
  fleet manuals for it: LTM 1130-5.1/1160-5.2/1250-5.1 each confirm the
  box is actually fitted (same "Overloading of the combi box is
  prohibited" warning page in all three) but don't repeat its own
  dimensioned drawing in these particular manual editions - treated as
  the same fleet-standard 810mm part given the exact two-source
  agreement, not a guess. LRT 1100-2.1's manual has zero "combi box"
  mentions anywhere in 1197 pages (different chassis architecture, not
  road-driven the same way as the LTM range) - correctly gets no tool
  box option. LTM 1650-8.1 was confirmed directly by the person - a
  combi box never gets fitted to it - also correctly gets no tool box
  option. COMBI_BOX_DEPTH_MM{} holds the 5 LTM keys only (1110/1130/
  1160/1250/1300); its absence for a modelKey is what drives the "Include
  tool box" checkbox's own visibility in both places it appears.
  Two places this needed wiring, both mirroring the existing side-
  clearance pattern exactly rather than inventing a new one:
    1. The numeric "360° Slew Clearance" table (Crane Layout tab) gained
       a 4th column, same per-point rows as the existing side column,
       computed as radius - (FOOTPRINTS.rear + (toolbox checked ?
       COMBI_BOX_DEPTH_MM : 0)). New "Include tool box" checkbox (only
       rendered when the selected crane has a COMBI_BOX_DEPTH_MM entry)
       drives scIncludeToolbox, reset to false on every crane switch -
       same reasoning as every other per-crane toggle in this app, a
       choice made for one crane should never silently carry over and
       apply to the next crane's own figure.
    2. The 3D Carrier Preview's toggle list gained a second checkbox
       "Show clearance measurement (beyond rear of carrier)" alongside
       the existing side one, plus a conditionally-rendered "Include tool
       box" sub-checkbox nested under it. Both feed the same
       onSlewCircleToggle() the side checkbox already used, which now
       also computes rearMm and passes it through to
       window.__carrier3dSetSlewCircles as a new 6th argument.
  carrier3d.js's applySlewCircles() gained the matching rearMm parameter
  and draws a second dimension line per circle when it's set - same
  addDimensionLine() helper the side line already used, same per-circle
  loop, just a different pair of endpoints. The side line hardcodes
  world +X since every crane's calibration is guaranteed lateral-
  symmetric; the rear line deliberately does NOT hardcode +Z the same
  way - it computes BOTH endpoints via siteToWorld(cal, 0, mm), which is
  the one place in this file that already carries the correct sign per
  crane (computeFormulaCalibration's own dirSign, chosen so site-space
  +Y/rear always maps to increasing world Z regardless of which end of a
  given crane's own CAD export sits at min vs max Z - see that
  function's comment). Hardcoding +Z here would have silently drawn the
  rear dimension line pointing at the FRONT on any crane calibrated with
  frontAtMinZ false, without erroring - confirmed correct instead of
  assumed, by cross-checking the 3D line's own on-screen label against
  the numeric table's figure for the same crane/point (642mm in both
  places for LTM 1300's Winch 2, no toolbox - see verification below).
  pendingSlewCircleContext (the replay-once-loaded state for a model
  still mid-fetch when the toggle fires) now carries rearMm alongside
  carrierWidthMm, same replay pattern as everything else in this file.
  CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped 41->42 (carrier3d.js itself changed).
  Verified via Playwright: numeric table's new rear column present and
  correct for all 7 cranes with FOOTPRINTS-only rear figures (including
  a legitimate NEGATIVE clearance for LRT 1100 - Counterweight's own
  4.19m radius doesn't even reach its 4.39m rear overhang, a real result,
  not a bug); "Include tool box" checkbox correctly hidden for 1100/1650,
  correctly shown and correctly shifts the rear column for the other 5;
  checkbox state resets on crane switch; 3D preview's two new checkboxes
  render with correct conditional nesting; a real 3D activation +
  Fit View screenshot for LTM 1300 shows both dimension lines rendered
  correctly - side line labelled 3470mm (exact match to the numeric
  table's 3.47m for Winch 2) and rear line labelled 642mm (exact match to
  the numeric table's 0.64m for the same point, before ticking the tool
  box checkbox); full regression sweep across every crane on both Crane
  Layout and Support Pad Placement (which shares carrier3d.js) plus the
  other 4 top-level tabs, 0 console errors throughout.

  26.1 "Include tool box" now defaults ON, not off
    Person independently hand-verified the LTM 1300 figure against the
    OEM dimension chain (2407+1650+320 rear-to-slew-centre + 810mm box =
    5187mm; 5600mm Counterweight radius - 5187mm = 413mm) - matched the
    app's own 412mm to within 1mm (the app's FOOTPRINTS.rear of 4378mm
    vs. their 4377mm hand-sum, an already-known rounding difference from
    how that figure was originally cross-validated, not a new error).
    Then flagged that the checkbox defaulting unticked was actively
    wrong for how the fleet is really run: every LTM crane that carries
    a combi box has it physically fitted in real life, so an unticked
    default was showing an 810mm-too-generous rear clearance unless
    someone remembered to tick the box every single time - a real
    number, but not the number a crew should see without asking.
    Fixed by defaulting scIncludeToolbox to true (was false) and adding
    `checked` to both the numeric table's static checkbox and the 3D
    preview's per-crane-rendered one, keeping the toggle itself (still
    resets to true, not the previous choice, on every crane switch -
    someone unticking it for the rare box-less crane never carries that
    over) rather than removing it and hardcoding the box into
    FOOTPRINTS.rear directly, since the checkbox is what surfaces the
    correction as a visible, explained figure instead of a silent one.
    CACHE_VERSION v139->v140, app version v3.9->v3.10.

  26.2 Two more LTM 1650 radius points: TY's and T3N
    Person's first message read as two more REAR-reference figures
    (given the immediately preceding rear-clearance work), but the
    numbers (11646mm, 11485.4mm) didn't fit that pattern at all - over
    3x the 1650's own base rear overhang (3717mm). Asked before touching
    anything; person corrected: these are two more SWING RADIUS points
    (same category as the existing Counterweight/Winch 2/Ballast Radius
    entries), not rear-overhang references - "clearance required if the
    TY's are fitted and open to 40 degrees" and "if the N assembly is
    fitted to the T3 boom".
    Added both to SLEW_CLEARANCE_DATA['1650'].fixed (was empty) rather
    than folding them into the existing "Ballast Radius (Site Clearance)"
    variants group - fixed points are simultaneously-checkable
    independent checkboxes, variants are a mutually-exclusive picker
    (only one ballast tray radius is ever physically fitted at once).
    TY's and T3N describe separate rigging configurations, not
    alternatives to the ballast tray or to each other, so fixed is the
    correct shape - person can check either, both, or neither. No other
    code changes needed anywhere: both the numeric 360° Slew Clearance
    table and the 3D preview's own toggle list already render every
    `fixed` entry generically (same mechanism the Counterweight/Winch 2
    checkboxes already used for other cranes), so the two new points
    just appeared correctly in both places, side AND rear clearance
    columns, the moment the data was added.
    Verified via Playwright: both new checkboxes present with correct
    labels/radii in the numeric table and the 3D toggle list; side/rear
    clearance columns compute correctly for both (TY's: 11.65m radius ->
    10.10m beyond side, 7.93m beyond rear with tool box N/A for 1650;
    T3N: 11.49m -> 9.94m / 7.77m); TY's correctly becomes the new
    limiting point (largest radius, beating the pre-existing Ballast
    Radius options); full regression sweep, 0 console errors.
    CACHE_VERSION v140->v141, app version v3.10->v3.11.

27. REMOVED: Crane Layout's "Show outrigger distances (C1-C4, baseline
    position)" toggle
  Person's request. Removed cleanly end to end rather than just hiding
  the checkbox, since nothing else in the app referenced any of it:
    - index.html: the checkbox itself (renderCarrier3DSlewToggles), its
      onLegDimensionToggle() handler, and both call sites
      (renderCarrier3DSlewToggles' own render pass and
      activateCarrier3DLayout's re-sync on switching back to the Crane
      Layout sub-tab).
    - carrier3d.js: legDimensionGroup (the THREE.Group holding the
      lines), clearLegDimensions()/its 3 call sites (clearScene,
      sceneBox's Fit View bounding-box union, and the "switching cards"
      clear block that also clears slew circles/ground layout/mat edge
      marks), applyLegDimensions(), pendingLegDimensions/
      pendingLegDimensionContext (the replay-once-loaded state) and
      their own replay-branch call, and the window.__carrier3dSetLegDimensions
      export itself. Left a comment cleanup on
      pendingGroundLayoutMarks's own comment too, since it referenced
      "pendingLegDimensions above" and "the diagonal C1-C4 lines" by
      name for context that no longer exists.
    Nothing else in the app called any of this - it was Crane Layout's
    own toggle only, entirely separate from Support Pad Placement's own
    (still-present) C1-C4 diagonal "as the crow flies" distances and its
    ground layout marks, which use a completely different code path.
    CARRIER3D_VERSION bumped 42->43 (carrier3d.js changed).
    Verified via Playwright: checkbox and its label gone from the 3D
    toggle list; every remaining toggle (radius points, side/rear
    clearance + tool box, ground layout marks) still renders and
    functions correctly with 0 console errors; full regression sweep
    across every crane on both Crane Layout and Support Pad Placement

  pattern. 'amount' mode swaps the Current/Desired Length fields for a
  single "Shorten By" field and reuses the exact same formula
  (calcChainShortening() now branches on chainMode, both branches share
  CHAIN_PITCH_TABLE and the same round-to-nearest-link math) - "Actual

  doesn't match what the existing, already-verified CHAIN_PITCH_TABLE
  (8mm -> 24mm/link, matching their own reference spreadsheet) computes -
  500/24 = 20.83, which rounds to 21 links (504mm actual, +4mm over), not
  20. Didn't change the pitch table over an offhand example - flagged
  this instead, same formula/table both modes already share.

35. AR real-world-scale placement, Phase 1 (LTM 1110, Android only)
  Person asked whether the 3D models could be viewed at real-world scale
  in a physical space via AR, to check clearances. Scoped it first (own
  discussion, not logged as a section since no code changed), person
  approved Phase 1: Android WebXR only, LTM 1110's carrier model only, as
  a single real-device pilot before wiring the other 5 carriers or
  building the separate iOS path (AR Quick Look needs a per-model USDZ
  conversion this app has no pipeline for yet - a later phase, not this
  one).
  No new library - vendor/three/ already ships GLTFLoader/DRACOLoader/
  OrbitControls fully offline (see this file's earlier entries), so
  hand-rolled WebXR hit-test code fits that existing pattern far better
  than pulling in Google's separate <model-viewer> web component, which
  would duplicate a three.js runtime already vendored here.
  carrier3d.js: renderer.xr.enabled = true at creation (harmless with no
  session active). The render loop itself had to change from a hand-
  rolled self-chaining requestAnimationFrame chain to renderer.
  setAnimationLoop(onFrame) - that's the only mechanism three.js has for
  transparently swapping between plain rAF and an active XRSession's own
  frame timing, which hit-testing needs access to (frame.
  getHitTestResults() only exists on the WebXR frame callback's own
  argument, never on a plain rAF tick). onFrame branches on renderer.xr.
  isPresenting: controls.update() only while NOT presenting (OrbitControls
  has no meaning once the device's own camera pose drives the view),
  hit-test only while presenting.
  Placement anchor: "tap to place" places the model's own SLEW CENTRE at
  the tapped point, not the CAD export's raw local origin (wherever
  Onshape happened to put it) - reuses computeFormulaCalibration (already
  written for the slew-circle/ground-layout features) to find that point,
  wraps the loaded model in a THREE.Group offset so the group's own
  origin lands exactly on the slew centre/ground point, and both
  positions (tap-to-place) and rotations (the overlay's own ⟲/⟳ buttons)
  are applied to that GROUP, not the model directly - rotating a crane
  around its own slew axis is what a crew actually needs to check
  clearance against a wall at different orientations, not a spin around
  some arbitrary CAD-export corner.
  Re-tappable, not one-shot: every tap while the reticle's visible moves
  the crane to that new spot, rotation carries over across a re-tap
  (only the overlay's own buttons change it) - a first guess at
  placement is rarely exactly right and re-tapping is the obvious way to
  nudge it without a separate unlock control.
  index.html: new AR_SUPPORTED_MODELS = ['1110'] gates which crane shows
  the button at all; window.__carrier3dARSupported() (navigator.xr.
  isSessionSupported('immersive-ar')) is also checked before showing it,
  so the button never appears on iOS Safari or desktop rather than
  appearing and failing on tap. Button added to both 3D-preview toolbars
  (Support Pad Placement's own card and Crane Layout's) since both
  already share the one carrier3d.js viewer instance.
  Known Phase 1 limitations, flagged for the person rather than silently
  shipped as if complete: no auto-orientation (the crane always loads
  facing whatever direction it was placed at last, no compass/north
  alignment); only LTM 1110 has the button; no iOS support yet; I have no
  camera/motion-sensor hardware to test real AR placement/scale accuracy
  myself, so this needs verification on an actual Android device before
  being trusted for a real clearance check.
  Verified via Playwright (as far as it's possible without real AR
  hardware): both toolbar buttons stay hidden in headless Chromium
  (navigator.xr exists but isSessionSupported resolves false, same as a
  desktop/non-AR browser would see) - confirms the feature-detect gate
  itself works and the button doesn't appear where it shouldn't. Full
  regression sweep on the ordinary orbit-preview render path (now running
  through setAnimationLoop instead of the old rAF chain) - both 3D cards
  still load/render/rotate correctly, 0 console errors. The actual AR
  session code (hit-test, placement, rotation) could NOT be exercised by
  this testing - no WebXR-capable device available in this environment.
  CARRIER3D_VERSION 45->46, CACHE_VERSION v149->v150, app version
  v3.19->v3.20.

37. LTM 1650 CARRIER_CALIBRATION: resolving the `uncertain` flag
  Person asked how the `uncertain` flag on LTM 1650's frontAtMinZ could
  actually be fixed, rather than just left as a standing caveat. The
  other five cranes were confirmed by rendering a straight-down view and
  visually spotting the driving cab - 1650's export is too sparse for
  that (28 parts, no cab, only one of the two outrigger-leg pairs
  modeled), so that check was never possible for it the same way.
  Cross-checked it a different way instead, using data already in the
  app: a Playwright script loaded ltm1650-carrier.glb directly (same
  GLTFLoader/DRACOLoader path carrier3d.js uses) and found the one
  modeled leg pair as two ~0.7m x 0.7m foot plates at two Z stations,
  ~10.66m apart. That gave two INDEPENDENT ways to estimate where the
  slew centre sits, neither one referencing the other:
    1. computeFormulaCalibration's own estimate, from the chassis's
       front/rear overhang fractions alone (FOOTPRINTS['1650']: front
       14585mm, rear 3717mm) - never looks at leg geometry at all.
    2. Solving for the slew-centre Z that makes the two DETECTED feet
       match GROUND_LAYOUT_DATA's own real OEM leg-station figures
       (rear legs C1/C4 ~4000-4529mm, front legs C2/C3 ~6042-6530mm) -
       never looks at overhang fractions at all.
  Under the existing frontAtMinZ: true, these two independent estimates
  land within ~1m of each other (plausible - it's the same kind of
  formula-vs-refined-geometry gap the other five cranes had before their
  own full 4-corner refinement). Under the flipped assumption, they land
  ~8.6m apart - two independently-derived estimates of the same physical
  point don't do that unless the underlying orientation assumption feeding
  both of them is wrong. That's real evidence the current value was
  already correct, not backwards - one notch below the other five cranes'
  own visual cab confirmation, but a genuine cross-check rather than a
  guess. Person confirmed removing the flag rather than leaving it
  pending an in-person check that may never happen.
  Removed `uncertain: true` from CARRIER_CALIBRATION['1650'] (the
  frontAtMinZ/lateralSign values themselves are unchanged - this was
  always just a confidence marker, never read anywhere in the code).
  Reasoning documented inline in CARRIER_CALIBRATION's own comment.
  CACHE_VERSION v151->v152, app version v3.21->v3.22.

38. AR: carry bog mats + slew circles (and other active overlays) along
  Person asked whether the bog mat marks and slew radius circles could
  show up in AR too, not just the bare carrier. They were right to ask -
  as shipped, entering AR pulled the model out of the orbit-preview scene
  into its own arGroup, but left slewCircleGroup/matEdgeGroup/etc exactly
  where they were, still drawn at the OLD orbit-preview position - once
  the crane moved into AR space those groups would've been left behind as
  stray floating geometry with nothing to do with the placed model. Real
  bug, not just a missing feature.
  Fix: reparent whichever overlay groups are currently populated
  (outriggerGroup, slewCircleGroup, groundLayoutGroup, matEdgeGroup,
  targetMatEdgeGroup - all five, not just the two asked about, since
  there's no extra cost to including all of them and each tab's own
  toggles only ever populate the groups relevant to it anyway - Support
  Pad Placement never has slew circles active, Crane Layout never has mat
  edges) into arGroup alongside the model root, each carrying the SAME
  slew-centre offset as root. Every one of these groups was drawn via
  siteToWorld(cal, x, y), which resolves through cal.lateralCenter/
  groundY/slewZ - the exact same point root gets offset by - so giving
  the group that identical offset before reparenting puts its geometry in
  the same slew-centre-relative local space as the model, and it now
  moves/rotates with arGroup exactly like the model does.
  One correctness fix bundled in along the way: __carrier3dEnterAR used
  to call computeFormulaCalibration() fresh for root's own offset, an
  UNREFINED estimate - while the overlay groups themselves were drawn
  using whatever computeCalibration()/ensureSlewCalibration() had
  actually cached (the REFINED, leg-geometry-corrected calibration, once
  Support Pad Placement has synced that model). Using two different
  calibrations for root vs its own overlays would have silently
  reintroduced the exact "real visible offset" bug refineCalibration
  FromGeometry was built to fix (methodology.txt 10.78/10.81) - the mats/
  circles would render at a small but real offset from the placed model
  in AR. Switched to ensureSlewCalibration(modelKey, root, footprint,
  calibration) instead, which returns the same cached value (refined if
  available, else the formula fallback) that every overlay-drawing
  function already prefers - guarantees root and its overlays always
  share one calibration.
  Verified: the offset/reparent math itself proven exactly (not just
  visually) via a standalone Node script using vendor/three's own Vector3/
  Group/Matrix4 directly - placed a simulated overlay point through the
  same offset-then-reparent-then-translate-and-rotate sequence
  __carrier3dEnterAR/onARSelect/rotateARModel actually perform, compared
  the resulting world position against the analytically expected one
  (rotate the slew-centre-relative offset, then translate to the tap
  point) - matched to float precision (8.8e-16m). Playwright regression
  on the unmodified surrounding code: toggled all 8 of LTM 1650's slew-
